dwara_core/config/
mod.rs

1//! Configuration schema v1 for the gateway (frozen domain vocabulary).
2//!
3//! The types in this module model the source form of a dwara configuration
4//! file. Compilation into a runtime snapshot is a later concern
5//! (config-compile pipeline); here we define strict, self-describing types:
6//!
7//! - every struct rejects unknown fields (`deny_unknown_fields`),
8//! - field order follows declaration order on serialization,
9//! - the root type [`Gateway`] exports a JSON Schema via `schemars`.
10//!
11//! YAML <-> typed conversions are provided by [`parse_gateway`] (YAML text to
12//! typed value, with path-precise error messages) and [`gateway_to_yaml`]
13//! (typed value back to normalized YAML text). Round-trip guarantee:
14//! `parse_gateway(gateway_to_yaml(cfg))` always succeeds and yields a value
15//! that serializes to the identical normalized text (stable normalization,
16//! not byte-identity with the original input).
17//!
18//! Submodules carry the parts of the config CONTRACT that are more than
19//! serde shapes: [`credentials`] defines the credential selector/stored-hash
20//! formats every credential holder must agree on plus the `${...}`
21//! secret-reference grammar (DW-045), [`limits`] the numeric
22//! bounds validation enforces, [`net`] the trusted-proxy IP/CIDR
23//! grammar shared by validation and the runtime matchers,
24//! [`versioning`] the HTTP-date and media-type grammar of the API
25//! versioning aids (DW-048), and [`transforms`] the JSON-pointer
26//! grammar and shapes of the request/response transforms and
27//! security-header injection (DW-028). [`cache`] carries the
28//! route-scoped response-caching grammar (DW-037), and [`ai`] the
29//! AI provider/model grammar of the provider-adapter pack (DW-075).
30
31pub mod ai;
32pub mod cache;
33pub mod credentials;
34pub mod includes;
35pub mod limits;
36pub mod mesh;
37pub mod net;
38pub mod quotas;
39pub mod ssrf;
40pub mod transforms;
41pub mod versioning;
42
43use schemars::JsonSchema;
44use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
45
46use cache::RouteCache;
47use transforms::{Masking, SecurityHeaders, Transforms};
48
49/// Error produced when a configuration document fails to parse.
50///
51/// Carries the dotted path of the offending node (e.g. `listeners[0].port`)
52/// and the underlying serde message.
53#[derive(Debug)]
54pub struct ConfigError {
55    pub path: String,
56    pub message: String,
57}
58
59impl std::fmt::Display for ConfigError {
60    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
61        write!(f, "config error at {}: {}", self.path, self.message)
62    }
63}
64
65impl std::error::Error for ConfigError {}
66
67/// CFG-03 (#239): the current config schema version. Increment when
68/// the schema gains a breaking change (removed fields, renamed
69/// fields, changed semantics). The `dwara lint` command warns when a
70/// config's `version` is older than this; `dwara migrate` upgrades
71/// configs to this version.
72pub const CURRENT_CONFIG_VERSION: u32 = 1;
73
74/// Default config version (assumed when `version` is absent from the
75/// YAML). The initial schema is version 1.
76fn default_config_version() -> u32 {
77    1
78}
79
80/// Whether the config version is the default (1). Used by
81/// `skip_serializing_if` to omit the field when it's the default.
82fn is_default_config_version(v: &u32) -> bool {
83    *v == 1
84}
85
86/// Root of a dwara configuration: one gateway process, N listeners, and one
87/// compiled config generation.
88#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
89#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
90pub struct Gateway {
91    /// CFG-03 (#239): config schema version. Defaults to 1 when
92    /// absent (the initial schema). The `dwara lint` command warns
93    /// when the version is older than the current schema version; the
94    /// `dwara migrate` command upgrades the config to the current
95    /// version.
96    #[serde(
97        default = "default_config_version",
98        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_config_version"
99    )]
100    pub version: u32,
101    /// Entry points the gateway binds.
102    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
103    pub listeners: Vec<Listener>,
104    /// Match rules routing requests to services.
105    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
106    pub routes: Vec<Route>,
107    /// Logical APIs being exposed.
108    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
109    pub services: Vec<Service>,
110    /// Load-balancing pools.
111    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
112    pub upstreams: Vec<Upstream>,
113    /// API caller identities.
114    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
115    pub consumers: Vec<Consumer>,
116    /// Named reusable rule bundles.
117    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
118    pub policies: Vec<Policy>,
119    /// Gateway-level (global) policy attachment (#123): names of policies
120    /// from the `policies` list that apply to EVERY request the gateway
121    /// serves — the LEAST specific link of the frozen precedence chain
122    /// consumer > route > service > listener > global. Named
123    /// `global_policies` (not `policies`, which is the policy REGISTRY at
124    /// this level) so the two roles cannot be confused. All applicable
125    /// levels' rules AND together (see the rate-limiter module docs), so a
126    /// global policy is an additional constraint on every request, not a
127    /// default that more specific levels replace. Global policies also
128    /// apply to UNROUTED traffic: a request whose path matches no route
129    /// is rate-limited by them before the 404 is answered (the reserved
130    /// paths /healthz, /readyz, and /metrics stay exempt).
131    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
132    pub global_policies: Vec<String>,
133    /// Gateway-level (global) authorization rules (#123): the least
134    /// specific link of the authorization precedence chain
135    /// consumer > route > service > listener > global (see [`Authz`] for
136    /// the rule semantics and the `authz` module for the merge: a deny at
137    /// ANY level wins; otherwise the most specific level with rules
138    /// governs). Applies to every request that resolved a route
139    /// (authorization runs after route resolution per the documented
140    /// request-path order). Same validation rules as route-level
141    /// authorization; see [`Authz`].
142    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
143    pub authorization: Option<Authz>,
144    /// Gateway-level default security headers baseline (SEC-09, #213):
145    /// applied to EVERY route that does not carry its own
146    /// `security_headers` block and has not set
147    /// `security_headers_opt_out: true`. Fail-secure defaults: a
148    /// route with no `security_headers` block inherits this posture
149    /// rather than shipping without HSTS/nosniff. A route that needs
150    /// to disable a header from the default sets its own
151    /// `security_headers` block (which fully replaces the default
152    /// for that route), or sets `security_headers_opt_out: true` to
153    /// opt out entirely. See [`SecurityHeaders`].
154    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
155    pub default_security_headers: Option<SecurityHeaders>,
156    /// SEC-08 (#212): Global WAF policy with CRS-compatible rules.
157    /// When enabled, the rules are evaluated on every request
158    /// (regardless of route-level WAF config). The anomaly score is
159    /// accumulated across all matching rules; the request is blocked
160    /// when the score exceeds the threshold. See [`GlobalWaf`].
161    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
162    pub waf: Option<GlobalWaf>,
163    /// IP addresses / CIDR ranges of proxies whose `X-Forwarded-For` claims
164    /// are trusted (gateway-level; the direct connection peer must be in
165    /// this list for an inbound XFF chain to be preserved and extended).
166    /// Each entry must be an IP address (e.g. `10.1.2.3`) or a CIDR
167    /// (e.g. `10.0.0.0/8`); anything else fails validation. Empty (the
168    /// default) trusts nobody: every proxied request carries an XFF of
169    /// exactly the direct peer, and inbound XFF values are discarded.
170    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
171    pub trusted_proxies: Vec<String>,
172    /// Gateway-level concurrency cap (DW-015): the maximum number of
173    /// requests admitted concurrently across the WHOLE gateway. Absent
174    /// (the default) is unlimited; 0 is invalid (validation rejects it —
175    /// omit the field for unlimited). Over-cap requests are rejected
176    /// immediately with 503 "gateway saturated" (no queueing). A slot is
177    /// reserved at request admission and released when the response body
178    /// completes (or the connection drops). The reserved paths
179    /// `/healthz` and `/readyz` bypass the cap so liveness/readiness
180    /// probes still answer under saturation.
181    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
182    pub max_concurrent_requests: Option<u32>,
183    /// Monitor mode (DW-041) for the gateway concurrency cap's load
184    /// shedding: when the cap is saturated and a request would be shed,
185    /// the would-shed is LOGGED and counted
186    /// (`dwara_policy_dry_run_total{phase="load_shed"}` + a
187    /// `dwara::policy` warn event) and the request is ADMITTED over the
188    /// cap instead — deliberately exceeding `max_concurrent_requests` so
189    /// an operator can observe what a cap would shed (and at which
190    /// priorities) before enforcing it. Only meaningful with a cap set;
191    /// validation rejects it on an uncapped gateway (it would be a
192    /// no-op flag that reads as coverage).
193    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
194    pub load_shed_dry_run: bool,
195    /// JWT verification providers (DW-019): trusted token issuers whose
196    /// keys are fetched from a JWKS endpoint. Each provider independently
197    /// verifies `Authorization: Bearer` tokens (alg allowlist, iss/aud,
198    /// exp with leeway) and maps the token to a consumer — via the
199    /// provider's explicit `consumer` binding, or by matching a
200    /// consumer's `jwt` credential `issuer` against the token's `iss`
201    /// claim. Empty (the default): the gateway does not interpret Bearer
202    /// tokens and forwards `Authorization` upstream untouched.
203    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
204    pub jwt_providers: Vec<JwtProvider>,
205    /// Admin API listener (DW-022, decision 6): mTLS-ONLY management
206    /// endpoint. Absent (the default): no admin listener is started at
207    /// all — the gateway is admin-silent until an operator configures
208    /// one. When present, `tls` must carry all three files (server
209    /// cert, key, and the CA that client certificates must chain to);
210    /// a missing `client_ca_file` is rejected by validation — plaintext
211    /// admin is not a supported production shape. The dev fallback
212    /// (`DWARA_ADMIN_DEV=1`, dwara-bin) allows loopback-only plaintext
213    /// and is loudly dev-only. The admin listener's bind set is fixed
214    /// at startup; changes take effect on restart.
215    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
216    pub admin: Option<AdminConfig>,
217    /// Deliberate opt-in to running a gateway with ZERO routes (#129,
218    /// maintainer decision). Default false: validation rejects a
219    /// route-less config. An empty route set is schema-valid (every
220    /// collection defaults empty), and a truncated or torn config
221    /// write (truncate-then-save, common in naive editors) is
222    /// schema-valid too — publishing it would drop ALL routing
223    /// mid-run (every request 404s while the file "looks fine"). The
224    /// guard applies to cold start AND hot reload alike; a rejected
225    /// reload keeps the running generation serving. Set this to true
226    /// only for deliberate route-less shapes (e.g. a gateway whose
227    /// sole job is the admin API): with the flag set, unrouted
228    /// requests answer 404 after listener/global policy checks, per
229    /// the documented request-path order.
230    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
231    pub allow_empty_routes: bool,
232    /// HMAC request-signing verification policy (DW-036): the
233    /// gateway-wide clock-skew window applied to the
234    /// `X-Dwara-Timestamp` header of every signed request. Absent
235    /// (the default) = [`DEFAULT_HMAC_CLOCK_SKEW_SECS`] (±5 minutes).
236    /// See the `security::authn` module docs for the full
237    /// signing contract (canonical string, headers, replay window).
238    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
239    pub hmac_auth: Option<HmacAuth>,
240    /// Alert/event webhook targets (DW-044): every gateway event whose
241    /// kind appears in a target's `events` list is POSTed to that
242    /// target's `url` as one JSON envelope (id, kind, timestamp,
243    /// gateway instance, payload). Delivery runs on a background task,
244    /// never on the request path; emission is a bounded non-blocking
245    /// hand-off (a full queue drops the event and counts the drop), so
246    /// webhook failures can never stall the dataplane. Header values
247    /// may be inline or `${...}` secret references (DW-045) — inline
248    /// values are redacted in every config echo. See the `events`
249    /// module docs for the envelope, the retry/budget shape, and the
250    /// egress posture. Empty (the default): no webhooks, and events
251    /// are still counted (emitted/dropped) but delivered nowhere.
252    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
253    pub webhooks: Vec<Webhook>,
254    /// The embedded analytics store (DW-043). Absent (the default):
255    /// no analytics database, request records go nowhere beyond the
256    /// Prometheus families and the sampled access log, and the admin
257    /// API's analytics endpoints answer 404. When present, the
258    /// gateway opens a SEPARATE SQLite file (never the state store's
259    /// database) at `path`, feeds every completed request's access
260    /// record to it on a fire-and-forget channel (a full channel
261    /// DROPS and counts — analytics can never slow the dataplane),
262    /// rolls raw records up through 1m/5m/1h/1d additive tables with
263    /// per-granularity retention, and serves the dashboard/Top-N/
264    /// structured-query endpoints from the admin listener. See the
265    /// `analytics` module docs for the write path and the bounded-disk
266    /// story. Part of startup wiring (the database opens once); the
267    /// DIMENSIONS list is read live from the current generation so a
268    /// reload can add or rename dimensions without a restart.
269    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
270    pub analytics: Option<AnalyticsConfig>,
271    /// Real-time access-record stream (DW-121, the opt-in firehose out):
272    /// every completed request's access record — not rollups, not the
273    /// discrete DW-044 ops events — streamed to an external sink in
274    /// flushed batches. Complements, never replaces, the embedded
275    /// analytics store: the two are configured independently and a
276    /// deployment can run either, both, or neither. Absent (the
277    /// default): no stream. The pipeline is fire-and-forget end to end
278    /// (a bounded channel that drops and counts — it can never slow or
279    /// block the dataplane). See the `events::stream` module docs for
280    /// the wire format, the batching contract, and the failure
281    /// isolation story. Startup wiring arms the pipeline (the channel
282    /// capacity is fixed at boot); the sink set — including the
283    /// enabled/disabled state — is live per config generation.
284    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
285    pub analytics_stream: Option<AnalyticsStreamConfig>,
286    /// The GeoIP database (DW-050): MaxMind-format .mmdb file backing
287    /// `authorization.geoip` country/ASN predicates. Absent: geo rules
288    /// are rejected by validation (an unevaluable gate is an authoring
289    /// error, not a silent pass).
290    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
291    pub geoip: Option<GeoipConfig>,
292    /// Bounded admission queues (DW-053): when the gateway concurrency
293    /// cap (`max_concurrent_requests`) is saturated, requests WAIT for a
294    /// permit up to `queue_timeout_ms` instead of being immediately
295    /// shed. This produces a graceful degradation curve (latency rises
296    /// before shedding begins) rather than the cliff of immediate
297    /// shedding. Absent (the default): the DW-016 behavior stands —
298    /// over-cap requests are shed immediately with 503. Requires
299    /// `max_concurrent_requests` (validation rejects an enabled queue
300    /// on an uncapped gateway, the same rule as `load_shed_dry_run`).
301    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
302    pub admission_queue: Option<AdmissionQueue>,
303    /// mTLS client-certificate consumer mapping (DW-035): maps a
304    /// VERIFIED client certificate (its SHA-256 fingerprint or subject
305    /// CN) to a consumer name, so a client presenting a valid mTLS
306    /// certificate is authenticated WITHOUT an API key, JWT, or other
307    /// header credential — the certificate IS the credential. The
308    /// certificate must still chain to a listener's `client_ca_file`
309    /// (verified at the TLS layer); this mapping only resolves an
310    /// ALREADY-VERIFIED certificate to a consumer. Distinct from the
311    /// per-consumer `mtls` credential (which binds a cert to ONE
312    /// consumer's credential list): this is a gateway-level table that
313    /// can map certificates independent of the consumer credential
314    /// registry. Absent (the default): mTLS certificates are matched
315    /// only through consumers' `mtls` credentials (the DW-019 path).
316    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
317    pub mtls_consumer_mapping: Option<MtlsConsumerMapping>,
318    /// Identity-forwarding headers (DW-035): when mTLS client auth is
319    /// used, the gateway forwards client certificate metadata to the
320    /// upstream as `X-Client-Cert-*` headers. These headers are
321    /// GATEWAY-SET: any inbound headers with these names are STRIPPED
322    /// from the client request before the gateway adds its own
323    /// (spoofing prevention — the "without headers spoofing" part of
324    /// the done-when). Absent (the default): no `X-Client-Cert-*`
325    /// headers are forwarded.
326    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
327    pub mtls_forward_headers: Option<MtlsForwardHeaders>,
328    /// Enterprise license configuration (DW-032). Absent (the default):
329    /// the gateway runs in OSS mode — all features available are
330    /// OSS-only and no license verification occurs. When present, the
331    /// gateway verifies the license file at startup (if compiled with
332    /// the `ent` cargo feature) and gates enterprise features behind
333    /// the license's feature claims. The public key is NOT configured
334    /// here — it comes from the `DWARA_LICENSE_PUBLIC_KEY` env var (or
335    /// the compiled-in development key when unset), never
336    /// user-configurable, so an operator cannot substitute their own
337    /// key to forge a license. When the `ent` feature is NOT compiled
338    /// in, the block is accepted but inert (the gate is always
339    /// `LicenseGate::none()`).
340    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
341    pub license: Option<LicenseConfig>,
342    /// OIDC providers (DW-034): trusted OpenID Connect issuers whose
343    /// Bearer tokens are validated by token introspection (RFC 7662)
344    /// rather than local JWT signature verification. Each provider
345    /// independently introspects `Authorization: Bearer` tokens that did
346    /// not verify against any JWT provider (DW-019) and maps the
347    /// introspection result to a consumer via the provider's explicit
348    /// `consumer` binding. The gateway also supports the
349    /// authorization-code + PKCE flow (acting as an OIDC relying party),
350    /// token exchange (RFC 8693), and token revocation (RFC 7009). Empty
351    /// (the default): the gateway does not introspect Bearer tokens and
352    /// OIDC is inert. See the `security::oidc` module docs for the full
353    /// flow, caching, and fail-open/fail-closed posture.
354    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
355    pub oidc_providers: Vec<OidcProvider>,
356    /// Distributed Redis rate limiter (DW-031). Absent (the default):
357    /// the local in-memory GCRA limiter is used (one limit per
358    /// process). When present, the `ent` cargo feature is compiled in,
359    /// AND a valid license with the `redis_rate_limiter` feature claim
360    /// is loaded, the gateway uses a Redis-backed GCRA limiter so two
361    /// or more instances share one rate limit. The same GCRA algorithm
362    /// runs, but the bucket state lives in Redis and is updated
363    /// atomically via a Lua script in a single round-trip. When the
364    /// `ent` feature is NOT compiled in, or the license lacks the
365    /// claim, the block is accepted but inert (the local limiter is
366    /// used and a warning is logged). When Redis is unreachable,
367    /// `fail_open` (default true) lets requests through; `false`
368    /// rejects with 429.
369    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
370    pub redis_rate_limiter: Option<RedisRateLimiterConfig>,
371    /// Distributed Redis-backed consumer request quotas (DW-155, ent
372    /// feature). Absent (the default): the local SQLite-backed quota
373    /// checker is used (one counter per instance, so a fleet of N
374    /// instances enforces N x the configured cap). When present, the
375    /// `ent` cargo feature is compiled in, AND a valid license with the
376    /// `redis_quotas` feature claim is loaded, the gateway uses a
377    /// Redis-backed quota checker so two or more instances share one
378    /// counter per (consumer, budget, window) — a fleet of N instances
379    /// enforces the CONFIGURED cap. The same daily/monthly budget
380    /// semantics run, but the counters live in Redis and are updated
381    /// atomically via a Lua script in a single round-trip. When the
382    /// `ent` feature is NOT compiled in, or the license lacks the
383    /// claim, the block is accepted but inert (the local SQLite checker
384    /// is used and a warning is logged). When Redis is unreachable,
385    /// `fail_open` (default true) lets requests through; `false`
386    /// rejects with 429.
387    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
388    pub redis_quotas: Option<RedisQuotaConfig>,
389    /// Distributed Redis-backed response cache (SCALE-04, #183, ent
390    /// feature). Absent (the default): the local in-memory moka cache
391    /// is used (one cache per instance, so a fleet of N instances has
392    /// N x cold caches). When present, the `ent` cargo feature is
393    /// compiled in, AND a valid license with the `redis_cache` feature
394    /// claim is loaded, the gateway uses a Redis-backed response cache
395    /// so two or more instances share one cache — fleet-wide hit
396    /// ratios. A local moka tier can front Redis (two-tier with Pub/Sub
397    /// invalidation) or be disabled for a pure Redis cache. When the
398    /// `ent` feature is NOT compiled in, or the license lacks the
399    /// claim, the block is accepted but inert (the local moka cache is
400    /// used and a one-line notice is logged at startup).
401    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
402    pub redis_cache: Option<RedisCacheConfig>,
403    /// Config convergence (DW-054, enterprise feature). Absent (the
404    /// default): each gateway instance serves only its local config
405    /// generation and never watches remote instances. When present,
406    /// the `ent` cargo feature is compiled in, AND a valid license
407    /// with the `config_convergence` feature claim is loaded, the
408    /// gateway publishes its config generation to a shared backend
409    /// (Redis in v1; etcd/Consul are deferred behind the
410    /// `ConfigConvergenceBackend` trait) and polls for generations
411    /// published by other instances, converging to the highest
412    /// generation within the poll interval. A drift report is emitted
413    /// at `drift_check_interval_ms` when instances diverge. When the
414    /// `ent` feature is NOT compiled in, or the license lacks the
415    /// claim, the block is accepted but inert (the local file watcher
416    /// runs alone) and a one-line notice is logged at startup.
417    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
418    pub config_convergence: Option<ConfigConvergenceConfig>,
419    /// Proxy-wasm plugin definitions (DW-055). Each plugin is a .wasm
420    /// module loaded and compiled with every config generation
421    /// (checksum-keyed hot-swap) and run on the request pipeline
422    /// phases it declares. Routes reference plugins by name via their
423    /// `plugins` field; the dataplane dispatches them through the
424    /// unified plugin chain (DW-157). A plugin whose .wasm cannot be
425    /// read or compiled is marked crashed at load time (logged) and
426    /// routes referencing it answer 500 fail-closed.
427    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
428    pub plugins: Vec<PluginConfig>,
429    /// SCALE-12 (#192): remote/signed plugin registry configuration.
430    /// When present, plugin `source.url` references are resolved
431    /// against the registry's base URL and signatures are verified
432    /// against the registry's pinned public keys. See
433    /// [`PluginRegistryConfig`].
434    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
435    pub plugin_registry: Option<PluginRegistryConfig>,
436    /// SCALE-12 (#191): global filter-chain ordering and dry-run
437    /// configuration. Per-route `filter_chain` overrides take
438    /// precedence. See [`FilterChainConfig`].
439    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
440    pub filter_chain: Option<FilterChainConfig>,
441    /// The AI provider-adapter pack (DW-075): the provider pool and the
442    /// model alias table behind every `ai` route action. Absent (the
443    /// default): no AI surface — an `ai` route action is rejected by
444    /// validation. When present, clients send OpenAI chat-completions
445    /// shaped requests to an `ai` route and the gateway translates to
446    /// the target provider's wire format (OpenAI, Anthropic, Gemini),
447    /// normalizing responses (and streaming deltas) back to the OpenAI
448    /// shape. See [`ai::AiConfig`] and the `ai` domain docs.
449    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
450    pub ai: Option<ai::AiConfig>,
451    /// Fleet operations (DW-098, enterprise feature). Absent (the
452    /// default): no fleet skew policy is enforced — the controller
453    /// accepts edges regardless of version. When present, the `ent`
454    /// cargo feature is compiled in, AND a valid license with the
455    /// `fleet_operations` feature claim is loaded, the controller
456    /// checks each connecting edge's version against the skew policy
457    /// and the admin API exposes `GET /fleet/skew` (per-edge skew
458    /// status) and `GET /fleet/status` (fleet-wide convergence +
459    /// version distribution). When the `ent` feature is NOT compiled
460    /// in, or the license lacks the claim, the block is accepted but
461    /// inert (the controller accepts all edges).
462    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
463    pub fleet: Option<FleetConfig>,
464    /// API lifecycle management (DW-110): the developer portal
465    /// (auto-generated from OpenAPI specs), environment profiles
466    /// (dev/staging/prod config overlays), and the API journey
467    /// recorder (request flow visualization for debugging). Absent
468    /// (the default): no lifecycle surface. The portal serving, profile
469    /// overlay application, and journey recording are scaffolded: the
470    /// block is accepted in every build and is inert until the runtime
471    /// wiring lands (validation
472    /// warns). See [`LifecycleConfig`].
473    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
474    pub lifecycle: Option<LifecycleConfig>,
475    /// Service mesh mode (DW-107, enterprise feature). Absent (the
476    /// default): the gateway runs as a regular reverse proxy. When
477    /// present, the `mesh` cargo feature is compiled in, AND a valid
478    /// license with the `service_mesh` feature claim is loaded, the
479    /// gateway runs as a sidecar in each pod: an init container
480    /// configures iptables/TPROXY redirects so all traffic to and from
481    /// the local application flows through the sidecar, which
482    /// terminates mTLS (inbound) and wraps mTLS (outbound) using
483    /// SPIFFE/SPIRE X.509 SVIDs fetched from the SPIRE Workload API.
484    /// When the `mesh` feature is NOT compiled in, or the license
485    /// lacks the claim, the block is accepted but inert (validation
486    /// warns, no sidecar listeners or SPIFFE client are wired). Ent-
487    /// only: validation warns when mesh is configured without the
488    /// `ent` feature. See [`mesh::MeshConfig`] and the `mesh` domain
489    /// module docs.
490    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
491    pub mesh: Option<mesh::MeshConfig>,
492    /// SSRF egress filter (SEC-13): when enabled, outbound connections
493    /// from webhook deliveries and OPA callouts are checked against a
494    /// deny set of private/loopback/link-local/metadata IP ranges.
495    /// An optional allowlist exempts specific CIDRs. Default disabled
496    /// (the v1 posture trusts operator-configured endpoints). See
497    /// [`ssrf::SsrfFilterConfig`] and [`ssrf::SsrfFilter`].
498    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
499    pub ssrf_filter: Option<ssrf::SsrfFilterConfig>,
500}
501
502/// Bounded admission queue config (DW-053, `gateway.admission_queue`).
503///
504/// When enabled and the concurrency cap is full, requests wait for a
505/// permit up to `queue_timeout_ms` instead of being immediately shed.
506/// The queue depth is bounded by `max_queue_size`; once full, further
507/// requests are shed immediately with 503 (the `queue_full` outcome).
508/// Per-priority splitting (`per_priority: true`, the default) reserves
509/// a fraction of queue capacity for high-priority requests so they are
510/// not starved by a low-priority queue fill.
511#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
512#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
513pub struct AdmissionQueue {
514    /// Master switch. Default false: the queue is inert and the
515    /// gateway sheds immediately on cap saturation (DW-016 behavior).
516    /// Requires `max_concurrent_requests` (validation rejects an
517    /// enabled queue on an uncapped gateway).
518    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
519    pub enabled: bool,
520    /// Total number of requests that may wait for a permit across all
521    /// priority classes (1..=10000). Once the queue is at capacity,
522    /// further over-cap requests are shed immediately with 503
523    /// (the `queue_full` outcome).
524    pub max_queue_size: u32,
525    /// Maximum time a request waits for a permit, in milliseconds
526    /// (1..=10000). If the timeout expires before a permit becomes
527    /// available, the request is shed with 503 (the `timeout`
528    /// outcome) and a `Retry-After` header.
529    pub queue_timeout_ms: u64,
530    /// Split queue capacity across priority classes (default true).
531    /// When true, a fraction of `max_queue_size` is reserved for
532    /// high-priority requests (priority >= 8) so they are not starved
533    /// by a low-priority queue fill. When false, the queue is a single
534    /// shared pool (first-come-first-served).
535    #[serde(default = "default_true", skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
536    pub per_priority: bool,
537}
538
539fn default_true() -> bool {
540    true
541}
542
543/// Default freshness target for live sketches (DW-092): 500 ms —
544/// sub-second freshness for the in-process rolling window.
545fn default_freshness_target() -> u64 {
546    500
547}
548
549/// Default baseline window count for the insights engine (DW-092):
550/// 1440 = 24 hours of 1-minute windows (one minute-of-day seasonal
551/// cycle).
552fn default_baseline_windows() -> u64 {
553    1440
554}

556/// mTLS client-certificate consumer mapping (DW-035,
557/// `gateway.mtls_consumer_mapping`).
558///
559/// Maps a VERIFIED client certificate to a consumer name by its
560/// SHA-256 fingerprint (lowercase colon-separated hex of the DER) or
561/// by subject CommonName. When enabled and a client presents a
562/// certificate that chains to a listener's `client_ca_file` but
563/// matches NO entry in this table, the gateway returns 401
564/// `mtls_consumer_not_mapped` (the certificate was verified but is not
565/// a known caller). A client that presents NO certificate on a
566/// listener with this mapping enabled falls through to the other
567/// authentication families (or 401 if the route requires auth and no
568/// family resolves).
569#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
570#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
571pub struct MtlsConsumerMapping {
572    /// Master switch. Default false: the mapping is inert and mTLS
573    /// certificates are matched only through consumers' `mtls`
574    /// credentials (the DW-019 path).
575    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
576    pub enabled: bool,
577    /// Fingerprint-to-consumer entries. Each `fingerprint` is the
578    /// SHA-256 of the client certificate DER as lowercase
579    /// colon-separated hex (e.g. `ab:cd:ef:...`). The fingerprint is
580    /// the EXACT bytes the gateway computes from the presented
581    /// certificate, so a re-issued certificate needs a new entry.
582    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
583    pub consumers: Vec<MtlsFingerprintMapping>,
584    /// Subject-CN-to-consumer mapping (alternative to `consumers`):
585    /// maps the certificate's subject CommonName to a consumer name.
586    /// Binding by subject CN survives certificate re-issue under the
587    /// same CN; the certificate must still chain to a listener's
588    /// `client_ca_file`. Keys are exact, case-sensitive CN strings.
589    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::collections::BTreeMap::is_empty")]
590    pub subject_cn_mapping: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, String>,
591}
592
593/// One fingerprint-to-consumer mapping entry (DW-035,
594/// `mtls_consumer_mapping.consumers[]`).
595#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
596#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
597pub struct MtlsFingerprintMapping {
598    /// SHA-256 fingerprint of the client certificate DER as lowercase
599    /// colon-separated hex (e.g. `ab:cd:ef:...`). Validated to be
600    /// 64 hex digits with optional colon separators.
601    pub fingerprint: String,
602    /// The consumer name this certificate resolves to. Must reference
603    /// a consumer in the `consumers` list (validation rejects an
604    /// unknown name).
605    pub consumer: String,
606}
607
608/// Identity-forwarding headers for mTLS client auth (DW-035,
609/// `gateway.mtls_forward_headers`).
610///
611/// When enabled, the gateway adds `X-Client-Cert-*` headers to the
612/// upstream request carrying metadata from the VERIFIED client
613/// certificate. These headers are GATEWAY-SET: any inbound headers
614/// whose names match the configured prefix are STRIPPED from the
615/// client request before the gateway adds its own, so a client cannot
616/// spoof certificate identity upstream.
617#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
618#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
619pub struct MtlsForwardHeaders {
620    /// Master switch. Default false: no `X-Client-Cert-*` headers are
621    /// forwarded.
622    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
623    pub enabled: bool,
624    /// Header name prefix (default `X-Client-Cert`). The gateway adds
625    /// `<prefix>-Fingerprint`, `<prefix>-Subject-CN`, `<prefix>-Issuer-CN`,
626    /// and `<prefix>-Not-After`. Inbound headers starting with this
627    /// prefix (case-insensitive) are stripped before injection.
628    #[serde(
629        default = "default_mtls_forward_prefix",
630        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_mtls_forward_prefix"
631    )]
632    pub prefix: String,
633}
634
635fn default_mtls_forward_prefix() -> String {
636    "X-Client-Cert".to_string()
637}
638
639fn is_default_mtls_forward_prefix(p: &str) -> bool {
640    p == "X-Client-Cert"
641}
642
643/// Enterprise license configuration (DW-032, `gateway.license`).
644///
645/// When present, the gateway verifies the license file at startup (if
646/// compiled with the `ent` cargo feature) and gates enterprise features
647/// behind the license's feature claims. The public key is NOT in this
648/// block — it comes from the `DWARA_LICENSE_PUBLIC_KEY` env var (or the
649/// compiled-in development key when unset), never user-configurable, so
650/// an operator cannot substitute their own key to forge a license. When
651/// the `ent` feature is NOT compiled in, the block is accepted but inert
652/// (the gate is always `LicenseGate::none()`).
653#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
654#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
655pub struct LicenseConfig {
656    /// Filesystem path of the license file (JSON: claims + Ed25519
657    /// signature). The file is read and verified at startup and on
658    /// every config reload. A missing file refuses to start at cold
659    /// start (exit 1); on reload, a missing file keeps the running
660    /// generation serving.
661    pub file: String,
662    /// Grace period in days after the license expires before the gate
663    /// degrades to OSS (default 7; validated to 0..=30). During the
664    /// grace window enterprise features still work and a warning is
665    /// logged. 0 means no grace (immediate degradation on expiry).
666    #[serde(
667        default = "default_license_grace_period_days",
668        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_license_grace_period_days"
669    )]
670    pub grace_period_days: u32,
671}
672
673fn default_license_grace_period_days() -> u32 {
674    crate::config::limits::DEFAULT_LICENSE_GRACE_PERIOD_DAYS
675}
676
677fn is_default_license_grace_period_days(v: &u32) -> bool {
678    *v == crate::config::limits::DEFAULT_LICENSE_GRACE_PERIOD_DAYS
679}

681/// Redis deployment topology (SCALE-11, #248). Controls how the
682/// gateway connects to Redis for the distributed rate limiter,
683/// quotas, and shared cache. The default `single` mode uses one
684/// Redis URL (the existing behavior). `sentinel` mode resolves the
685/// master through a Sentinel deployment. `cluster` mode connects
686/// to a Redis Cluster.
687#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
688#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase", deny_unknown_fields)]
689pub enum RedisTopology {
690    /// Single Redis instance (the default). Uses the `url` field
691    /// directly. No HA — if the instance is down, the fail-open
692    /// policy applies.
693    #[default]
694    Single,
695    /// Redis Sentinel: resolves the master via the Sentinel nodes
696    /// listed in `nodes`, using `master_name` to identify the
697    /// monitored instance. The gateway connects to the resolved
698    /// master via a `ConnectionManager` (automatic reconnection on
699    /// failover is handled by re-resolving on disconnect).
700    Sentinel,
701    /// Redis Cluster: connects to the cluster via the node URLs in
702    /// `nodes` (or `url` if `nodes` is empty). Uses the redis crate's
703    /// `ClusterClient` which handles slot mapping, MOVED/ASK
704    /// redirections, and cross-slot routing automatically. Lua
705    /// scripts use hash tags (`{key}`) to ensure all keys in a script
706    /// land on the same slot.
707    Cluster,
708}
709
710/// Redis HA and multi-region config (SCALE-11, #248). Embedded in
711/// each Redis-backed config block (rate limiter, quotas, cache) to
712/// control the connection topology and per-request timeout.
713#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
714#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
715pub struct RedisHaConfig {
716    /// Deployment topology (default `single`).
717    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_default_redis_topology")]
718    pub topology: RedisTopology,
719    /// Additional node URLs for `sentinel` or `cluster` mode. For
720    /// `sentinel`, these are the Sentinel node URLs (e.g.
721    /// `redis://sentinel-1:26379`). For `cluster`, these are the
722    /// cluster node URLs (any subset of the cluster's nodes; the
723    /// client discovers the rest). When empty, the `url` field on
724    /// the parent config is used as the sole node.
725    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
726    pub nodes: Vec<String>,
727    /// The master name for `sentinel` mode (the `master_name` in
728    /// Sentinel config). Required when `topology = sentinel`; ignored
729    /// otherwise.
730    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
731    pub master_name: Option<String>,
732    /// Per-request timeout in milliseconds (default 500; validated to
733    /// 50..=30 000). Applied to each Redis command (Lua script calls,
734    /// GET/SET for cache). A timed-out command is treated as a backend
735    /// error and the fail-open policy applies. 0 means no per-request
736    /// timeout (use the connection's default).
737    #[serde(
738        default = "default_redis_request_timeout_ms",
739        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_redis_request_timeout_ms"
740    )]
741    pub request_timeout_ms: u64,
742}
743
744impl Default for RedisHaConfig {
745    fn default() -> Self {
746        RedisHaConfig {
747            topology: RedisTopology::Single,
748            nodes: Vec::new(),
749            master_name: None,
750            request_timeout_ms: default_redis_request_timeout_ms(),
751        }
752    }
753}
754
755fn is_default_redis_topology(t: &RedisTopology) -> bool {
756    *t == RedisTopology::default()
757}
758
759fn default_redis_request_timeout_ms() -> u64 {
760    500
761}
762
763fn is_default_redis_request_timeout_ms(v: &u64) -> bool {
764    *v == default_redis_request_timeout_ms()
765}
766
767/// Distributed Redis-backed GCRA rate limiter config (DW-031,
768/// `gateway.redis_rate_limiter`).
769///
770/// When present and the `ent` cargo feature is compiled in AND a valid
771/// license with the `redis_rate_limiter` feature claim is loaded, the gateway
772/// uses a Redis-backed GCRA rate limiter instead of the local in-memory
773/// one — so two or more gateway instances share one rate limit per key. The
774/// same stacked-window GCRA semantics run, but the per-key TAT lives in
775/// Redis and is updated atomically via a Lua script in a single round-trip.
776/// When the `ent` feature is NOT compiled in, or the license lacks the
777/// claim, the block is accepted but inert (the local GCRA limiter is used).
778/// When Redis is unreachable, `fail_open` (default true) lets requests
779/// through; set it to false to reject with 429 instead.
780#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
781#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
782pub struct RedisRateLimiterConfig {
783    /// Redis connection URL (e.g. `redis://127.0.0.1:6379` or
784    /// `redis-cluster://...`). The connection is established once at
785    /// startup and pooled via a multiplexed `ConnectionManager`.
786    pub url: String,
787    /// Fail-open behavior when Redis is unreachable (default true):
788    /// `true` allows the request (no rate limiting); `false` rejects
789    /// with 429. Fail-open is the safer default for availability — a
790    /// Redis outage should not take down the gateway — but operators
791    /// who need hard limits can set this to `false`.
792    #[serde(default = "default_true", skip_serializing_if = "is_true")]
793    pub fail_open: bool,
794    /// Prefix for rate-limit keys in Redis (default `dwara:rl:`). Each
795    /// rule's per-key bucket is stored as `{prefix}{key}:{window_index}`.
796    #[serde(
797        default = "default_redis_key_prefix",
798        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_redis_key_prefix"
799    )]
800    pub key_prefix: String,
801    /// Connection timeout in milliseconds (default 1000; validated to
802    /// 100..=30 000). The timeout applies to the initial connection
803    /// establishment at startup, not to per-request Lua script calls
804    /// (those use the pooled connection's own timeout).
805    #[serde(
806        default = "default_redis_connection_timeout_ms",
807        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_redis_connection_timeout_ms"
808    )]
809    pub connection_timeout_ms: u64,
810    /// TTL for rate-limit keys in Redis, in seconds (default 3600).
811    /// Stale keys auto-expire so a key that stops receiving traffic
812    /// does not accumulate forever. The per-window GCRA Lua script
813    /// also sets an EXPIRE based on the burst tolerance; this value is
814    /// a floor that ensures cleanup even for long-burst windows.
815    #[serde(
816        default = "default_redis_key_ttl_s",
817        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_redis_key_ttl_s"
818    )]
819    pub key_ttl_s: u64,
820    /// HA and topology config (SCALE-11, #248). Controls whether the
821    /// gateway connects to a single Redis, a Sentinel deployment, or a
822    /// Redis Cluster. Defaults to single-instance mode (the existing
823    /// behavior).
824    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_default_redis_ha")]
825    pub ha: RedisHaConfig,
826}
827
828/// Sentinel: returns true when the `RedisHaConfig` is at its default
829/// (single topology, no nodes, no master name, default request
830/// timeout). Used by `skip_serializing_if` to keep the config output
831/// clean for the common single-Redis case.
832fn is_default_redis_ha(ha: &RedisHaConfig) -> bool {
833    ha == &RedisHaConfig::default()
834}

836/// Distributed Redis-backed consumer request quotas config (DW-155,
837/// `gateway.redis_quotas`).
838///
839/// When present and the `ent` cargo feature is compiled in AND a valid
840/// license with the `redis_quotas` feature claim is loaded, the gateway
841/// uses a Redis-backed quota checker instead of the local SQLite-backed
842/// one — so two or more gateway instances share one quota counter per
843/// (consumer, budget, window). The same daily/monthly budget semantics
844/// run, but the counters live in Redis and are updated atomically via a
845/// Lua script in a single round-trip. When the `ent` feature is NOT
846/// compiled in, or the license lacks the claim, the block is accepted
847/// but inert (the local SQLite checker is used). When Redis is
848/// unreachable, `fail_open` (default true) lets requests through; set
849/// it to false to reject with 429 instead.
850#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
851#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
852pub struct RedisQuotaConfig {
853    /// Redis connection URL (e.g. `redis://127.0.0.1:6379` or
854    /// `redis-cluster://...`). The connection is established once at
855    /// startup and pooled via a multiplexed `ConnectionManager`.
856    /// May be the same Redis instance as the rate limiter
857    /// (`redis_rate_limiter.url`) — the key prefixes are distinct.
858    pub url: String,
859    /// Fail-open behavior when Redis is unreachable (default true):
860    /// `true` allows the request (no quota enforcement); `false`
861    /// rejects with 429. Fail-open is the safer default for
862    /// availability — a Redis outage should not take down the gateway
863    /// — but operators who need hard quotas can set this to `false`.
864    #[serde(default = "default_true", skip_serializing_if = "is_true")]
865    pub fail_open: bool,
866    /// Prefix for quota keys in Redis (default `dwara:quota:`). Each
867    /// (consumer, budget, window) counter is stored as
868    /// `{prefix}{consumer_id}:{budget}:{window_start}`.
869    #[serde(
870        default = "default_redis_quota_key_prefix",
871        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_redis_quota_key_prefix"
872    )]
873    pub key_prefix: String,
874    /// Connection timeout in milliseconds (default 1000; validated to
875    /// 100..=30 000). The timeout applies to the initial connection
876    /// establishment at startup, not to per-request Lua script calls.
877    #[serde(
878        default = "default_redis_connection_timeout_ms",
879        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_redis_connection_timeout_ms"
880    )]
881    pub connection_timeout_ms: u64,
882    /// HA and topology config (SCALE-11, #248). Same shape as
883    /// `redis_rate_limiter.ha`.
884    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_default_redis_ha")]
885    pub ha: RedisHaConfig,
886}
887
888fn default_redis_quota_key_prefix() -> String {
889    "dwara:quota:".to_string()
890}
891
892fn is_default_redis_quota_key_prefix(p: &str) -> bool {
893    p == "dwara:quota:"
894}

896fn default_redis_key_prefix() -> String {
897    "dwara:rl:".to_string()
898}
899
900fn is_default_redis_key_prefix(p: &str) -> bool {
901    p == "dwara:rl:"
902}

904fn default_redis_connection_timeout_ms() -> u64 {
905    1000
906}

909    *v == 1000
910}

913    3600

917    *v == 3600

920/// Distributed Redis-backed response cache config (SCALE-04, #183,
921/// `gateway.redis_cache`, ent feature).
922///
923/// When present and the `ent` cargo feature is compiled in AND a valid
924/// license with the `redis_cache` feature claim is loaded, the gateway
925/// uses a Redis-backed response cache instead of (or in addition to)
926/// the local in-memory moka cache — so two or more gateway instances
927/// share one response cache. Fleet-wide cache hit ratios replace N x
928/// per-instance cold caches, particularly impactful for canary and
929/// rolling-deploy scenarios where new instances start empty. When the
930/// `ent` feature is NOT compiled in, or the license lacks the claim,
931/// the block is accepted but inert (the local moka cache is used and a
932/// one-line notice is logged at startup).
933#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
934#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
935pub struct RedisCacheConfig {
936    /// Redis connection URL (e.g. `redis://127.0.0.1:6379` or
937    /// `redis-cluster://...`). The connection is established once at
938    /// startup with the configured timeout.
939    pub url: String,
940    /// Prefix for cache keys in Redis (default `dwara:cache:`). Each
941    /// cached response is stored as `{prefix}{key}`.
942    #[serde(
943        default = "default_redis_cache_key_prefix",
944        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_redis_cache_key_prefix"
945    )]
946    pub key_prefix: String,
947    /// Connection timeout in milliseconds (default 1000; validated to
948    /// 100..=30 000).
949    #[serde(
950        default = "default_redis_connection_timeout_ms",
951        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_redis_connection_timeout_ms"
952    )]
953    pub connection_timeout_ms: u64,
954    /// Whether to keep a local fronting cache (two-tier: local moka +
955    /// Redis). Default true: the local cache fronts Redis for hot keys,
956    /// and Redis Pub/Sub invalidation evicts local entries when another
957    /// instance purges a key. Set to false for a pure Redis cache (no
958    /// local tier).
959    #[serde(default = "default_true", skip_serializing_if = "is_true")]
960    pub local_tier: bool,
961    /// HA and topology config (SCALE-11, #248). Same shape as
962    /// `redis_rate_limiter.ha`.
963    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_default_redis_ha")]
964    pub ha: RedisHaConfig,
965}
966
967fn default_redis_cache_key_prefix() -> String {
968    "dwara:cache:".to_string()
969}
970
971fn is_default_redis_cache_key_prefix(p: &str) -> bool {
972    p == "dwara:cache:"
973}

975/// Config convergence config (DW-054, `gateway.config_convergence`).
976///
977/// When present and the `ent` cargo feature is compiled in AND a valid
978/// license with the `config_convergence` feature claim is loaded, the
979/// gateway publishes its config generation to a shared backend and
980/// polls for generations published by other instances, converging to
981/// the highest generation within `poll_interval_ms`. The backend is
982/// the seam for etcd/Consul (deferred); v1 ships a Redis
983/// implementation. The coordinator runs ALONGSIDE the local file
984/// watcher: a local file change triggers a local reload plus a publish
985/// to the backend; a remote change (detected by polling) triggers a
986/// remote reload. Both go through `compile_and_publish`. When the
987/// `ent` feature is NOT compiled in, or the license lacks the claim,
988/// the block is accepted but inert (the local file watcher runs
989/// alone).
990#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
991#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
992pub struct ConfigConvergenceConfig {
993    /// Master switch. Default false: convergence is inert and the
994    /// gateway serves only its local config (the local file watcher
995    /// still runs). When true (and licensed), the coordinator
996    /// publishes and polls.
997    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
998    pub enabled: bool,
999    /// Convergence backend type. v1 ships `"redis"`; etcd and Consul
1000    /// are deferred behind the `ConfigConvergenceBackend` trait (the
1001    /// value is the seam a future backend plugs into). Validation
1002    /// rejects any value other than `"redis"`.
1003    pub backend: String,
1004    /// Redis connection URL (e.g. `redis://127.0.0.1:6379`), required
1005    /// when `backend = "redis"`. The connection is established once at
1006    /// startup and pooled via a multiplexed `ConnectionManager`.
1007    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1008    pub redis_url: Option<String>,
1009    /// Prefix for convergence keys in Redis (default `dwara:config`).
1010    /// Instance generations live under `{prefix}:instances` and config
1011    /// bodies under `{prefix}:config`.
1012    #[serde(
1013        default = "default_config_convergence_key_prefix",
1014        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_config_convergence_key_prefix"
1015    )]
1016    pub key_prefix: String,
1017    /// How often to poll the backend for generations published by
1018    /// other instances, in milliseconds (default 1000; validated to
1019    /// 100..=60 000). The done-when targets sub-second convergence;
1020    /// lower values converge faster at the cost of backend load.
1021    #[serde(
1022        default = "default_config_convergence_poll_interval_ms",
1023        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_config_convergence_poll_interval_ms"
1024    )]
1025    pub poll_interval_ms: u64,
1026    /// How often to read all instances' generations and report drift,
1027    /// in milliseconds (default 5000; validated to 1 000..=300 000).
1028    /// Drift is reported (structured log + metric) when instances
1029    /// serve different config hashes.
1030    #[serde(
1031        default = "default_config_convergence_drift_check_interval_ms",
1032        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_config_convergence_drift_check_interval_ms"
1033    )]
1034    pub drift_check_interval_ms: u64,
1035    /// Fail-open behavior when the convergence backend is unreachable
1036    /// (default true): `true` keeps serving the local config
1037    /// generation (convergence pauses until the backend recovers);
1038    /// `false` refuses to start at cold start when the backend cannot
1039    /// be reached. Fail-open is the safer default for availability --
1040    /// a backend outage should not take down the gateway.
1041    #[serde(default = "default_true", skip_serializing_if = "is_true")]
1042    pub fail_open: bool,
1043}
1044
1045fn default_config_convergence_key_prefix() -> String {
1046    "dwara:config".to_string()
1047}
1048
1049fn is_default_config_convergence_key_prefix(p: &str) -> bool {
1050    p == "dwara:config"
1051}
1052
1053fn default_config_convergence_poll_interval_ms() -> u64 {
1054    1000
1055}
1056
1057fn is_default_config_convergence_poll_interval_ms(v: &u64) -> bool {
1058    *v == 1000
1059}
1060
1061fn default_config_convergence_drift_check_interval_ms() -> u64 {
1062    5000
1063}
1064
1065fn is_default_config_convergence_drift_check_interval_ms(v: &u64) -> bool {
1066    *v == 5000
1067}

1069/// Fleet operations config (DW-098, `gateway.fleet`).
1070///
1071/// Configures the version-skew policy and rolling-upgrade order for a
1072/// fleet of edge instances managed by a controller (CP/DP split,
1073/// DW-066). When present and the `ent` cargo feature is compiled in
1074/// AND a valid license with the `fleet_operations` feature claim is
1075/// loaded, the controller checks each connecting edge's version
1076/// against the skew policy and the admin API exposes fleet status
1077/// endpoints. When the `ent` feature is NOT compiled in, or the
1078/// license lacks the claim, the block is accepted but inert.
1079#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1080#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1081pub struct FleetConfig {
1082    /// Master switch. Default false: fleet operations are inert and
1083    /// the controller accepts edges regardless of version. When true
1084    /// (and licensed), the skew policy is enforced.
1085    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
1086    pub enabled: bool,
1087    /// The rolling-upgrade and version-skew policy.
1088    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1089    pub upgrade: Option<FleetUpgradeConfig>,
1090    /// The controller's own version (the reference version edges are
1091    /// checked against). When absent, the gateway binary's
1092    /// `CARGO_PKG_VERSION` is used.
1093    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1094    pub controller_version: Option<String>,
1095    /// How long an edge can go without being seen (no ack, no
1096    /// re-registration) before it is flagged stale, in seconds
1097    /// (default 60; validated to 5..=3600). Stale edges are reported
1098    /// by `GET /fleet/status` but are NOT automatically removed — the
1099    /// operator decides whether to drain or wait.
1100    #[serde(
1101        default = "default_fleet_stale_timeout_secs",
1102        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_fleet_stale_timeout_secs"
1103    )]
1104    pub stale_timeout_secs: u64,
1105}

1107/// API lifecycle management config (DW-110, `gateway.lifecycle`).
1108///
1109/// Three sub-concerns, each additive and independently enabled:
1110///
1111/// - `portal` -- the developer portal: a read-only static HTML page
1112///   auto-generated from the configured OpenAPI spec sources, served
1113///   at a reserved path.
1114/// - `profiles` -- environment profiles: dev/staging/prod config
1115///   overlays. The profile is selected via the `DWARA_PROFILE` env
1116///   var; the selected profile's patch is merged onto the base config
1117///   at load time.
1118/// - `journey` -- the API journey recorder: records the request flow
1119///   through the gateway as a JSON document for debugging, stored via
1120///   the existing analytics raw table.
1121///
1122/// The block is accepted in every build and is inert until the
1123/// runtime wiring lands (validation warns).
1124#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1125#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1126pub struct LifecycleConfig {
1127    /// The developer portal config. Absent (the default): no portal.
1128    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1129    pub portal: Option<LifecyclePortalConfig>,
1130    /// Environment profile overlays. Absent (the default): no
1131    /// overlays; the base config is used as-is.
1132    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1133    pub profiles: Option<LifecycleProfilesConfig>,
1134    /// The journey recorder config. Absent (the default): no
1135    /// journeys are recorded.
1136    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1137    pub journey: Option<LifecycleJourneyConfig>,
1138}
1139
1140/// Developer portal config (DW-110, `gateway.lifecycle.portal`).
1141///
1142/// The portal is a read-only static HTML page that aggregates the
1143/// configured OpenAPI specs. `enabled` defaults to false: even with
1144/// the `api_lifecycle` feature compiled in, the portal is inert until
1145/// `enabled: true`.
1146#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1147#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1148pub struct LifecyclePortalConfig {
1149    /// Master switch. Default false: the portal is not served.
1150    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
1151    pub enabled: bool,
1152    /// The URL path the portal is served at (a reserved HTTP path,
1153    /// shadowing any configured route, like `/healthz`). Must start
1154    /// with `/`. Default `/portal`.
1155    #[serde(
1156        default = "default_lifecycle_portal_path",
1157        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_lifecycle_portal_path"
1158    )]
1159    pub path: String,
1160    /// The OpenAPI spec sources to aggregate. Each source is either a
1161    /// `file` path or a `url` (an upstream `/openapi.json` endpoint).
1162    /// Empty (the default): the portal renders an empty listing.
1163    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
1164    pub specs: Vec<LifecyclePortalSpec>,
1165}
1166
1167fn default_lifecycle_portal_path() -> String {
1168    "/portal".to_string()
1169}
1170
1171fn is_default_lifecycle_portal_path(p: &String) -> bool {
1172    p == "/portal"
1173}
1174
1175/// One OpenAPI spec source for the developer portal (DW-110,
1176/// `gateway.lifecycle.portal.specs[]`).
1177///
1178/// Either a `file` path (read from disk) or a `url` (an upstream
1179/// `/openapi.json` endpoint, fetched at render time). Exactly one of
1180/// `file`/`url` must be set; validation rejects a spec with neither
1181/// or both.
1182#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1183#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1184pub struct LifecyclePortalSpec {
1185    /// A file path to an OpenAPI 3.x JSON document on disk. The file
1186    /// must exist and be readable (validation checks this at config
1187    /// publish time).
1188    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1189    pub file: Option<String>,
1190    /// An upstream `/openapi.json` endpoint URL (http or https). The
1191    /// portal fetches the spec from this URL at render time. NOT
1192    /// fetched at validation time (the upstream may be down at publish
1193    /// time but up at render time).
1194    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1195    pub url: Option<String>,
1196    /// An optional display name for the spec. When absent, the portal
1197    /// derives the name from the spec's `info.title` (or the file name
1198    /// / URL host when the spec cannot be parsed).
1199    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1200    pub name: Option<String>,
1201}

1203/// Environment profile overlays config (DW-110,
1204/// `gateway.lifecycle.profiles`).
1205///
1206/// Carries a base config (as a YAML string) plus per-profile config
1207/// patches. The profile is selected via the `DWARA_PROFILE` env var;
1208/// the selected profile's patch is merged onto the base at load time.
1209#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1210#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1211pub struct LifecycleProfilesConfig {
1212    /// The base config, as a YAML string. This is the config that
1213    /// applies when no profile is selected (or the selected profile
1214    /// has no patch).
1215    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "String::is_empty")]
1216    pub base_config: String,
1217    /// Per-profile config patches, keyed by profile name (`dev`,
1218    /// `staging`, `prod`). Each value is a YAML string merged onto the
1219    /// base config when that profile is selected.
1220    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::collections::BTreeMap::is_empty")]
1221    pub profile_overrides: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, String>,
1222    /// Directory of file-based profile patches (CFG-01, #180). When
1223    /// set, the selected profile's patch is loaded from
1224    /// `<profiles_dir>/<profile>.yaml` instead of the inline
1225    /// `profile_overrides` map. Relative paths resolve against the
1226    /// config file's directory. Takes precedence over
1227    /// `profile_overrides` when both are set.
1228    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1229    pub profiles_dir: Option<String>,
1230}

1232/// Journey recorder config (DW-110, `gateway.lifecycle.journey`).
1233#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1234#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1235pub struct LifecycleJourneyConfig {
1236    /// Master switch. Default false: no journeys are recorded.
1237    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
1238    pub enabled: bool,
1239    /// The retention window for in-memory journeys, in hours. Must be
1240    /// greater than 0 (validation rejects 0). The durable copy's
1241    /// retention is the raw table's own retention; this cap only bounds
1242    /// the in-memory ring buffer. Default 24.
1243    #[serde(
1244        default = "default_lifecycle_journey_retention",
1245        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_lifecycle_journey_retention"
1246    )]
1247    pub retention_hours: u64,
1248}

1250fn default_lifecycle_journey_retention() -> u64 {
1251    24
1252}
