1254fn is_default_lifecycle_journey_retention(h: &u64) -> bool {
1255    *h == 24
1256}
1257
1258/// Rolling-upgrade and version-skew policy (DW-098,
1259/// `gateway.fleet.upgrade`).
1260#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1261#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1262pub struct FleetUpgradeConfig {
1263    /// The version-skew policy: how tolerant the controller is of
1264    /// edges running a different version.
1265    ///
1266    /// - `"allow"` — accept any version (no skew check).
1267    /// - `"allow_minor_skew"` (default) — edges must match the
1268    ///   controller's major version and be within 1 minor version
1269    ///   (N-1 or N+1).
1270    /// - `"require_exact"` — edges must match the controller's version
1271    ///   exactly.
1272    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_default_version_skew_policy")]
1273    pub skew: VersionSkewPolicyConfig,
1274    /// The upgrade order: which edges to upgrade first, identified by
1275    /// label selector. Edges matching earlier entries are upgraded
1276    /// before edges matching later entries. An edge matches an entry
1277    /// when it has ALL the labels in the entry's `labels` map. Empty
1278    /// (the default) means no ordering — all edges are upgraded
1279    /// simultaneously (canary/zone-based rollouts are configured by
1280    /// adding entries here).
1281    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
1282    pub order: Vec<FleetUpgradeOrderEntry>,
1283    /// The maximum number of edges that may be upgraded
1284    /// simultaneously (a rolling-upgrade concurrency cap). 0 (the
1285    /// default) means no cap — all matching edges are upgraded at
1286    /// once. A typical canary rollout sets this to 1.
1287    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_zero_u32")]
1288    pub max_concurrent: u32,
1289    /// Whether to fail the rollout if any edge reports a failed ack
1290    /// (applied: false). Default true: the controller stops pushing
1291    /// to further edges when one fails. false: the controller
1292    /// continues pushing to all matching edges regardless of
1293    /// individual failures (the operator monitors and rolls back
1294    /// manually).
1295    #[serde(default = "default_true", skip_serializing_if = "is_true")]
1296    pub halt_on_failure: bool,
1297}
1298
1299/// A label-selector entry in the upgrade order list.
1300#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1301#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1302pub struct FleetUpgradeOrderEntry {
1303    /// A name for this upgrade wave (for logging and the status
1304    /// endpoint). Must be non-empty.
1305    pub name: String,
1306    /// The label selector: an edge matches this entry when it has
1307    /// ALL of these labels (key=value). Must be non-empty.
1308    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::collections::HashMap::is_empty")]
1309    pub labels: std::collections::HashMap<String, String>,
1310}
1311
1312/// The version-skew policy, mirroring the CP/DP
1313/// `cluster_sync::VersionSkewPolicy` but living in the config schema
1314/// (so the gateway config carries it, not just the controller
1315/// runtime).
1316#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema, Default)]
1317#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case", deny_unknown_fields)]
1318pub enum VersionSkewPolicyConfig {
1319    /// Allow any version skew. The edge accepts the config regardless
1320    /// of version.
1321    Allow,
1322    /// Allow skew within a minor version (e.g. 1.2.x can talk to
1323    /// 1.3.x, but not 2.0.x). This is the default.
1324    #[default]
1325    AllowMinorSkew,
1326    /// Require exact version match. The edge rejects the config if
1327    /// its version differs from the controller's.
1328    RequireExact,
1329}
1330
1331fn default_fleet_stale_timeout_secs() -> u64 {
1332    60
1333}
1334
1335fn is_default_fleet_stale_timeout_secs(v: &u64) -> bool {
1336    *v == 60
1337}
1338
1339fn is_default_version_skew_policy(p: &VersionSkewPolicyConfig) -> bool {
1340    *p == VersionSkewPolicyConfig::AllowMinorSkew
1341}
1342
1343fn is_zero_u32(v: &u32) -> bool {
1344    *v == 0
1345}
1346
1347/// The embedded analytics store config (DW-043, `gateway.analytics`).
1348#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1349#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1350pub struct AnalyticsConfig {
1351    /// Filesystem path of the analytics SQLite database. The file is
1352    /// created (and migrated) on first open. A separate file from the
1353    /// state store on purpose: retention deletes and incremental
1354    /// vacuum churn must never compact the identity store.
1355    pub path: String,
1356    /// Maximum batch latency of the background writer, in
1357    /// milliseconds (default 1000; validated to [100, 60 000]). Not a
1358    /// correctness knob — the rollup grace covers multiples of this.
1359    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1360    pub flush_ms: Option<u64>,
1361    /// Per-store retention, in milliseconds (defaults: raw 24 h, 1m
1362    /// 48 h, 5m 7 d, 1h 30 d, 1d 365 d). Validation enforces each
1363    /// floor (a granularity must outlive its own window comfortably)
1364    /// and monotonicity (no coarser table may expire before a finer
1365    /// one).
1366    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1367    pub retention: Option<AnalyticsRetention>,
1368    /// Custom dimensions (DW-043): request-header-sourced tags that
1369    /// become analytics dimensions — `x-plan` as dimension `plan`,
1370    /// for consumer tier / plan / feature-flag style cuts the fixed
1371    /// dimensions cannot express. Extracted at request completion
1372    /// capture; analytics-only (never added to the access log). At
1373    /// most 16 (cardinality guard); values are capped at 128 bytes
1374    /// (longer values are skipped for that request).
1375    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
1376    pub dimensions: Vec<AnalyticsDimension>,
1377    /// Scheduled usage-report exports (DW-120): durable CSV/JSON
1378    /// dumps of the per-consumer usage statement, one file set per
1379    /// closed UTC calendar window, written by a background worker
1380    /// that reuses the analytics store's scheduling machinery. Absent
1381    /// (the default): no exports run and the admin export endpoints
1382    /// answer that exports are not configured. See the
1383    /// `analytics::exports` module docs for the schedule, the
1384    /// statement's reconciliation contract with the query API, and
1385    /// the quota-column window-alignment rule.
1386    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1387    pub exports: Option<AnalyticsExports>,
1388    /// Live in-process sketches (DW-092): sub-second-freshness
1389    /// aggregation maintained synchronously on the request-completion
1390    /// hot path (per-route rolling window with counts, errors, and
1391    /// latency samples). Never blocks the dataplane — the sketch is
1392    /// updated in place under atomics and a short mutex over a capped
1393    /// sample vector. Absent (the default): no live sketches; the
1394    /// `GET /analytics/live` endpoint answers
1395    /// `analytics_not_configured`.
1396    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1397    pub live_sketches: Option<AnalyticsLiveSketches>,
1398    /// ML traffic insights (DW-092): EWMA-based capacity forecasting
1399    /// and seasonal-baseline anomaly detection over the live sketch
1400    /// window rotations. The insights engine is in-process and
1401    /// hand-rolled (no ML crates): a minute-of-day ring buffer holds
1402    /// the seasonal pattern, an EWMA over recent windows carries the
1403    /// trend, and anomaly detection compares the current window's
1404    /// shape to the seasonal baseline. Absent (the default): no
1405    /// forecasting or anomaly detection; the
1406    /// `GET /analytics/forecast` and `GET /analytics/anomalies`
1407    /// endpoints answer `analytics_not_configured`.
1408    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1409    pub insights: Option<AnalyticsInsights>,
1410    /// Replay time-travel debugging capture (DW-102): opt-in capture of
1411    /// request detail (redacted headers, auth identity) into the raw
1412    /// analytics table for offline replay against candidate configs.
1413    /// Absent (the default): no replay capture; the raw table's
1414    /// `request_headers_redacted` and `auth_identity` columns stay
1415    /// NULL. When present with `enabled: true`, the fire-and-forget
1416    /// analytics writer records the redacted headers and auth identity
1417    /// alongside the existing access fields, bounded by `retain_hours`.
1418    /// See [`AnalyticsReplayCapture`].
1419    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1420    pub replay_capture: Option<AnalyticsReplayCapture>,
1421    /// REL-10 (#221): sampled degradation under channel pressure. When
1422    /// the analytics channel's fill ratio exceeds `high_watermark`,
1423    /// the offer path probabilistically drops records (keeping 1 in
1424    /// `sample_rate`) BEFORE the channel fills, so a sustained burst
1425    /// degrades to sampled telemetry rather than a hard drop cliff.
1426    /// Below `low_watermark` every record is kept. Defaults to off
1427    /// (no sampling); when enabled, `high_watermark` must be >
1428    /// `low_watermark` and both are in (0.0, 1.0].
1429    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1430    pub sampled_degradation: Option<AnalyticsSampledDegradation>,
1431}
1432
1433/// Sampled degradation config (REL-10, #221). See
1434/// [`AnalyticsConfig::sampled_degradation`].
1435#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1436#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1437pub struct AnalyticsSampledDegradation {
1438    /// Channel fill ratio above which sampling kicks in. Must be in
1439    /// (0.0, 1.0) and > `low_watermark`.
1440    #[serde(default = "default_sampled_high_watermark")]
1441    pub high_watermark: f64,
1442    /// Channel fill ratio below which sampling stops (every record
1443    /// kept). Must be in (0.0, 1.0) and < `high_watermark`.
1444    #[serde(default = "default_sampled_low_watermark")]
1445    pub low_watermark: f64,
1446    /// Fraction of records to KEEP when sampling (1 in N). 1.0 keeps
1447    /// all (no sampling); 0.1 keeps 10%. Must be in (0.0, 1.0].
1448    #[serde(default = "default_sampled_keep_rate")]
1449    pub keep_rate: f64,
1450}
1451
1452fn default_sampled_high_watermark() -> f64 {
1453    0.8
1454}
1455
1456fn default_sampled_low_watermark() -> f64 {
1457    0.5
1458}
1459
1460fn default_sampled_keep_rate() -> f64 {
1461    0.1
1462}
1463
1464/// Live in-process sketches config (DW-092, `analytics.live_sketches`).
1465///
1466/// The sketches are maintained inside the embedded analytics runtime
1467/// and updated on every `record()` call — a per-route rolling window
1468/// (size = `freshness_target_ms`) with request/error counts and a capped
1469/// latency-sample vector. Percentiles are computed at snapshot time
1470/// (sort-and-pick, O(n log n) with n capped at 1000), never on the hot
1471/// path. No new dependencies: the sketches are hand-rolled with atomics
1472/// and a short mutex, the same approach as the load-generator's
1473/// histogram.
1474#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1475#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1476pub struct AnalyticsLiveSketches {
1477    /// Whether live sketches are maintained (default true when the
1478    /// block is present). Setting false disables the in-process
1479    /// sketches while keeping the block present (a no-op shape that
1480    /// reads as coverage).
1481    #[serde(default = "default_true", skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
1482    pub enabled: bool,
1483    /// The rolling window size in milliseconds (default 500). Also the
1484    /// freshness target: a snapshot reflects at most this much
1485    /// staleness. Validated to [100, 5000] — below 100 is timer churn,
1486    /// above 5000 is no longer "live".
1487    #[serde(
1488        default = "default_freshness_target",
1489        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_freshness_target"
1490    )]
1491    pub freshness_target_ms: u64,
1492}
1493
1494fn is_default_freshness_target(v: &u64) -> bool {
1495    *v == 500
1496}
1497
1498/// ML traffic insights config (DW-092, `analytics.insights`).
1499///
1500/// The insights engine is hand-rolled (no ML crates): an EWMA over
1501/// recent sketch-window rotations carries the trend, a minute-of-day
1502/// ring buffer (1440 entries) holds the seasonal baseline, and anomaly
1503/// detection compares the current window's shape to the seasonal
1504/// baseline with a configurable factor. Forecasting combines the
1505/// seasonal average for the next minute with the EWMA trend
1506/// adjustment.
1507#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1508#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1509pub struct AnalyticsInsights {
1510    /// Whether capacity forecasting is enabled (default false). When
1511    /// true, the `GET /analytics/forecast` endpoint returns a
1512    /// prediction for the next window.
1513    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
1514    pub forecast: bool,
1515    /// Whether seasonal-baseline anomaly detection is enabled (default
1516    /// false). When true, the `GET /analytics/anomalies` endpoint
1517    /// returns the current window's anomaly status.
1518    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
1519    pub anomaly_baseline: bool,
1520    /// The number of recent windows retained for the EWMA trend and
1521    /// the seasonal baseline ring (default 1440 = 24 hours of 1-minute
1522    /// windows). Validated to be > 0 when forecast or anomaly_baseline
1523    /// is enabled.
1524    #[serde(
1525        default = "default_baseline_windows",
1526        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_baseline_windows"
1527    )]
1528    pub baseline_windows: u64,
1529}
1530
1531fn is_default_baseline_windows(v: &u64) -> bool {
1532    *v == 1440
1533}
1534
1535/// Replay time-travel debugging capture config (DW-102,
1536/// `analytics.replay_capture`). Opt-in capture of request detail
1537/// (redacted headers, auth identity) into the raw analytics table for
1538/// offline replay against candidate configs. The capture reuses the
1539/// existing fire-and-forget analytics writer path and the existing PII
1540/// redaction patterns (`ai::redaction`); body capture is OFF by
1541/// default (PII risk).
1542#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1543#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1544pub struct AnalyticsReplayCapture {
1545    /// Whether replay capture is active (default false — opt-in). When
1546    /// false the raw table's `request_headers_redacted` and
1547    /// `auth_identity` columns stay NULL even when the block is
1548    /// present (a no-op shape that reads as coverage).
1549    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
1550    pub enabled: bool,
1551    /// Time-bounded retention for replay-captured raw rows, in hours
1552    /// (default 24). Rows older than the retention window are deleted
1553    /// by the existing raw retention sweep. Validated to be > 0.
1554    #[serde(
1555        default = "default_replay_retain_hours",
1556        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_replay_retain_hours"
1557    )]
1558    pub retain_hours: u64,
1559    /// Whether to capture redacted request headers (default true when
1560    /// the block is present). Headers are scrubbed via the existing PII
1561    /// redaction patterns before storage.
1562    #[serde(default = "default_true", skip_serializing_if = "is_true")]
1563    pub capture_headers: bool,
1564    /// Whether to capture the request body (default false — PII risk).
1565    /// Body capture is off by default; enabling it is an explicit
1566    /// operator decision to accept the PII exposure of stored bodies
1567    /// (even redacted bodies may carry residual sensitive content).
1568    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
1569    pub capture_body: bool,
1570}
1571
1572fn default_replay_retain_hours() -> u64 {
1573    24
1574}
1575
1576fn is_default_replay_retain_hours(v: &u64) -> bool {
1577    *v == 24
1578}

1580/// Scheduled usage-report exports (DW-120, `analytics.exports`).
1581///
1582/// The exports worker ticks on the same background machinery as the
1583/// rollup cascade, closes each UTC calendar window of the configured
1584/// [`AnalyticsExportWindow`] kind once its data has settled, and
1585/// writes one file per configured format into `directory` (created on
1586/// demand; unwritable directories fail the run LOUD in the run record
1587/// and logs — never the dataplane). Parquet is deliberately NOT a v1
1588/// format: the arrow/parquet dependency weight is deferred (see the
1589/// DW-156 backlog issue).
1590#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1591#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1592pub struct AnalyticsExports {
1593    /// Directory the export files are written into (created on demand
1594    /// if missing). One deterministic file per (window, format); a
1595    /// re-export of the same window overwrites it (idempotent output,
1596    /// the rollup recompute philosophy).
1597    pub directory: String,
1598    /// The schedule/window kind (fixed UTC calendar windows). Default
1599    /// `daily` (midnight-to-midnight UTC — aligned with the quota
1600    /// daily budget's window, so a daily statement's quota column is
1601    /// exactly that day's counter).
1602    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1603    pub window: Option<AnalyticsExportWindow>,
1604    /// Output formats, a subset of `csv`/`json`; omitted or empty
1605    /// means BOTH csv and json (the default). Validation rejects only
1606    /// duplicates.
1607    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
1608    pub formats: Vec<AnalyticsExportFormat>,
1609}
1610
1611/// One export schedule kind (DW-120, `analytics.exports.window`).
1612/// Closed set; fixed UTC calendar windows.
1613#[derive(
1614    Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema,
1615)]
1616#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
1617pub enum AnalyticsExportWindow {
1618    Hourly,
1619    Daily,
1620    Monthly,
1621}
1622
1623/// One export output format (DW-120, `analytics.exports.formats[]`).
1624/// Closed set: `csv`, `json`, `parquet_csv`.
1625///
1626/// SCALE-12 (#190): `parquet_csv` writes a CSV file with a
1627/// `.parquet.csv` extension and a JSON metadata header describing
1628/// the schema in Parquet-compatible terms. The operator converts it
1629/// to true Parquet using an external tool (e.g., `duckdb -c "COPY
1630/// input.parquet.csv TO output.parquet (FORMAT PARQUET)"`). This
1631/// avoids adding the heavy `arrow`/`parquet` crate stack while
1632/// providing a clear path to Parquet output.
1633#[derive(
1634    Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema,
1635)]
1636#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
1637pub enum AnalyticsExportFormat {
1638    Csv,
1639    Json,
1640    /// SCALE-12 (#190): CSV with Parquet-compatible metadata header.
1641    /// The file extension is `.parquet.csv`.
1642    ParquetCsv,
1643}
1644
1645/// Per-granularity retention (DW-043, `analytics.retention`).
1646#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1647#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1648pub struct AnalyticsRetention {
1649    /// Raw access records.
1650    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1651    pub raw_ms: Option<i64>,
1652    /// The 1-minute rollup.
1653    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1654    pub m1_ms: Option<i64>,
1655    /// The 5-minute rollup.
1656    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1657    pub m5_ms: Option<i64>,
1658    /// The 1-hour rollup.
1659    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1660    pub h1_ms: Option<i64>,
1661    /// The 1-day rollup.
1662    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1663    pub d1_ms: Option<i64>,
1664}
1665
1666/// Default per-granularity retention (ms), indexed [raw, 1m, 5m, 1h,
1667/// 1d] — a week of traffic at every granularity a week query wants,
1668/// bounded disk at the 1-minute grain (DW-043). Lives in `config`
1669/// (the lowest consuming domain) so both validation and the analytics
1670/// store read ONE definition.
1671pub const ANALYTICS_DEFAULT_RETENTION_MS: [i64; 5] = [
1672    86_400_000,     // raw
1673    172_800_000,    // 1m
1674    604_800_000,    // 5m
1675    2_592_000_000,  // 1h
1676    31_536_000_000, // 1d
1677];
1678
1679impl AnalyticsRetention {
1680    /// Resolve to the effective [raw, m1, m5, h1, d1] millisecond
1681    /// set, defaulting absent entries (and clamping each to its
1682    /// floor so programmatic builders cannot under-retain below a
1683    /// window's own lifetime).
1684    pub fn effective(&self) -> [i64; 5] {
1685        let d = ANALYTICS_DEFAULT_RETENTION_MS;
1686        let floors = [
1687            5 * 60_000,      // raw: five fine windows
1688            10 * 60_000,     // m1
1689            60 * 60_000,     // m5
1690            24 * 3_600_000,  // h1
1691            30 * 86_400_000, // d1
1692        ];
1693        [
1694            self.raw_ms.unwrap_or(d[0]).max(floors[0]),
1695            self.m1_ms.unwrap_or(d[1]).max(floors[1]),
1696            self.m5_ms.unwrap_or(d[2]).max(floors[2]),
1697            self.h1_ms.unwrap_or(d[3]).max(floors[3]),
1698            self.d1_ms.unwrap_or(d[4]).max(floors[4]),
1699        ]
1700    }
1701}
1702
1703/// GeoIP allow/deny rules on the effective client IP (DW-050,
1704/// `authorization.geoip`). Countries are ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 codes
1705/// (case-insensitive in config, compared uppercase); ASNs are plain
1706/// numbers. Evaluation mirrors `allowed_consumers`/`denied_consumers`:
1707/// a denied match rejects (403) regardless of the allow lists; a
1708/// NON-EMPTY allow list admits only matches; empty allow lists
1709/// constrain nothing. The UNKNOWN case (unresolvable address, no
1710/// database) matches neither side.
1711#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1712#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1713pub struct GeoipRules {
1714    /// Countries allowed (empty = any not denied).
1715    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
1716    pub allowed_countries: Vec<String>,
1717    /// Countries rejected, even when otherwise allowed.
1718    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
1719    pub denied_countries: Vec<String>,
1720    /// Autonomous system numbers allowed (empty = any not denied).
1721    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
1722    pub allowed_asns: Vec<u32>,
1723    /// Autonomous system numbers rejected.
1724    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
1725    pub denied_asns: Vec<u32>,
1726}
1727
1728/// The GeoIP database config (DW-050, `gateway.geoip`). One MaxMind-
1729/// format database serves both country and ASN predicates (a
1730/// GeoLite2-Country, GeoLite2-ASN, or combined database — whichever
1731/// subtrees it carries are the ones the rules can use). The file is
1732/// opened at startup (a failed open is LOUD but non-fatal: the gateway
1733/// serves with geo lookups resolving UNKNOWN) and hot-reloaded: the
1734/// watcher swaps the reader when the file changes, with no restart.
1735#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1736#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1737pub struct GeoipConfig {
1738    /// Path to the .mmdb database file.
1739    pub path: String,
1740}
1741
1742/// The source of a custom analytics dimension value (DW-093). The
1743/// default (when `source` is absent) is [`DimensionSource::Header`],
1744/// preserving the original DW-043 header-only config shape.
1745#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1746#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
1747pub enum DimensionSource {
1748    /// Read the value from a request header (the original DW-043
1749    /// source; requires the `header` field).
1750    Header,
1751    /// Read the value from a verified JWT claim (requires the `claim`
1752    /// field; the claim must be present in the resolved `Identity`'s
1753    /// claims map — only string- and number-valued top-level claims
1754    /// are available, the same subset authn exposes).
1755    Claim,
1756    /// Read the value from the request body via an RFC 6901 JSON
1757    /// pointer (requires the `body_path` field; only works when the
1758    /// body is buffered — retries, hedging, request transforms, or
1759    /// request validation opt the route into buffering; the
1760    /// zero-buffering default skips body-path dimensions).
1761    BodyPath,
1762}
1763
1764/// One custom analytics dimension (DW-043,
1765/// `analytics.dimensions[]`).
1766///
1767/// DW-093 extends the source beyond headers: a `source` of `claim`
1768/// extracts from the verified JWT's claims, and `body_path` extracts
1769/// from the request body via a JSON pointer (only when the body is
1770/// buffered). The original header-only shape (`header` without
1771/// `source`) is preserved for backward compatibility.
1772#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1773#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1774pub struct AnalyticsDimension {
1775    /// The dimension name: lowercase `[a-z0-9_]`, at most 32 bytes —
1776    /// it becomes the rollup table's `dim` key and the query field.
1777    pub name: String,
1778    /// The dimension source (DW-093). Absent defaults to `header`
1779    /// (the original DW-043 behavior). When `header`, the `header`
1780    /// field is required; when `claim`, the `claim` field is
1781    /// required; when `body_path`, the `body_path` field is required.
1782    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1783    pub source: Option<DimensionSource>,
1784    /// The request header whose value is captured (case-insensitive,
1785    /// as HTTP header names are). Required when `source` is `header`
1786    /// (or absent). The FIRST value of a repeated header wins;
1787    /// non-UTF-8 and over-128-byte values are skipped.
1788    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1789    pub header: Option<String>,
1790    /// The JWT claim name whose value is captured (DW-093). Required
1791    /// when `source` is `claim`. The claim must be a string- or
1792    /// number-valued top-level claim in the verified token; other
1793    /// types and absent claims are skipped for that request.
1794    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1795    pub claim: Option<String>,
1796    /// An RFC 6901 JSON pointer into the request body (DW-093).
1797    /// Required when `source` is `body_path`. Only works when the
1798    /// body is buffered (retries, hedging, request transforms, or
1799    /// request validation); the zero-buffering default skips
1800    /// body-path dimensions silently. The body must be valid JSON;
1801    /// non-JSON bodies and unresolved pointers are skipped.
1802    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1803    pub body_path: Option<String>,
1804}
1805
1806impl AnalyticsDimension {
1807    /// The effective source (defaults to [`DimensionSource::Header`]
1808    /// when `source` is absent — the backward-compatible DW-043
1809    /// behavior).
1810    pub fn effective_source(&self) -> DimensionSource {
1811        self.source.unwrap_or(DimensionSource::Header)
1812    }
1813}
1814
1815/// One alert/event webhook target (DW-044, `gateway.webhooks[]`).
1816/// Compiled per generation into an `events::webhook::WebhookTarget`
1817/// (secret references resolved at compile time, URL decomposed); see
1818/// that module for the delivery contract.
1819#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1820#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1821pub struct Webhook {
1822    /// Absolute `http://` or `https://` URL the envelope is POSTed to.
1823    /// Host and port come from the URL; the path (and query, if any) is
1824    /// preserved. `https://` verifies against the public webpki root set
1825    /// (no `trusted_ca_file` override for webhooks in v1).
1826    pub url: String,
1827    /// Event kinds this target receives (subset of the kinds the gateway
1828    /// emits; validation rejects unknown kinds). At least one entry.
1829    pub events: Vec<String>,
1830    /// Extra headers sent on every delivery. Values may be inline or
1831    /// `${ENV_NAME}` / `${file:/path}` secret references (DW-045),
1832    /// resolved at config-compile time; inline values are redacted in
1833    /// every config echo. Use a reference for bearer tokens and signing
1834    /// secrets.
1835    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::collections::BTreeMap::is_empty")]
1836    pub headers: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, String>,
1837    /// TOTAL budget for one delivery (all retry attempts share it):
1838    /// connect, write, and response-head read per attempt plus the
1839    /// backoff waits between attempts. A slow or hung target can never
1840    /// occupy more than this. Default 2000; validation enforces
1841    /// 1..=[`limits::MAX_WEBHOOK_TIMEOUT_MS`].
1842    #[serde(
1843        default = "default_webhook_timeout_ms",
1844        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_webhook_timeout_ms"
1845    )]
1846    pub timeout_ms: u64,
1847    /// Total attempts per delivery (the first try plus retries). Retries
1848    /// happen for transport failures and the transient status set
1849    /// (429/502/503/504), honoring a seconds-form `Retry-After` when the
1850    /// target sends one. Default 3; validation enforces
1851    /// 1..=[`limits::MAX_WEBHOOK_ATTEMPTS`].
1852    #[serde(
1853        default = "default_webhook_attempts",
1854        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_webhook_attempts"
1855    )]
1856    pub max_attempts: u32,
1857    /// First backoff between delivery attempts, doubling per retry up to
1858    /// `backoff_cap_ms` (a `Retry-After` answer replaces the computed
1859    /// value for that wait). Default 100.
1860    #[serde(
1861        default = "default_webhook_backoff_base_ms",
1862        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_webhook_backoff_base_ms"
1863    )]
1864    pub backoff_base_ms: u64,
1865    /// Upper bound on the computed backoff. Default 1000; must be >=
1866    /// `backoff_base_ms`.
1867    #[serde(
1868        default = "default_webhook_backoff_cap_ms",
1869        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_webhook_backoff_cap_ms"
1870    )]
1871    pub backoff_cap_ms: u64,
1872}
1873
1874/// Default `gateway.webhooks[].timeout_ms` (DW-044): one shared
1875/// delivery budget, generous for a remote alerting endpoint, small
1876/// enough that a hung target cannot pile up deliveries.
1877pub const DEFAULT_WEBHOOK_TIMEOUT_MS: u64 = 2_000;
1878/// Default `gateway.webhooks[].max_attempts` (DW-044).
1879pub const DEFAULT_WEBHOOK_ATTEMPTS: u32 = 3;
1880/// Default `gateway.webhooks[].backoff_base_ms` (DW-044).
1881pub const DEFAULT_WEBHOOK_BACKOFF_BASE_MS: u64 = 100;
1882/// Default `gateway.webhooks[].backoff_cap_ms` (DW-044).
1883pub const DEFAULT_WEBHOOK_BACKOFF_CAP_MS: u64 = 1_000;
1884
1885fn default_webhook_timeout_ms() -> u64 {
1886    DEFAULT_WEBHOOK_TIMEOUT_MS
1887}
1888fn default_webhook_attempts() -> u32 {
1889    DEFAULT_WEBHOOK_ATTEMPTS
1890}
1891fn default_webhook_backoff_base_ms() -> u64 {
1892    DEFAULT_WEBHOOK_BACKOFF_BASE_MS
1893}
1894fn default_webhook_backoff_cap_ms() -> u64 {
1895    DEFAULT_WEBHOOK_BACKOFF_CAP_MS
1896}
1897fn is_default_webhook_timeout_ms(v: &u64) -> bool {
1898    *v == DEFAULT_WEBHOOK_TIMEOUT_MS
1899}
1900fn is_default_webhook_attempts(v: &u32) -> bool {
1901    *v == DEFAULT_WEBHOOK_ATTEMPTS
1902}
1903fn is_default_webhook_backoff_base_ms(v: &u64) -> bool {
1904    *v == DEFAULT_WEBHOOK_BACKOFF_BASE_MS
1905}
1906fn is_default_webhook_backoff_cap_ms(v: &u64) -> bool {
1907    *v == DEFAULT_WEBHOOK_BACKOFF_CAP_MS
1908}
1909
1910/// Real-time access-record stream (DW-121,
1911/// `gateway.analytics_stream`).
1912///
1913/// Every completed request's access record is copied onto a bounded
1914/// channel at request completion (drop-and-count on full — the stream
1915/// can never slow the dataplane), and a background flusher turns the
1916/// queue into ordered batches delivered to the configured sink: one
1917/// delivery per flushed BATCH, not per record. The channel capacity is
1918/// fixed at boot; the sink set, flush cadence, and batch bound are read
1919/// live from the current generation, so a reload can retarget or
1920/// disable the stream without a restart.
1921#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1922#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1923pub struct AnalyticsStreamConfig {
1924    /// Where flushed batches go. One sink in v1 (`type: webhook`); the
1925    /// set is closed so a second implementation (a Kafka producer is
1926    /// the documented slot) is an additive config change, not a silent
1927    /// behavior change.
1928    pub sink: AnalyticsStreamSink,
1929    /// Capacity of the bounded record channel (default 8192; validated
1930    /// to [64, 65536]). The queue's entire memory story: every queued
1931    /// record is an owned copy, and a full queue DROPS (and counts)
1932    /// rather than blocking the request path. Startup wiring: the
1933    /// capacity is fixed when the gateway boots (a live reload changes
1934    /// the sink and the cadence, not this).
1935    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1936    pub buffer: Option<u64>,
1937    /// Maximum batch latency in milliseconds (default 1000; validated
1938    /// to [100, 60000]): a batch is flushed when it holds
1939    /// `batch_max` records, reaches the batch byte cap, or this much
1940    /// time has passed since its first record — whichever comes first.
1941    /// Read live per flush cycle.
1942    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1943    pub flush_ms: Option<u64>,
1944    /// Maximum records per flushed batch (default 512; validated to
1945    /// [1, 4096]). With the batch byte cap, whichever comes first. One
1946    /// batch is one delivery, so this is also the largest single
1947    /// delivery's record count.
1948    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
1949    pub batch_max: Option<u64>,
1950}
1951
1952/// The access-record stream's sink (DW-121,
1953/// `gateway.analytics_stream.sink`). Closed set, internally tagged
1954/// (`type: webhook` or `type: kafka`): `webhook` ships today; `kafka`
1955/// (SCALE-08, #187) produces NDJSON batches to a Kafka topic via a
1956/// Kafka REST Proxy (HTTP-based, no native client dependency). The
1957/// variant payloads carry their own `deny_unknown_fields`, so a
1958/// misspelled knob inside a sink is still a rejected config.
1959#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1960#[serde(tag = "type", rename_all = "lowercase")]
1961pub enum AnalyticsStreamSink {
1962    /// POST each flushed batch as one NDJSON body (one JSON object per
1963    /// line, one line per record) to the configured URL, reusing the
1964    /// DW-044 webhook delivery engine's retry/budget shape — one
1965    /// delivery (with its retries) per batch.
1966    Webhook(AnalyticsStreamWebhook),
1967    /// Produce each flushed batch as NDJSON messages to a Kafka topic
1968    /// via a Kafka REST Proxy (SCALE-08, #187). The REST Proxy
1969    /// endpoint receives the batch as an HTTP POST with the
1970    /// `Content-Type: application/vnd.kafka.binary.v2+json` media
1971    /// type; each NDJSON line becomes a Kafka message value (base64
1972    /// encoded per the REST Proxy spec). TLS is via `https://`; SASL
1973    /// auth is via headers. This avoids the native `rdkafka`
1974    /// dependency while providing a production-usable Kafka
1975    /// integration.
1976    Kafka(AnalyticsStreamKafka),
1977}
1978
1979/// The webhook batch sink (DW-121,
1980/// `gateway.analytics_stream.sink.webhook`). Same URL/header/retry
1981/// grammar as `gateway.webhooks[]` (DW-044) — the delivery engine is
1982/// shared — minus the `events` filter (a record stream has no kinds to
1983/// filter: every record goes to the sink).
1984#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
1985#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
1986pub struct AnalyticsStreamWebhook {
1987    /// Absolute `http://` or `https://` URL the batch is POSTed to.
1988    /// `https://` verifies against the public webpki root set (no
1989    /// `trusted_ca_file` override — v1 scope, same as alert webhooks).
1990    pub url: String,
1991    /// Headers sent on every batch delivery (e.g. the collector's auth
1992    /// token). Values may be inline or `${ENV_NAME}` / `${file:/path}`
1993    /// secret references (DW-045), resolved at config-compile time;
1994    /// inline values are redacted in every config echo. Use a
1995    /// reference for bearer tokens and signing secrets.
1996    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::collections::BTreeMap::is_empty")]
1997    pub headers: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, String>,
1998    /// TOTAL budget for one batch delivery (all retry attempts share
1999    /// it), in milliseconds. Default 5000: a BATCH is heavier than a
2000    /// single alert envelope, and the flusher delivers batches strictly
2001    /// in order, so this bounds how long one unlucky batch can hold
2002    /// back the queue. Validation enforces
2003    /// 1..=[`limits::MAX_WEBHOOK_TIMEOUT_MS`] (the shared engine's
2004    /// bound).
2005    #[serde(
2006        default = "default_stream_webhook_timeout_ms",
2007        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_stream_webhook_timeout_ms"
2008    )]
2009    pub timeout_ms: u64,
2010    /// Total attempts per batch delivery (first try plus retries);
2011    /// retries cover transport failures and the transient status set
2012    /// (429/502/503/504), honoring a seconds-form `Retry-After`.
2013    /// Default 3; validation enforces
2014    /// 1..=[`limits::MAX_WEBHOOK_ATTEMPTS`].
2015    #[serde(
2016        default = "default_stream_webhook_attempts",
2017        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_stream_webhook_attempts"
2018    )]
2019    pub max_attempts: u32,
2020    /// First backoff between batch attempts, doubling per retry up to
2021    /// `backoff_cap_ms` (a `Retry-After` answer replaces the computed
2022    /// value for that wait). Default 250 — a batch retry is heavier
2023    /// than an alert retry, so it starts slower.
2024    #[serde(
2025        default = "default_stream_webhook_backoff_base_ms",
2026        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_stream_webhook_backoff_base_ms"
2027    )]
2028    pub backoff_base_ms: u64,
2029    /// Upper bound on the computed backoff. Default 4000; must be >=
2030    /// `backoff_base_ms`.
2031    #[serde(
2032        default = "default_stream_webhook_backoff_cap_ms",
2033        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_stream_webhook_backoff_cap_ms"
2034    )]
2035    pub backoff_cap_ms: u64,
2036}
2037
2038/// The Kafka batch sink (SCALE-08, #187,
2039/// `gateway.analytics_stream.sink.kafka`). Produces each flushed
2040/// batch as NDJSON messages to a Kafka topic via a Kafka REST Proxy
2041/// (HTTP-based, no native client dependency). The REST Proxy
2042/// endpoint receives the batch as an HTTP POST with the
2043/// `Content-Type: application/vnd.kafka.binary.v2+json` media type;
2044/// each NDJSON line becomes a Kafka message value (base64 encoded
2045/// per the REST Proxy spec). TLS is via `https://`; SASL auth is via
2046/// headers (e.g. `Authorization: Basic ...`).
2047#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2048#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
2049pub struct AnalyticsStreamKafka {
2050    /// Absolute `http://` or `https://` URL of the Kafka REST Proxy
2051    /// endpoint for producing to the topic, e.g.
2052    /// `https://kafka-rest:8082/topics/<topic>`. `https://` verifies
2053    /// against the public webpki root set.
2054    pub rest_proxy_url: String,
2055    /// Headers sent on every batch delivery (e.g. the REST Proxy's
2056    /// auth token, `Authorization: Basic ...` for SASL). Values may
2057    /// be inline or `${ENV_NAME}` / `${file:/path}` secret references
2058    /// (DW-045), resolved at config-compile time; inline values are
2059    /// redacted in every config echo.
2060    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::collections::BTreeMap::is_empty")]
2061    pub headers: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, String>,
2062    /// TOTAL budget for one batch delivery (all retry attempts share
2063    /// it), in milliseconds. Default 5000. Validation enforces
2064    /// 1..=[`limits::MAX_WEBHOOK_TIMEOUT_MS`] (the shared engine's
2065    /// bound).
2066    #[serde(
2067        default = "default_stream_webhook_timeout_ms",
2068        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_stream_webhook_timeout_ms"
2069    )]
2070    pub timeout_ms: u64,
2071    /// Max delivery attempts per batch. Default 3.
2072    #[serde(
2073        default = "default_stream_webhook_attempts",
2074        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_stream_webhook_attempts"
2075    )]
2076    pub max_attempts: u32,
2077    /// First backoff between batch attempts, doubling per retry up to
2078    /// `backoff_cap_ms`. Default 250.
2079    #[serde(
2080        default = "default_stream_webhook_backoff_base_ms",
2081        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_stream_webhook_backoff_base_ms"
2082    )]
2083    pub backoff_base_ms: u64,
2084    /// Upper bound on the computed backoff. Default 4000; must be >=
2085    /// `backoff_base_ms`.
2086    #[serde(
2087        default = "default_stream_webhook_backoff_cap_ms",
2088        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_stream_webhook_backoff_cap_ms"
2089    )]
2090    pub backoff_cap_ms: u64,
2091}

2093/// Default `analytics_stream.buffer` (DW-121): the bounded record
2094/// channel's capacity when the block is absent at boot (the stream is
2095/// always constructed so a live reload can arm it). Lives in `config`
2096/// with the retention defaults — the lowest consuming domain — so
2097/// validation docs, the stream, and the binary read one definition.
2098pub const DEFAULT_STREAM_BUFFER: u64 = 8_192;
2099/// Default `analytics_stream.flush_ms` (DW-121): maximum batch latency.
2100pub const DEFAULT_STREAM_FLUSH_MS: u64 = 1_000;
2101/// Default `analytics_stream.batch_max` (DW-121): records per flushed
2102/// batch.
2103pub const DEFAULT_STREAM_BATCH_MAX: u64 = 512;
2104/// Default `analytics_stream.sink.webhook.timeout_ms` (DW-121): a
2105/// batch carries up to `batch_max` records, so its budget starts
2106/// heavier than the alert webhook's 2000 but stays far under the
2107/// one-minute ceiling.
2108pub const DEFAULT_STREAM_WEBHOOK_TIMEOUT_MS: u64 = 5_000;
2109/// Default `analytics_stream.sink.webhook.max_attempts` (DW-121).
2110pub const DEFAULT_STREAM_WEBHOOK_ATTEMPTS: u32 = 3;
2111/// Default `analytics_stream.sink.webhook.backoff_base_ms` (DW-121).
2112pub const DEFAULT_STREAM_WEBHOOK_BACKOFF_BASE_MS: u64 = 250;
2113/// Default `analytics_stream.sink.webhook.backoff_cap_ms` (DW-121).
2114pub const DEFAULT_STREAM_WEBHOOK_BACKOFF_CAP_MS: u64 = 4_000;
2115
2116fn default_stream_webhook_timeout_ms() -> u64 {
2117    DEFAULT_STREAM_WEBHOOK_TIMEOUT_MS
2118}
2119fn default_stream_webhook_attempts() -> u32 {
2120    DEFAULT_STREAM_WEBHOOK_ATTEMPTS
2121}
2122fn default_stream_webhook_backoff_base_ms() -> u64 {
2123    DEFAULT_STREAM_WEBHOOK_BACKOFF_BASE_MS
2124}
2125fn default_stream_webhook_backoff_cap_ms() -> u64 {
2126    DEFAULT_STREAM_WEBHOOK_BACKOFF_CAP_MS
2127}
2128fn is_default_stream_webhook_timeout_ms(v: &u64) -> bool {
2129    *v == DEFAULT_STREAM_WEBHOOK_TIMEOUT_MS
2130}
2131fn is_default_stream_webhook_attempts(v: &u32) -> bool {
2132    *v == DEFAULT_STREAM_WEBHOOK_ATTEMPTS
2133}
2134fn is_default_stream_webhook_backoff_base_ms(v: &u64) -> bool {
2135    *v == DEFAULT_STREAM_WEBHOOK_BACKOFF_BASE_MS
2136}
2137fn is_default_stream_webhook_backoff_cap_ms(v: &u64) -> bool {
2138    *v == DEFAULT_STREAM_WEBHOOK_BACKOFF_CAP_MS
2139}

2141/// Gateway-wide HMAC request-signing policy (DW-036). The credential
2142/// itself (key id + secret) lives on a consumer
2143/// (`credentials: [{type: hmac, ...}]`); this block carries the
2144/// VERIFICATION policy that applies to every signed request.
2145#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2146#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
2147pub struct HmacAuth {
2148    /// Maximum accepted absolute difference, in seconds, between the
2149    /// request's `X-Dwara-Timestamp` and the gateway clock. A timestamp
2150    /// outside the window is rejected 401 BEFORE any HMAC computation
2151    /// (an expired window is not a MAC problem). The window is also the
2152    /// replay window: nonces are remembered for twice this duration.
2153    /// Default 300 (±5 minutes, the §4.6 recommendation); validation
2154    /// enforces 1..=3600 (a sub-second window rejects every request
2155    /// with any clock drift; an unbounded one pins nonce-cache memory
2156    /// forever).
2157    #[serde(default = "default_hmac_clock_skew_secs")]
2158    pub max_clock_skew_secs: u64,
2159}
2160
2161/// Default `hmac_auth.max_clock_skew_secs` (±5 minutes, DW-036 §4.6).
2162pub const DEFAULT_HMAC_CLOCK_SKEW_SECS: u64 = 300;
2163
2164fn default_hmac_clock_skew_secs() -> u64 {
2165    DEFAULT_HMAC_CLOCK_SKEW_SECS
2166}
2167
2168impl Gateway {
2169    /// A display-safe copy of this config (DW-045): every INLINE
2170    /// `api_key` value is replaced by the
2171    /// [`credentials::redact_inline_secret`] placeholder
2172    /// (`${redacted:sha256:<prefix>}`); `${...}` references pass through
2173    /// unchanged (an env-var name or file path is not secret bytes —
2174    /// the config file itself carries it). This is the TYPED redaction
2175    /// behind every surface that echoes configuration (admin
2176    /// `GET /config`); it is deliberately a transform, not a custom
2177    /// serializer, so it cannot drift from the schema — a future
2178    /// secret-bearing field fails visibly (its value reaches the dump
2179    /// unredacted) instead of silently escaping an allowlist regex.
2180    pub fn redacted(&self) -> Gateway {
2181        let mut redacted = self.clone();
2182        for consumer in &mut redacted.consumers {
2183            for credential in &mut consumer.credentials {
2184                match credential {
2185                    Credential::ApiKey { key } => {
2186                        *key = credentials::redact_inline_secret(key);
2187                    }
2188                    // DW-036: the HMAC secret is inline secret material
2189                    // exactly like an api key — same placeholder, same
2190                    // unresolvable-by-design round-trip rejection.
2191                    Credential::Hmac { secret, .. } => {
2192                        *secret = credentials::redact_inline_secret(secret);
2193                    }
2194                    Credential::Jwt { .. } | Credential::Mtls { .. } => {}
2195                }
2196            }
2197        }
2198        // DW-044: webhook header values are secret-bearing config fields
2199        // exactly like api keys (bearer tokens, signing secrets) — the
2200        // same placeholder, the same reference passthrough, the same
2201        // unresolvable-by-design round-trip rejection.
2202        for webhook in &mut redacted.webhooks {
2203            for value in webhook.headers.values_mut() {
2204                *value = credentials::redact_inline_secret(value);
2205            }
2206        }
2207        // DW-075: AI provider auth values are inline secret material
2208        // exactly like api keys — the same placeholder, the same
2209        // reference passthrough.
2210        if let Some(ai) = &mut redacted.ai {
2211            for provider in &mut ai.providers {
2212                if let Some(auth) = &mut provider.auth {
2213                    auth.value = credentials::redact_inline_secret(&auth.value);
2214                }
2215                // DW-080: redact credential pool entry values too.
2216                if let Some(pool) = &mut provider.credential_pool {
2217                    for cred in &mut pool.credentials {
2218                        cred.value = credentials::redact_inline_secret(&cred.value);
2219                    }
2220                }
2221            }
2222        }
2223        redacted
2224    }
2225}

2227/// Admin listener configuration (DW-022).
2228#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2229#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
2230pub struct AdminConfig {
2231    /// Bind address of the admin listener. Default `127.0.0.1:2019`
2232    /// (loopback-only; frozen decision 6) — override only to place the
2233    /// admin API on a dedicated management interface, and rely on mTLS
2234    /// for access control: there is no token layer in v1 (the client
2235    /// certificate IS the auth).
2236    #[serde(default = "default_admin_bind")]
2237    pub bind: String,
2238    /// mTLS material for the admin listener; all three files are
2239    /// required.
2240    pub tls: AdminTlsConfig,
2241    /// SEC-01: RBAC bindings for mTLS-authenticated admin clients.
2242    /// Maps client certificate fingerprints (SHA-256 of the cert
2243    /// DER) to roles. When present, a valid client certificate with
2244    /// NO binding is denied (fail-closed: no implicit admin). When
2245    /// absent (the default), any CA-valid client certificate is
2246    /// treated as full admin (the v1 behavior — backward compatible).
2247    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2248    pub rbac: Option<AdminRbacConfig>,
2249    /// SEC-01: API token authentication. When present, the admin API
2250    /// accepts an `Authorization: Bearer <token>` header in addition
2251    /// to mTLS. Tokens are hashed (SHA-256) at rest; the config lists
2252    /// token hashes, not plaintext tokens. When absent (the default),
2253    /// only mTLS is accepted (the v1 behavior).
2254    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2255    pub api_tokens: Option<AdminApiTokensConfig>,
2256    /// SEC-01: Admin audit log. When present, all mutating admin
2257    /// actions (PATCH /config, purge) are recorded in an append-only
2258    /// audit table in the state store. Each entry includes the actor
2259    /// (cert fingerprint or token hash), the action, the before/after
2260    /// config hash, and a timestamp. When absent (the default), no
2261    /// audit log is kept.
2262    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2263    pub audit: Option<AdminAuditConfig>,
2264}
2265
2266/// SEC-01: Admin RBAC configuration. Maps client certificate
2267/// fingerprints to roles. A valid certificate with no binding is
2268/// denied (fail-closed).
2269#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2270#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
2271pub struct AdminRbacConfig {
2272    /// Certificate-fingerprint-to-role bindings. Each entry maps one
2273    /// SHA-256 fingerprint (lowercase hex, 64 chars) to a role.
2274    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
2275    pub bindings: Vec<AdminRbacBinding>,
2276}
2277
2278/// One certificate-fingerprint-to-role binding.
2279#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2280#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
2281pub struct AdminRbacBinding {
2282    /// SHA-256 fingerprint of the client certificate (lowercase hex,
2283    /// 64 chars). Computed over the full DER encoding of the cert.
2284    pub cert_fingerprint: String,
2285    /// The role assigned to this certificate: `admin` (full access)
2286    /// or `readonly` (GET only; PATCH/purge denied).
2287    pub role: AdminRole,
2288}
2289
2290/// Admin roles.
2291#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema, Default)]
2292#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
2293pub enum AdminRole {
2294    /// Full admin access: all admin API endpoints.
2295    #[default]
2296    Admin,
2297    /// Read-only access: GET endpoints only. PATCH /config and purge
2298    /// are denied (403).
2299    Readonly,
2300}
2301
2302/// SEC-01: API token authentication for the admin API. Tokens are
2303/// hashed (SHA-256) at rest; the config lists token hashes, not
2304/// plaintext tokens. A client presents a token via
2305/// `Authorization: Bearer <token>`.
2306#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2307#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
2308pub struct AdminApiTokensConfig {
2309    /// The hashed API tokens (SHA-256 of the plaintext token, as
2310    /// lowercase hex, 64 chars). A request with a matching bearer
2311    /// token is authenticated as the token's role.
2312    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
2313    pub tokens: Vec<AdminApiToken>,
2314}
2315
2316/// One hashed API token with a role.
2317#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2318#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
2319pub struct AdminApiToken {
2320    /// SHA-256 hash of the plaintext token (lowercase hex, 64 chars).
2321    pub token_hash: String,
2322    /// The role assigned to this token.
2323    pub role: AdminRole,
2324}

2326/// SEC-01: Admin audit log configuration.
2327#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2328#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
2329pub struct AdminAuditConfig {
2330    /// Whether the audit log is enabled. Default true (when the block
2331    /// is present, the audit log is on).
2332    #[serde(
2333        default = "default_audit_enabled",
2334        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_audit_enabled"
2335    )]
2336    pub enabled: bool,
2337}
2338
2339fn default_audit_enabled() -> bool {
2340    true
2341}

2343fn is_default_audit_enabled(b: &bool) -> bool {
2344    *b
2345}
