2347/// mTLS material for the admin listener (DW-022). Unlike dataplane
2348/// listeners there is no mode: the admin listener always terminates TLS
2349/// and always requires a client certificate chaining to `client_ca_file`.
2350#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2351#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
2352pub struct AdminTlsConfig {
2353    /// Path to the PEM certificate chain the admin server presents.
2354    pub cert_file: String,
2355    /// Path to the PEM private key for `cert_file`.
2356    pub key_file: String,
2357    /// Path to the PEM CA bundle client certificates must chain to.
2358    /// Required (mTLS-only): validation rejects an admin block without
2359    /// it rather than silently serving no-auth TLS.
2360    pub client_ca_file: String,
2361}
2362
2363fn default_admin_bind() -> String {
2364    "127.0.0.1:2019".to_string()
2365}
2366
2367/// One JWT verification provider (DW-019).
2368#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2369#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
2370pub struct JwtProvider {
2371    pub name: String,
2372    /// JWKS endpoint (`http://` or `https://`). Keys are fetched lazily on
2373    /// the first Bearer request, refreshed after `refresh_secs`, and
2374    /// re-fetched on an unknown `kid` (key rotation mid-flight).
2375    pub jwks_url: String,
2376    /// Path to a PEM file of CA certificates the JWKS fetcher trusts for
2377    /// its `https://` connection INSTEAD of the default public (webpki)
2378    /// root set (#121): for JWKS endpoints served over TLS by a private
2379    /// CA. The file may carry several certificates (a typical CA bundle).
2380    /// Only meaningful with an `https://` jwks_url — no TLS is negotiated
2381    /// toward an `http://` endpoint, so validation rejects that
2382    /// combination. The file must exist and be readable at config compile
2383    /// time; validation names this field otherwise.
2384    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2385    pub trusted_ca_file: Option<String>,
2386    /// Required token issuer (`iss` claim). Absent: any issuer accepted.
2387    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2388    pub issuer: Option<String>,
2389    /// Required audience (`aud` claim). Audience is validated ONLY when
2390    /// this is configured (#124, maintainer decision): a provider
2391    /// without an `audience` ACCEPTS tokens that carry any (or no) `aud`
2392    /// claim — `aud` is not interpreted unless the provider names one.
2393    /// All other validations (exp, nbf, iss when configured, the
2394    /// algorithm allowlist) are identical either way.
2395    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2396    pub audience: Option<String>,
2397    /// Allowed signature algorithms (default `["RS256", "ES256"]`).
2398    /// `none` and HMAC (`HS*`) algorithms are never allowed implicitly —
2399    /// they must not appear in this list; validation rejects them
2400    /// (asymmetric verification only: the gateway holds no shared
2401    /// secrets with issuers).
2402    #[serde(default = "default_jwt_algorithms")]
2403    pub algorithms: Vec<String>,
2404    /// JWKS cache staleness bound in seconds (default 300): a cached key
2405    /// set older than this is refreshed before use.
2406    #[serde(
2407        default = "default_jwt_refresh_secs",
2408        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_jwt_refresh_secs"
2409    )]
2410    pub refresh_secs: u64,
2411    /// exp/nbf clock-skew leeway in seconds (default 30).
2412    #[serde(
2413        default = "default_jwt_leeway_secs",
2414        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_jwt_leeway_secs"
2415    )]
2416    pub leeway_secs: u64,
2417    /// Consumer this provider's tokens authenticate. Absent: the token's
2418    /// `iss` claim is matched against consumers' `jwt` credentials.
2419    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2420    pub consumer: Option<String>,
2421    /// How long a key REMOVED from a fresh JWKS fetch keeps verifying
2422    /// (DW-046), in seconds (default 86400 = 24 h; 0 disables). The
2423    /// dual-validity window for JWKS rotation: issuers drop the old
2424    /// key from their JWKS the moment the new one appears, while
2425    /// previously-issued tokens still carry the old `kid` — during
2426    /// this grace the gateway honors the immediately-previous key set
2427    /// for kids the fresh set no longer carries. Only ONE previous set
2428    /// is retained (rotation is one step at a time). Validation caps
2429    /// the value at 7 days (604800).
2430    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2431    pub retired_key_grace_secs: Option<u64>,
2432    /// How long to serve stale (cached) keys after a JWKS fetch failure
2433    /// (SEC-12, #215), in seconds (default: same as `refresh_secs`,
2434    /// capped at 300). When a JWKS fetch fails, the cached key set
2435    /// keeps serving for this duration before a new fetch is attempted,
2436    /// so a transient JWKS provider outage does not cascade into auth
2437    /// failures. 0 disables stale-on-error (a fetch failure surfaces
2438    /// immediately when the cache is empty; when the cache is non-empty
2439    /// the existing degraded-mode behavior applies).
2440    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2441    pub stale_on_error_secs: Option<u64>,
2442}
2443
2444impl JwtProvider {
2445    /// `retired_key_grace_secs` resolved to its effective value
2446    /// (default 86 400; the DW-046 dual-validity window).
2447    pub fn retired_key_grace_secs(&self) -> u64 {
2448        self.retired_key_grace_secs.unwrap_or(86_400)
2449    }
2450    /// `stale_on_error_secs` resolved to its effective value (SEC-12,
2451    /// #215): the default is `min(refresh_secs, 300)` — a failed fetch
2452    /// backs off for at most one refresh interval (capped at 5 min) so
2453    /// a down JWKS endpoint does not chain every Bearer request through
2454    /// a doomed fetch attempt.
2455    pub fn stale_on_error_secs(&self) -> u64 {
2456        self.stale_on_error_secs
2457            .unwrap_or_else(|| self.refresh_secs.min(300))
2458    }
2459}
2460
2461fn default_jwt_algorithms() -> Vec<String> {
2462    vec!["RS256".to_string(), "ES256".to_string()]
2463}
2464
2465fn default_jwt_refresh_secs() -> u64 {
2466    300
2467}
2468
2469fn default_jwt_leeway_secs() -> u64 {
2470    30
2471}
2472
2473fn is_default_jwt_refresh_secs(v: &u64) -> bool {
2474    *v == 300
2475}
2476
2477fn is_default_jwt_leeway_secs(v: &u64) -> bool {
2478    *v == 30
2479}
2480
2481/// One OIDC provider (DW-034): an OpenID Connect issuer whose Bearer
2482/// tokens are validated by token introspection (RFC 7662) rather than
2483/// local JWT signature verification. The gateway fetches the issuer's
2484/// discovery document (`{issuer}/.well-known/openid-configuration`) to
2485/// learn the introspection, revocation, authorization, and token
2486/// endpoints, then introspects presented Bearer tokens (caching the
2487/// result keyed by token hash with a configurable TTL). The provider
2488/// also backs the authorization-code + PKCE relying-party flow, token
2489/// exchange (RFC 8693), and token revocation (RFC 7009).
2490#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2491#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
2492pub struct OidcProvider {
2493    pub name: String,
2494    /// The OIDC issuer URL (e.g. `https://idp.example.com`). The
2495    /// discovery document is fetched from
2496    /// `{issuer}/.well-known/openid-configuration`. Must be an absolute
2497    /// `http(s)://` URL; validation rejects anything else. The
2498    /// introspection result's `iss` (when present) is checked against
2499    /// this issuer for defense against token confusion.
2500    pub issuer: String,
2501    /// The gateway's client identifier at the IdP (RFC 6749 section
2502    /// 2.2). Sent as the username of the HTTP Basic auth header to the
2503    /// introspection and token endpoints, and as `client_id` in the
2504    /// authorization-code and token-exchange request bodies.
2505    pub client_id: String,
2506    /// The gateway's client secret (RFC 6749 section 2.3.1). Either the
2507    /// value INLINE (redacted in every config echo, DW-045) or a
2508    /// `${...}` secret reference (env or file) resolved at config-compile
2509    /// time and re-read on every reload. Sent as the password of the
2510    /// HTTP Basic auth header. SECRET: never logged, never in Debug.
2511    pub client_secret: String,
2512    /// Path to a PEM file of CA certificates the OIDC HTTP client
2513    /// trusts for its `https://` connections INSTEAD of the default
2514    /// public (webpki) root set: for an IdP served over TLS by a private
2515    /// CA. The file may carry several certificates (a typical CA bundle).
2516    /// Only meaningful with an `https://` issuer; validation rejects the
2517    /// combination with an `http://` issuer. The file must exist and be
2518    /// readable at config compile time.
2519    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2520    pub trusted_ca_file: Option<String>,
2521    /// Scopes to request (default `["openid"]`). Sent in the
2522    /// authorization-code flow's authorization request and the
2523    /// token-exchange request body (space-joined).
2524    #[serde(
2525        default = "default_oidc_scopes",
2526        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_oidc_scopes"
2527    )]
2528    pub scopes: Vec<String>,
2529    /// Cache TTL for introspection results, in seconds (default 60). A
2530    /// cached `active: true` result short-circuits the IdP call for this
2531    /// duration; a cached `active: false` result is NOT cached (it is
2532    /// re-checked on the next request so a revoked token is noticed
2533    /// promptly). Must be > 0; validation rejects 0.
2534    #[serde(
2535        default = "default_oidc_introspection_cache_ttl_s",
2536        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_oidc_introspection_cache_ttl_s"
2537    )]
2538    pub introspection_cache_ttl_s: u64,
2539    /// Override the discovery-discovered introspection endpoint. Absent
2540    /// (the default): the endpoint is taken from the discovery document.
2541    /// Useful when the IdP's discovery document is incorrect or the
2542    /// endpoint is behind a different host.
2543    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2544    pub introspection_endpoint: Option<String>,
2545    /// Override the discovery-discovered revocation endpoint. Absent
2546    /// (the default): the endpoint is taken from the discovery document.
2547    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2548    pub revocation_endpoint: Option<String>,
2549    /// Consumer this provider's tokens authenticate (like
2550    /// [`JwtProvider::consumer`]). Absent: the introspection result's
2551    /// `sub` claim is used as the consumer name. When set, every
2552    /// successfully introspected token resolves to this consumer
2553    /// regardless of the `sub` claim.
2554    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2555    pub consumer: Option<String>,
2556    /// Fail-open posture when the IdP is unreachable (network error,
2557    /// non-2xx, malformed response). Default false (fail closed = 401):
2558    /// the gateway refuses to authenticate a token it cannot introspect.
2559    /// When true, the gateway treats an IdP failure as anonymous
2560    /// (pass-through) rather than rejecting the request. Fail-open
2561    /// trades security for availability; use with care.
2562    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
2563    pub fail_open: bool,
2564}
2565
2566fn default_oidc_scopes() -> Vec<String> {
2567    vec!["openid".to_string()]
2568}
2569
2570fn is_default_oidc_scopes(v: &[String]) -> bool {
2571    v.len() == 1 && v[0] == "openid"
2572}
2573
2574fn default_oidc_introspection_cache_ttl_s() -> u64 {
2575    60
2576}
2577
2578fn is_default_oidc_introspection_cache_ttl_s(v: &u64) -> bool {
2579    *v == 60
2580}
2581
2582/// Entry point: bind address + port + TLS termination (or passthrough) config.
2583#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2584#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
2585pub struct Listener {
2586    pub name: String,
2587    /// Bind address, e.g. `0.0.0.0` or `127.0.0.1`.
2588    pub address: String,
2589    pub port: u16,
2590    #[serde(default = "default_listener_protocol")]
2591    pub protocol: ListenerProtocol,
2592    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2593    pub tls: Option<ListenerTls>,
2594    /// Accept a PROXY protocol v1 or v2 header as the FIRST bytes of
2595    /// every connection on this listener (DW-030), replacing the peer
2596    /// address the gateway uses everywhere the socket peer is used
2597    /// today: the authz IP ACL's effective-client-IP base, rate-limit
2598    /// keying, `X-Forwarded-For` / `X-Real-IP` on the forwarded
2599    /// request. OPT-IN (default false): a plaintext listener that does
2600    /// not expect a PROXY line must never interpret the first request
2601    /// bytes as one, and a client that spoofs a PROXY line on a listener
2602    /// without this flag is simply serving garbage to the HTTP parser.
2603    /// The header is read BEFORE the TLS handshake (the L4 LB in front
2604    /// wraps the whole stream, TLS included) and a MALFORMED header
2605    /// fails closed: the connection is answered with a `400` error
2606    /// envelope and closed, never handed to HTTP parsing. A v2 `LOCAL`
2607    /// command or a v1 `UNKNOWN` line keeps the real peer address (the
2608    /// spec's own fallback for connections the LB did not originate).
2609    /// Validation rejects the combination with `tls.mode passthrough`:
2610    /// a passthrough listener splices raw bytes and never runs the
2611    /// HTTP pipeline that consumes the client address. Part of the
2612    /// restart-only bind set (like address/port) — toggling it takes a
2613    /// restart, not a reload.
2614    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
2615    pub proxy_protocol: bool,
2616    /// Policies attached to this listener (#123): names from the
2617    /// gateway's `policies` list, applying to every request the listener
2618    /// accepts (the second-least specific link of the frozen chain
2619    /// consumer > route > service > listener > global; all applicable
2620    /// levels' rules AND together — see the rate-limiter module docs).
2621    /// Listener policies also apply to UNROUTED traffic accepted by this
2622    /// listener: a request whose path matches no route is rate-limited by
2623    /// them before the 404 is answered (reserved paths stay exempt).
2624    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
2625    pub policies: Vec<String>,
2626    /// Listener-level authorization rules (#123): applies to every
2627    /// request this listener accepts that resolved a route (authorization
2628    /// runs after route resolution per the documented request-path
2629    /// order). Link of the frozen chain consumer > route > service >
2630    /// listener > global; see [`Authz`] for the rule semantics and the
2631    /// `authz` module for the merge.
2632    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2633    pub authorization: Option<Authz>,
2634    /// Alt-Svc header value advertised on H1/H2 responses for this
2635    /// listener (DW-088). When set, an `Alt-Svc` response header is
2636    /// added telling clients that HTTP/3 is available. The value is
2637    /// the alt-svc payload (e.g. `h3=":8443"; ma=86400`). Only
2638    /// meaningful on `http` and `https` listeners (an H3 listener
2639    /// advertising itself is a no-op and rejected by validation).
2640    /// The header is added after all other response processing (it
2641    /// is a transport-level advertisement, not a route-level
2642    /// transform).
2643    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2644    pub alt_svc: Option<String>,
2645    /// L4 proxying configuration (DW-103). Only meaningful on
2646    /// `protocol: tcp` and `protocol: udp` listeners; rejected by
2647    /// validation on other protocols. The `upstream` field names the
2648    /// upstream that receives the spliced stream (or, when
2649    /// `sni_routing` is true, the upstream selected by the TLS
2650    /// ClientHello SNI via the upstream's `tls.sni_routes` -- reusing
2651    /// the SNI extraction from DW-008 TLS passthrough). Compiled into
2652    /// every build (no cargo feature).
2653    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2654    pub l4: Option<L4Config>,
2655    /// HTTP/2 flow-control tuning for this listener (PERF-11, #244).
2656    /// When set, the h2 server builder uses these values instead of
2657    /// the process-wide defaults from `DWARA_H2_*` env vars. Only
2658    /// meaningful on `http` and `https` listeners (h2 is negotiated
2659    /// via ALPN or prior knowledge); ignored on `h3` (HTTP/3 has its
2660    /// own transport-level flow control) and `tcp`/`udp`.
2661    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2662    pub http2: Option<Http2ListenerConfig>,
2663}
2664
2665/// HTTP/2 flow-control tuning for a listener (PERF-11, #244,
2666/// `listeners[].http2`). Every field is optional; an omitted field
2667/// keeps the process-wide default from `DWARA_H2_*` env vars. This
2668/// allows per-listener tuning for streaming (SSE) workloads where
2669/// larger windows reduce flow-control stalls on long-lived streams.
2670#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2671#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
2672pub struct Http2ListenerConfig {
2673    /// Initial stream-level window size in bytes (hyper
2674    /// `initial_stream_window_size`). Absent: process default
2675    /// (1 MiB via `DWARA_H2_STREAM_WINDOW_KIB`). Larger values
2676    /// reduce stalls on streaming responses but increase memory
2677    /// per stream. Bounds: at most 16 MiB.
2678    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2679    pub initial_stream_window_size: Option<u32>,
2680    /// Initial connection-level window size in bytes (hyper
2681    /// `initial_connection_window_size`). Absent: process default
2682    /// (4 MiB via `DWARA_H2_CONNECTION_WINDOW_KIB`). Bounds: at
2683    /// most 64 MiB.
2684    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2685    pub initial_connection_window_size: Option<u32>,
2686    /// Enable adaptive flow-control window (hyper
2687    /// `adaptive_window`). Absent: false. When true, the
2688    /// connection window grows and shrinks based on throughput,
2689    /// which helps streaming workloads on high-BDP paths.
2690    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
2691    pub adaptive_window: bool,
2692    /// Maximum number of concurrent streams per connection (hyper
2693    /// `max_concurrent_streams`). Absent: process default (128 via
2694    /// `DWARA_H2_MAX_CONCURRENT_STREAMS`). Bounds: at most 1_000_000.
2695    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2696    pub max_concurrent_streams: Option<u32>,
2697}
2698
2699fn default_listener_protocol() -> ListenerProtocol {
2700    ListenerProtocol::Http
2701}
2702
2703#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2704#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
2705pub enum ListenerProtocol {
2706    Http,
2707    Https,
2708    /// HTTP/3 (QUIC) ingress (DW-088). A UDP socket is bound on the
2709    /// listener's address:port; the QUIC handshake uses the `tls`
2710    /// block's certificates (terminate mode only — passthrough is
2711    /// rejected). Compiled into every build (no cargo feature; QUIC
2712    /// via the unconditional `quinn`/`h3` deps). The `alt_svc` field on
2713    /// sibling H1/H2 listeners advertises this listener's H3 port to
2714    /// clients for protocol upgrade discovery.
2715    H3,
2716    /// L4 TCP proxying (DW-103). A raw TCP socket is bound on the
2717    /// listener's address:port; accepted connections are spliced
2718    /// byte-for-byte to an upstream endpoint (optionally selected by
2719    /// peeking the TLS ClientHello SNI, reusing the SNI extraction
2720    /// from DW-008 TLS passthrough). Compiled into every build (no
2721    /// cargo feature). The `l4` block configures the
2722    /// upstream and SNI-routing flag.
2723    Tcp,
2724    /// L4 UDP proxying (DW-103). A UDP socket is bound on the
2725    /// listener's address:port; datagrams are forwarded to an upstream
2726    /// endpoint. STUBBED: the UDP dispatcher returns Unimplemented --
2727    /// UDP session semantics and NAT timeouts are a follow-up. Compiled
2728    /// into every build (no cargo feature). The `l4` block configures
2729    /// the upstream.
2730    Udp,
2731}
2732
2733/// TLS handling for a listener: terminate at the edge or pass through.
2734#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2735#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
2736pub struct ListenerTls {
2737    #[serde(default = "default_tls_mode")]
2738    pub mode: TlsMode,
2739    /// Path to the PEM certificate chain (termination mode only). Serves as
2740    /// the default/fallback certificate when `certificates` is also set.
2741    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2742    pub cert_file: Option<String>,
2743    /// Path to the PEM private key (termination mode only).
2744    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2745    pub key_file: Option<String>,
2746    /// Additional SNI-scoped certificate pairs (termination mode only).
2747    /// Each entry serves the listed `server_names`; the single
2748    /// cert_file/key_file pair (if present) is the fallback for unmatched
2749    /// or absent SNI. With no single pair, the first entry is the fallback.
2750    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
2751    pub certificates: Vec<TlsCertificate>,
2752    /// SNI routing rules for passthrough mode: each entry maps its
2753    /// `server_names` to an upstream (by name) that receives the raw TLS
2754    /// bytes. Rejected in terminate mode.
2755    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
2756    pub sni_routes: Vec<SniRoute>,
2757    /// PEM file of CA certificates that CLIENT certificates must chain
2758    /// to (#124, termination mode only). When set, the listener
2759    /// REQUESTS a client certificate and verifies any presented one
2760    /// against this bundle; a connection without a certificate is still
2761    /// accepted (other credential families apply) — the verified
2762    /// certificate is matched against consumers' `mtls` credentials to
2763    /// resolve an identity, and an UNVERIFIED certificate fails the
2764    /// handshake (mTLS authn never sees it). Rejected in passthrough
2765    /// mode (the TLS layer is not terminated, so client certificates
2766    /// cannot be verified). The file must exist and be readable at
2767    /// config compile time; validation names this field otherwise.
2768    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2769    pub client_ca_file: Option<String>,
2770    /// 0-RTT (early data) policy for HTTP/3 listeners (DW-088). When
2771    /// `accept`, the QUIC handshake accepts 0-RTT early data from
2772    /// clients with a saved session ticket — at the cost of replay
2773    /// risk. Non-idempotent requests (POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE) are
2774    /// rejected under 0-RTT with a 425 Too Early response (the client
2775    /// retries over a 1-RTT connection). When `reject` (the default),
2776    /// 0-RTT early data is refused and the client must complete a
2777    /// full 1-RTT handshake before sending a request. Only meaningful
2778    /// on `protocol: h3` listeners; ignored (and rejected by
2779    /// validation) on H1/H2 listeners.
2780    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_default_zero_rtt")]
2781    pub zero_rtt: ZeroRttPolicy,
2782    /// DW-105: opt in to post-quantum hybrid key exchange
2783    /// (X25519+ML-KEM) for this listener. When `true`, the
2784    /// X25519+ML-KEM hybrid kx group is
2785    /// prepended to the rustls provider's kx group list, PREFERRING
2786    /// the hybrid group while keeping the classical X25519 fallback
2787    /// for non-PQ clients. Compiled into every build (no cargo
2788    /// feature). EXPERIMENTAL: the rustls PQ
2789    /// API is not yet stable. Rejected when combined with FIPS mode
2790    /// (ML-KEM is not on the FIPS-validated list for aws-lc-rs).
2791    /// Terminate mode only (passthrough does not terminate TLS, so
2792    /// the kx group list is irrelevant); validation rejects `pq: true`
2793    /// on a passthrough listener.
2794    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
2795    pub pq: bool,
2796    /// SEC-02: ACME / Let's Encrypt automation. When present, the
2797    /// gateway automatically obtains and renews certificates for the
2798    /// configured domains from the ACME directory. Terminates mode
2799    /// only (passthrough does not terminate TLS). When `acme` is
2800    /// present, `cert_file`/`key_file`/`certificates` are optional
2801    /// (ACME-managed certs serve as the fallback); manual certs still
2802    /// take precedence for their SNI names. See [`AcmeConfig`].
2803    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2804    pub acme: Option<AcmeConfig>,
2805}
2806
2807/// SEC-02: ACME (Let's Encrypt) certificate automation configuration.
2808/// The gateway acts as an ACME client: orders certificates for the
2809/// configured domains, completes the challenge (HTTP-01 or TLS-ALPN-01),
2810/// and installs the issued certificates into the hot-reloadable TLS
2811/// termination/SNI resolver. Renewal runs automatically before expiry.
2812#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2813#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
2814pub struct AcmeConfig {
2815    /// The domains to obtain certificates for. At least one must be
2816    /// present (validation rejects an empty list).
2817    pub domains: Vec<String>,
2818    /// The ACME directory URL. Defaults to Let's Encrypt production
2819    /// (`https://acme-v02.api.letsencrypt.org/directory`). Set to the
2820    /// staging directory (`https://acme-staging-v02.api.letsencrypt.org/directory`)
2821    /// for testing to avoid rate limits.
2822    #[serde(
2823        default = "default_acme_directory",
2824        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_acme_directory"
2825    )]
2826    pub directory_url: String,
2827    /// Contact email addresses registered with the ACME account. Let's
2828    /// Encrypt requires at least one for production issuance.
2829    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
2830    pub contact: Vec<String>,
2831    /// Challenge type: `http-01` (serves a token over HTTP on port 80)
2832    /// or `tls-alpn-01` (serves a challenge cert over TLS-ALPN on port
2833    /// 443). Default `tls-alpn-01` (no separate HTTP listener needed).
2834    #[serde(
2835        default = "default_acme_challenge",
2836        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_acme_challenge"
2837    )]
2838    pub challenge: AcmeChallenge,
2839    /// Use the staging directory instead of production. When true,
2840    /// `directory_url` is overridden to the Let's Encrypt staging
2841    /// endpoint. Staging certs are NOT trusted by browsers — use for
2842    /// testing only. Default false.
2843    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
2844    pub staging: bool,
2845    /// Path to the directory where ACME state is persisted (account
2846    /// key, issued certificates). The gateway must have read/write
2847    /// access. Defaults to `./acme-state`.
2848    #[serde(
2849        default = "default_acme_state_dir",
2850        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_acme_state_dir"
2851    )]
2852    pub state_dir: String,
2853}
2854
2855/// ACME challenge type.
2856#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema, Default)]
2857#[serde(rename_all = "kebab-case")]
2858pub enum AcmeChallenge {
2859    /// HTTP-01 challenge: serves a token over HTTP on port 80. Requires
2860    /// a separate HTTP listener bound to port 80 (or port forwarding).
2861    Http01,
2862    /// TLS-ALPN-01 challenge: serves a challenge certificate over TLS
2863    /// with the `acme-tls/1` ALPN protocol on port 443. No separate
2864    /// listener needed (the default). Default.
2865    #[default]
2866    TlsAlpn01,
2867}
2868
2869fn default_acme_directory() -> String {
2870    "https://acme-v02.api.letsencrypt.org/directory".to_string()
2871}
2872
2873fn is_default_acme_directory(s: &str) -> bool {
2874    s == default_acme_directory()
2875}
2876
2877fn default_acme_challenge() -> AcmeChallenge {
2878    AcmeChallenge::TlsAlpn01
2879}
2880
2881fn is_default_acme_challenge(c: &AcmeChallenge) -> bool {
2882    *c == AcmeChallenge::TlsAlpn01
2883}
2884
2885fn default_acme_state_dir() -> String {
2886    "./acme-state".to_string()
2887}
2888
2889fn is_default_acme_state_dir(s: &str) -> bool {
2890    s == default_acme_state_dir()
2891}
2892
2893fn is_default_zero_rtt(p: &ZeroRttPolicy) -> bool {
2894    *p == ZeroRttPolicy::Reject
2895}
2896
2897/// 0-RTT (early data) acceptance policy for HTTP/3 (DW-088).
2898#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema, Default)]
2899#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
2900pub enum ZeroRttPolicy {
2901    /// Accept 0-RTT early data. Non-idempotent requests are rejected
2902    /// with 425 Too Early (replay-safe).
2903    Accept,
2904    /// Reject 0-RTT early data (default). The client must complete a
2905    /// full 1-RTT handshake before sending a request.
2906    #[default]
2907    Reject,
2908}
2909
2910/// One SNI-scoped certificate pair for TLS termination.
2911#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2912#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
2913pub struct TlsCertificate {
2914    /// Server names (SNI values) this certificate answers. Exact match,
2915    /// case-insensitive per rustls.
2916    pub server_names: Vec<String>,
2917    /// Path to the PEM certificate chain.
2918    pub cert_file: String,
2919    /// Path to the PEM private key.
2920    pub key_file: String,
2921}
2922
2923/// One SNI-to-upstream routing rule for TLS passthrough.
2924#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2925#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
2926pub struct SniRoute {
2927    /// Server names (SNI values) routed to `upstream`. Exact match.
2928    pub server_names: Vec<String>,
2929    /// Name of the upstream that receives the spliced TLS stream.
2930    pub upstream: String,
2931}
2932
2933/// L4 proxying configuration for a `tcp` or `udp` listener (DW-103).
2934///
2935/// The listener accepts raw L4 connections and splices them byte-for-byte
2936/// to an upstream endpoint. When `sni_routing` is true (TCP only), the
2937/// dispatcher peeks the TLS ClientHello SNI (reusing the SNI extraction
2938/// from DW-008 TLS passthrough) and selects the upstream from the
2939/// listener's `tls.sni_routes` -- the same routing table passthrough
2940/// uses. When `sni_routing` is false, the configured `upstream` receives
2941/// every connection.
2942#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2943#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
2944pub struct L4Config {
2945    /// Name of the upstream that receives the spliced stream. Required
2946    /// when `sni_routing` is false; when `sni_routing` is true the
2947    /// upstream is selected per-connection from `tls.sni_routes`, and
2948    /// this field is the fallback for connections with no SNI or an
2949    /// unmatched name (absent = close the connection on no match).
2950    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2951    pub upstream: Option<String>,
2952    /// Peek the TLS ClientHello SNI and route the connection to the
2953    /// upstream matched by the listener's `tls.sni_routes` (reusing the
2954    /// SNI extraction from DW-008 TLS passthrough). TCP only; rejected
2955    /// on UDP listeners by validation (UDP has no byte-stream handshake
2956    /// to peek). When false, every connection is spliced to the
2957    /// configured `upstream`.
2958    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
2959    pub sni_routing: bool,
2960    /// Idle timeout in seconds for an established splice. When neither
2961    /// side sends data for this duration, the splice is closed. Absent
2962    /// (or 0) means no idle timeout (the splice runs until either side
2963    /// closes). Bounded by validation to at most 1 hour.
2964    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2965    pub idle_timeout_s: Option<u64>,
2966    /// DP-01 (#234): UDP session idle timeout in seconds. A UDP session
2967    /// (per-client upstream socket) that receives no datagrams for this
2968    /// duration is evicted from the session table. Default 30s. UDP
2969    /// only; ignored for TCP listeners.
2970    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2971    pub session_timeout_s: Option<u64>,
2972    /// DP-01 (#234): maximum concurrent UDP sessions. When the session
2973    /// table is full, the oldest idle session is evicted (LRU). Default
2974    /// 4096. UDP only; ignored for TCP listeners.
2975    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
2976    pub max_sessions: Option<usize>,
2977}
2978
2979fn default_tls_mode() -> TlsMode {
2980    TlsMode::Terminate
2981}
2982
2983#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2984#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
2985pub enum TlsMode {
2986    Terminate,
2987    Passthrough,
2988}
2989
2990/// Match rules (path/host/method/header) plus action and attached policies.
2991#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
2992#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
2993pub struct Route {
2994    pub name: String,
2995    /// Name of the service this route targets.
2996    pub service: String,
2997    pub r#match: RouteMatch,
2998    pub action: RouteAction,
2999    /// Attached policy names. All applicable levels' rules AND together
3000    /// (see the rate-limiter module docs); the resolution order is
3001    /// consumer > route > service > listener > global.
3002    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
3003    pub policies: Vec<String>,
3004    /// Request priority class for load shedding (DW-016): 0 (lowest) to 10
3005    /// (highest); absent means the default 5. When the gateway concurrency
3006    /// cap is saturated, requests at `high_priority` (>= 8) draw from a
3007    /// small reserved sub-allowance of the cap that lower-priority traffic
3008    /// cannot use (see `proxy` module docs); preemption is impossible, so
3009    /// normal traffic is shed first rather than displaced. Validation
3010    /// rejects values above 10.
3011    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3012    pub priority: Option<u8>,
3013    /// Require authenticated requests (DW-019): a request that arrives
3014    /// WITHOUT a recognized credential (or with an invalid one) is
3015    /// rejected with 401 and a `WWW-Authenticate` challenge. Absent
3016    /// (the default) allows anonymous traffic through; note that an
3017    /// INVALID presented credential is always rejected with 401
3018    /// regardless of this flag.
3019    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
3020    pub auth_required: bool,
3021    /// Route-scoped CORS policy (DW-027, feature analysis 4.14): controls
3022    /// how the gateway answers browser cross-origin requests on this
3023    /// route. When present, a CORS PREFLIGHT (`OPTIONS` carrying both
3024    /// `Origin` and `Access-Control-Request-Method`) is answered by the
3025    /// gateway itself, before authentication and never forwarded
3026    /// upstream; actual (non-preflight) responses carry the policy's
3027    /// CORS headers. Non-CORS requests are untouched. A preflight on a
3028    /// route whose `match.methods` list excludes `OPTIONS` never matches
3029    /// the route (the documented request-path order applies first) —
3030    /// include `OPTIONS` in the method list of CORS routes.
3031    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3032    pub cors: Option<Cors>,
3033    /// Route-scoped response compression (DW-027, feature analysis 4.13):
3034    /// negotiates gzip/brotli/zstd against the request's `Accept-Encoding`
3035    /// and compresses the response body per policy. Streaming-safe: the
3036    /// body is compressed chunk-by-chunk with a per-chunk flush, never
3037    /// buffered whole. Disabled (absent) by default — the dataplane
3038    /// forwards bytes untouched.
3039    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3040    pub compression: Option<Compression>,
3041    /// Route-scoped request size limits (DW-027, feature analysis 4.16):
3042    /// per-route body and header caps enforced after route resolution.
3043    /// Over-limit requests are rejected by the gateway with the JSON
3044    /// error envelope (413 for bodies, 431 for headers) before any
3045    /// upstream contact when the size is declared up front
3046    /// (`Content-Length`); streaming bodies of unknown length are
3047    /// aborted as soon as they cross the cap.
3048    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3049    pub limits: Option<RequestLimits>,
3050    /// Route-level authorization rules (DW-020, feature analysis 4.7).
3051    /// Absent (the default) imposes no authorization on the route. A
3052    /// PRESENT-but-entirely-empty block (no consumers, groups, scopes,
3053    /// claims, or `ip_acl`) is likewise a no-op at evaluation time —
3054    /// it imposes nothing, exactly like an absent one — but it is
3055    /// always a config-authoring mistake (a rule block with no rules),
3056    /// so validation REJECTS it: omit the block instead of emptying it.
3057    /// When present (and non-empty), the rules are evaluated after
3058    /// authentication and BEFORE
3059    /// rate limiting; see [`Authz`] for the rule semantics. Presence of
3060    /// any identity rule implies authentication (an anonymous request is
3061    /// rejected 401); an `ip_acl`-only block is the one case that can
3062    /// permit anonymous access (from an allowed IP). Precedence across
3063    /// levels (consumer > route > service > listener > global) is
3064    /// resolved by the `authz` module's resolver; every level has a
3065    /// config attachment point (`consumers[].authorization`,
3066    /// `services[].authorization`, `listeners[].authorization`, and the
3067    /// gateway-level `authorization`).
3068    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3069    pub authorization: Option<Authz>,
3070    /// API deprecation policy (DW-048): emits the standard deprecation
3071    /// signal headers on this route's responses. See [`Deprecation`].
3072    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3073    pub deprecation: Option<Deprecation>,
3074    /// Route maintenance mode (DW-041): when present, every request the
3075    /// route matches is answered by the GATEWAY with a 503 carrying
3076    /// `Retry-After` and the JSON error envelope (`maintenance` code) —
3077    /// before route limits, CORS preflight handling (preflights
3078    /// themselves are exempt, see [`Maintenance`]), authentication, and
3079    /// the route action (proxy, redirect, and respond actions alike
3080    /// never run). Toggled by config reload like every route field:
3081    /// publish a generation with the block to enter maintenance and one
3082    /// without it to leave. See [`Maintenance`].
3083    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3084    pub maintenance: Option<Maintenance>,
3085    /// Request/response transforms (DW-028, feature analysis 4.12):
3086    /// header and query manipulation on the forwarded request and the
3087    /// route's responses, plus the size-capped JSON-pointer body
3088    /// transform — the transforms surface's ONE explicitly buffering
3089    /// piece. Absent (the default): bytes and headers flow untouched.
3090    /// See [`Transforms`].
3091    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3092    pub transforms: Option<Transforms>,
3093    /// Security-header injection (DW-028, feature analysis 5-Security):
3094    /// HSTS, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`,
3095    /// `Content-Security-Policy`, and `X-Frame-Options`, stamped on
3096    /// EVERY response the route emits (action responses and gateway
3097    /// short-circuits alike), replacing any upstream-sent values — the
3098    /// gateway is the source of truth at its edge. See
3099    /// [`SecurityHeaders`].
3100    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3101    pub security_headers: Option<SecurityHeaders>,
3102    /// Opt out of the gateway-level `default_security_headers`
3103    /// baseline (SEC-09, #213): when true, a route with no
3104    /// `security_headers` block ships WITHOUT the gateway's default
3105    /// security headers. A route that carries its own
3106    /// `security_headers` block always uses it (the block fully
3107    /// replaces the default); this flag is for the rare route that
3108    /// deliberately needs no security headers at all. Default:
3109    /// false (inherit the default when present).
3110    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
3111    pub security_headers_opt_out: bool,
3112    /// Response field masking (DW-029, feature analysis 5-Security):
3113    /// redacts the named RFC 6901 JSON pointers from the route's
3114    /// responses — the floor `fields` for every consumer, plus the
3115    /// `groups` additions for members of each consumer group — before
3116    /// any other body-handling stage. The redaction is fail-closed: a
3117    /// response the gateway cannot prove clean (encoded, non-JSON,
3118    /// over the cap, unparseable, or missing a configured pointer)
3119    /// answers 502, never a passthrough. See [`Masking`].
3120    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3121    pub masking: Option<Masking>,
3122    /// Route-scoped response caching (DW-037, feature analysis
3123    /// 5-Protocol): opts the route's cacheable GET traffic into the
3124    /// local response cache behind the `CacheStore` extension seam.
3125    /// Absent (the default): responses are never cached or buffered.
3126    /// The cache key folds in the route, the consumer, the inbound
3127    /// path + query, and the effective vary set — masked (DW-029) and
3128    /// consumer-group-specific variants never cross consumers. Stored
3129    /// bodies are the POST-masking/transform bytes (replay consistency
3130    /// under route-scoped policies), stored identity (never compressed;
3131    /// compression re-negotiates per request), and the decoration tail
3132    /// (compression onward) re-runs on every replay. See
3133    /// [`RouteCache`] and `dataplane::response_cache`.
3134    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3135    pub cache: Option<RouteCache>,
3136    /// Per-route METHOD ALLOWLIST (DW-030): when non-empty, a request
3137    /// that resolved this route but whose method is not in the list is
3138    /// answered by the gateway with `405 Method Not Allowed` plus an
3139    /// `Allow` header listing the configured methods (RFC 9110 10.2.1).
3140    /// Distinct from `match.methods`, which gates ROUTE RESOLUTION (a
3141    /// miss falls through to 404 / other routes); this list gates the
3142    /// ALREADY-matched route. Placement (frozen): after route
3143    /// resolution and the maintenance 503, before the route limits and
3144    /// CORS preflight short-circuit and authentication — the allowlist
3145    /// is a statement about the route, not the request's shape (the
3146    /// DW-041 maintenance ordering argument), and a CORS PREFLIGHT
3147    /// (`OPTIONS` + preflight markers) is exempt exactly like the
3148    /// maintenance 503 (the preflight asks about the GATEWAY's
3149    /// cross-origin policy, not the resource; failing it would hide the
3150    /// CORS answer from the browser). Matching is case-insensitive, the
3151    /// same comparison `match.methods` uses. HEAD is NOT implicitly
3152    /// granted by GET — a route supporting HEAD alongside GET lists
3153    /// both (the allowlist is exhaustive by design; implicit grants
3154    /// would leak methods the operator never named). Empty (the
3155    /// default) allows every method, exactly like pre-DW-030.
3156    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
3157    pub methods: Vec<String>,
3158    /// Service-level objectives (DW-052, feature analysis 4.17): the
3159    /// route's availability target and optional latency objective,
3160    /// exported as `dwara_slo_burn_rate` / `dwara_slo_target` metrics
3161    /// over process-local 5m and 1h sliding windows for multiwindow
3162    /// burn-rate alerting. Availability counts a request BAD only when
3163    /// the GATEWAY answers 5xx (client errors are the caller's policy,
3164    /// not this route's availability). Absent (the default): the route
3165    /// records nothing and exports no SLO series. See [`RouteSlo`].
3166    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3167    pub slo: Option<RouteSlo>,
3168    /// WebSocket policy (DW-039, feature analysis 4.13): origin
3169    /// allowlisting before an upgrade is offered and post-upgrade
3170    /// frame-rate policing on the tunneled connection. Absent (the
3171    /// default): upgrades are transparent — any origin, unlimited
3172    /// frame rate, the generic 101 tunnel unchanged. See
3173    /// [`RouteWebsocket`].
3174    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3175    pub websocket: Option<RouteWebsocket>,
3176    /// WAF-lite heuristic filtering (DW-051): pattern-matching
3177    /// inspection of the request path, query string, selected
3178    /// headers, and body (when JSON or form-urlencoded) for common
3179    /// SQL-injection, XSS, and path-traversal signatures. Absent (the
3180    /// default): no inspection. When `enabled` is true, the WAF runs
3181    /// after the route method allowlist and before the route limits —
3182    /// a content filter that rejects malicious requests before any
3183    /// resource is spent on auth or rate limiting. `dry_run` evaluates
3184    /// and logs matches without blocking (audit-log-only mode). See
3185    /// [`RouteWaf`].
3186    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3187    pub waf: Option<RouteWaf>,
3188    /// GraphQL awareness (DW-099): query depth/complexity limits and
3189    /// optional persisted-query enforcement for routes that front a
3190    /// GraphQL server. When enabled, the gateway rejects abusive
3191    /// queries before the route limits and authentication. Absent
3192    /// (the default): no GraphQL checks. Compiled into every build
3193    /// (no cargo feature). See [`RouteGraphql`].
3194    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3195    pub graphql: Option<RouteGraphql>,
3196    /// gRPC-Web framing translation + JSON-to-gRPC transcoding (DW-101):
3197    /// when enabled, the gateway translates gRPC-Web framing to native
3198    /// gRPC for the upstream and wraps the response back. When
3199    /// transcoding is enabled, JSON requests are translated to/from
3200    /// protobuf using .proto descriptors supplied via config. Absent
3201    /// (the default): no translation. The block parses and validates in
3202    /// every build (no cargo feature); the runtime translation is
3203    /// scaffolded and not yet dispatched from the request path. See
3204    /// [`GrpcWeb`].
3205    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3206    pub grpc_web: Option<GrpcWeb>,
3207    /// Protocol translation (DW-100): translates the request/response
3208    /// bodies between two wire protocols on this route's forward and
3209    /// response paths. When enabled, the gateway converts the client's
3210    /// body to the upstream's wire format and the upstream's response
3211    /// back to the client's wire format. Absent (the default): no
3212    /// translation. The block parses and validates in every build (no
3213    /// cargo feature); the runtime translation is scaffolded and not
3214    /// yet dispatched from the request path.
3215    /// See [`Translation`].
3216    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3217    pub translation: Option<Translation>,
3218    /// Request validation (DW-047): an optional JSON-schema block the
3219    /// gateway validates the request body against before the route
3220    /// action runs. A mismatch answers 400 `validation_failed` with the
3221    /// offending paths in the JSON error envelope; a match proceeds to
3222    /// the action (proxy, mock, ...). This is a SIMPLER surface than
3223    /// full OpenAPI parameter/header/query validation — it covers the
3224    /// body only, the common case for write endpoints. The schema is a
3225    /// minimal JSON-Schema subset (type, required, properties, items,
3226    /// enum, minimum, maximum, minLength, maxLength); `$ref` is not
3227    /// supported. See [`RequestValidation`].
3228    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3229    pub request_validation: Option<RequestValidation>,
3230    /// OpenAPI import metadata (DW-047): when this route was generated
3231    /// by `dwara import openapi`, this block records the source
3232    /// operation's `operationId`, `summary`, and `tags` for
3233    /// traceability. It has no runtime effect — the gateway ignores it
3234    /// — but it survives round-trips so the operator can audit which
3235    /// spec operation a route came from. Absent for hand-authored
3236    /// routes.
3237    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3238    pub openapi: Option<OpenApiMeta>,
3239    /// Shadow traffic mirroring (DW-062): fire-and-forget duplicate
3240    /// requests to a mirror upstream. The mirror response is discarded
3241    /// and never impacts the primary request's latency. A percentage
3242    /// controls sampling (0 = never, 100 = always). Absent (the
3243    /// default): no mirroring. See [`MirrorConfig`].
3244    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3245    pub mirror: Option<MirrorConfig>,
3246    /// Fault injection (DW-062): percentage-based delays and aborts for
3247    /// chaos testing. A delay injects a fixed latency before the
3248    /// request is forwarded; an abort short-circuits with a configured
3249    /// status without contacting the upstream. Both are sampled by
3250    /// percentage (0 = never, 100 = always). Absent (the default): no
3251    /// fault injection. See [`FaultInjection`].
3252    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3253    pub fault_injection: Option<FaultInjection>,
3254    /// Plugin names to run on this route (DW-055). Each name must
3255    /// reference a plugin defined in the top-level `plugins` list.
3256    /// Plugins run in declaration order at their declared phases
3257    /// (request-path dispatch, DW-157). Routes without plugins take
3258    /// the untouched fast path.
3259    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
3260    pub plugins: Vec<String>,
3261    /// SCALE-12 (#191): per-route filter-chain ordering and dry-run
3262    /// overrides. When absent, the global `filter_chain` config (or
3263    /// the default order) applies. See [`FilterChainConfig`].
3264    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3265    pub filter_chain: Option<FilterChainConfig>,
3266    /// SEC-05: OIDC browser login flow as a route auth mode. When
3267    /// present, the gateway acts as an OIDC relying party: unauthenticated
3268    /// browser requests are redirected to the IdP's authorization
3269    /// endpoint, and the callback (`redirect_uri`) exchanges the
3270    /// authorization code for tokens, sets a session cookie, and
3271    /// redirects the user back to the original URL. Subsequent requests
3272    /// carry the session cookie (validated by the gateway). Absent (the
3273    /// default): no browser login flow (the route uses its normal auth
3274    /// mode). See [`OidcLoginConfig`].
3275    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3276    pub oidc_login: Option<OidcLoginConfig>,
3277}

3279/// SEC-05: OIDC browser login flow configuration for a route. The
3280/// gateway redirects unauthenticated browser requests to the IdP,
3281/// exchanges the authorization code for tokens, sets a session cookie,
3282/// and validates the cookie on subsequent requests.
3283#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3284#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
3285pub struct OidcLoginConfig {
3286    /// The OIDC provider name (must match an entry in
3287    /// `gateway.oidc_providers`).
3288    pub provider: String,
3289    /// The redirect URI the IdP sends the authorization code to. This
3290    /// must be a path on the gateway (e.g. `/auth/callback`) and must
3291    /// be registered with the IdP. The gateway handles the callback
3292    /// automatically when this route is matched.
3293    pub redirect_uri: String,
3294    /// The session cookie name. Default `dwara_session`. The cookie is
3295    /// `HttpOnly`, `Secure` (when the listener is TLS), `SameSite=Lax`,
3296    /// and carries a signed session ID.
3297    #[serde(
3298        default = "default_oidc_session_cookie",
3299        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_oidc_session_cookie"
3300    )]
3301    pub session_cookie: String,
3302    /// Session lifetime in seconds (default 3600 = 1 hour). After this
3303    /// duration the cookie expires and the user must re-authenticate.
3304    #[serde(
3305        default = "default_oidc_session_ttl_s",
3306        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_oidc_session_ttl_s"
3307    )]
3308    pub session_ttl_s: u64,
3309    /// The post-logout redirect URL. When the user visits the logout
3310    /// path (the redirect URI with `/logout` appended, e.g.
3311    /// `/auth/callback/logout`), the session cookie is cleared and the
3312    /// user is redirected to this URL. Default: the IdP's
3313    /// `end_session_endpoint` (from discovery) with
3314    /// `post_logout_redirect_uri` set to the gateway's root.
3315    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3316    pub post_logout_url: Option<String>,
3317}

3319fn default_oidc_session_cookie() -> String {
3320    "dwara_session".to_string()
3321}
3322
3323fn is_default_oidc_session_cookie(s: &str) -> bool {
3324    s == default_oidc_session_cookie()
3325}
3326
3327fn default_oidc_session_ttl_s() -> u64 {
3328    3600
3329}
3330
3331fn is_default_oidc_session_ttl_s(v: &u64) -> bool {
3332    *v == 3600
3333}

3335/// OpenAPI import metadata attached to a route (DW-047). No runtime
3336/// effect; purely for traceability from the generated config back to
3337/// the source spec operation.
3338#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3339#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
3340pub struct OpenApiMeta {
3341    /// The `operationId` from the source OpenAPI operation (may be
3342    /// absent if the operation had none — the route name is then a
3343    /// path+method fallback).
3344    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3345    pub operation_id: Option<String>,
3346    /// The `summary` from the source OpenAPI operation.
3347    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3348    pub summary: Option<String>,
3349    /// The `tags` from the source OpenAPI operation.
3350    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
3351    pub tags: Vec<String>,
3352    /// The HTTP method of the source operation (uppercased).
3353    pub method: String,
3354    /// The path template from the source OpenAPI spec (e.g.
3355    /// `/pets/{id}`).
3356    pub path: String,
3357}

3359/// Shadow traffic mirroring configuration (DW-062,
3360/// `routes[].mirror`). Fire-and-forget duplicate requests to a named
3361/// mirror upstream; the mirror response is discarded and never impacts
3362/// the primary request's latency. A percentage controls sampling.
3363#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3364#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
3365pub struct MirrorConfig {
3366    /// Name of the upstream to receive mirrored (shadow) requests. The
3367    /// mirror upstream is separate from the route's service upstream —
3368    /// it exists solely to receive shadow traffic. The upstream must
3369    /// exist in the `upstreams` list.
3370    pub upstream: String,
3371    /// Percentage of requests to mirror (0..=100). 0 mirrors nothing;
3372    /// 100 mirrors every request. The sampling is per-request (a
3373    /// random draw against this percentage).
3374    pub percentage: u8,
3375    /// Hard timeout for the mirror request in milliseconds. If the
3376    /// mirror upstream does not respond within this duration, the
3377    /// mirror request is abandoned. Default 2000 (2 seconds). The
3378    /// mirror is fire-and-forget, so this timeout never affects the
3379    /// primary request.
3380    #[serde(
3381        default = "default_mirror_timeout_ms",
3382        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_mirror_timeout_ms"
3383    )]
3384    pub timeout_ms: u64,
3385}
3386
3387fn default_mirror_timeout_ms() -> u64 {
3388    2000
3389}
3390
3391fn is_default_mirror_timeout_ms(v: &u64) -> bool {
3392    *v == 2000
3393}

3395/// Fault injection configuration (DW-062, `routes[].fault_injection`).
3396/// Percentage-based delays and aborts for chaos testing. A delay
3397/// injects a fixed latency before the request is forwarded; an abort
3398/// short-circuits with a configured status without contacting the
3399/// upstream. Both may be present simultaneously — the abort is
3400/// evaluated first, and if it does not fire, the delay is applied.
3401#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3402#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
3403pub struct FaultInjection {
3404    /// Abort injection: return a configured status without forwarding.
3405    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3406    pub abort: Option<FaultAbort>,
3407    /// Delay injection: inject a fixed delay before forwarding.
3408    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3409    pub delay: Option<FaultDelay>,
3410}
3411
3412/// Abort fault injection (DW-062): short-circuit the request with a
3413/// configured HTTP status, sampled by percentage.
3414#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3415#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
3416pub struct FaultAbort {
3417    /// Percentage of requests to abort (0..=100).
3418    pub percentage: u8,
3419    /// HTTP status code to return (100..=599).
3420    pub status: u16,
3421}
3422
3423/// Delay fault injection (DW-062): inject a fixed delay before
3424/// forwarding the request, sampled by percentage.
3425#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3426#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
3427pub struct FaultDelay {
3428    /// Percentage of requests to delay (0..=100).
3429    pub percentage: u8,
3430    /// Fixed delay in milliseconds (1..=300000, i.e. up to 5 minutes).
3431    pub fixed_ms: u64,
3432}
3433
3434/// Route-scoped request-body validation (DW-047,
3435/// `routes[].request_validation`).
3436///
3437/// A minimal JSON-Schema subset the gateway validates the request body
3438/// against before the route action runs. A mismatch answers 400
3439/// `validation_failed` (the JSON error envelope carries the offending
3440/// instance paths); a match proceeds normally. The schema is compiled
3441/// at config publish time (an invalid schema fails validation); the
3442/// body is buffered up to the route's `limits.max_body_bytes` (or a
3443/// 1 MiB default when no limit is set) for validation, then replayed
3444/// to the action. See [`JsonSchema`] for the supported keywords.
3445#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3446#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
3447pub struct RequestValidation {
3448    /// The JSON Schema the request body must satisfy. A minimal subset
3449    /// of JSON Schema Draft 2020-12 is supported: `type`, `required`,
3450    /// `properties`, `items`, `enum`, `minimum`, `maximum`,
3451    /// `minLength`, `maxLength`, and `additionalProperties` (bool or a
3452    /// schema). `$ref` is NOT supported (inline your schemas). An
3453    /// invalid schema fails config validation at publish time.
3454    pub body_schema: BodySchema,
3455    /// SEC-14: dry-run mode. When true, the gateway evaluates the
3456    /// schema and records violations (log + metric) but does NOT reject
3457    /// the request — the body is forwarded to the upstream even when it
3458    /// fails validation. Useful for rolling out a new schema without
3459    /// breaking existing callers. Default false (reject on mismatch).
3460    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
3461    pub dry_run: bool,
3462}
3463
3464/// A minimal JSON-Schema subset (DW-047) for request-body validation.
3465///
3466/// Supports the keywords sufficient for typical OpenAPI-derived body
3467/// schemas: `type`, `required`, `properties`, `items`, `enum`,
3468/// `minimum`, `maximum`, `minLength`, `maxLength`, and
3469/// `additionalProperties`. Nested objects and arrays compose
3470/// recursively. Unknown keywords are IGNORED (forward compatibility
3471/// with richer specs) EXCEPT at the root `RequestValidation.body_schema`
3472/// where `deny_unknown_fields` on the surrounding config struct already
3473/// rejects typos in the wrapper; this struct itself is permissive so a
3474/// real-world schema carrying `description`, `example`, or `$schema`
3475/// does not fail to parse.
3476#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3477pub struct BodySchema {
3478    /// The JSON type: `object`, `array`, `string`, `number`,
3479    /// `integer`, `boolean`, or `null`.
3480    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3481    pub r#type: Option<String>,
3482    /// For `object`: the required property names.
3483    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
3484    pub required: Vec<String>,
3485    /// For `object`: named property schemas.
3486    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::collections::BTreeMap::is_empty")]
3487    pub properties: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, BodySchema>,
3488    /// For `object`: `true` (default, allows extra properties), `false`
3489    /// (rejects any property not in `properties`), or a schema every
3490    /// extra property must satisfy.
3491    #[serde(
3492        default,
3493        skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none",
3494        rename = "additionalProperties"
3495    )]
3496    pub additional_properties: Option<Box<AdditionalProperties>>,
3497    /// For `array`: the schema every element must satisfy.
3498    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3499    pub items: Option<Box<BodySchema>>,
3500    /// An enum of allowed values (compared by JSON equality).
3501    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
3502    pub r#enum: Vec<serde_json::Value>,
3503    /// Inclusive numeric minimum (for `number`/`integer`).
3504    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3505    pub minimum: Option<f64>,
3506    /// Inclusive numeric maximum (for `number`/`integer`).
3507    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3508    pub maximum: Option<f64>,
3509    /// Minimum string length (for `string`).
3510    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none", rename = "minLength")]
3511    pub min_length: Option<u64>,
3512    /// Maximum string length (for `string`).
3513    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none", rename = "maxLength")]
3514    pub max_length: Option<u64>,
3515}
3516
3517/// The `additionalProperties` keyword accepts either a boolean or a
3518/// nested schema; this union models both.
3519#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3520#[serde(untagged)]
3521pub enum AdditionalProperties {
3522    /// A boolean: `true` allows any extra property, `false` rejects
3523    /// any property not listed in `properties`.
3524    Bool(bool),
3525    /// A schema every extra property must satisfy.
3526    Schema(BodySchema),
3527}
3528
3529/// Route-scoped WAF-lite config (DW-051, `routes[].waf`).
3530///
3531/// A lightweight, heuristic-based web application filter: regex pattern
3532/// matching for SQL injection, XSS, and path traversal signatures across
3533/// the request path, query string, selected headers (User-Agent, Referer,
3534/// Cookie, X-Forwarded-For), and body (when the content type is JSON or
3535/// form-urlencoded, up to `max_body_inspect_bytes`). This is NOT a full
3536/// WAF — it is a first-line traffic filter that rejects obvious attack
3537/// signatures before authentication or rate limiting. Per-route opt-in;
3538/// routes without a `waf` block are never inspected.
3539#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3540#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
3541pub struct RouteWaf {
3542    /// Master switch. Default false: the WAF block is inert even when
3543    /// present (allows staged rollout). When true, the configured
3544    /// filter categories run on every request the route matches.
3545    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
3546    pub enabled: bool,
3547    /// Audit-log-only mode (dry-run synergy, DW-041): when true, the
3548    /// WAF evaluates every filter and LOGS matches (a `dwara::policy`
3549    /// warn event + the `dwara_waf_total{outcome="logged"}` counter)
3550    /// but does NOT block — the request continues to the upstream.
3551    /// Lets an operator observe what the WAF would catch before
3552    /// enforcing it. Default false: matches are blocked with 403.
3553    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
3554    pub dry_run: bool,
3555    /// Which filter categories to run. An empty or absent list
3556    /// defaults to all three (`sqli`, `xss`, `path_traversal`).
3557    /// Validation rejects an enabled WAF with an explicit non-empty
3558    /// list containing an unknown category name.
3559    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
3560    pub filters: Vec<String>,
3561    /// Maximum number of body bytes to inspect (0 = do not inspect
3562    /// the body at all; 1..=1048576 = inspect up to this many bytes).
3563    /// Default 131072 (128 KiB). Bodies larger than this limit are
3564    /// inspected only up to the limit (a malicious payload beyond the
3565    /// limit is not caught — the trade-off for a bounded inspection
3566    /// cost). Validation rejects values > 1048576 (1 MiB).
3567    #[serde(
3568        default = "default_waf_max_body_inspect_bytes",
3569        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_waf_max_body_inspect_bytes"
3570    )]
3571    pub max_body_inspect_bytes: u64,
3572    /// Optional custom regex pattern overrides (additional patterns
3573    /// appended to the built-in signatures for each filter category).
3574    /// Each entry is a regex string compiled at config publish time;
3575    /// an invalid regex fails validation. When absent, only the
3576    /// built-in signatures are used.
3577    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
3578    pub custom_patterns: Vec<String>,
3579}
3580
3581fn default_waf_max_body_inspect_bytes() -> u64 {
3582    131_072
3583}
3584
3585fn is_default_waf_max_body_inspect_bytes(v: &u64) -> bool {
3586    *v == 131_072
3587}
3588
3589/// SEC-08 (#212): A single OWASP CRS-compatible WAF rule. Each rule
3590/// has a numeric ID (CRS convention: 9xxxx), a severity level, a
3591/// phase (1 = request headers, 2 = request body), a list of tags, a
3592/// regex pattern, and the inspection targets to apply the pattern
3593/// to. Rules are evaluated in order; matching rules add their
3594/// severity to the request's anomaly score. The request is blocked
3595/// when the score exceeds the configured threshold.
3596#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3597#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
3598pub struct CrsRule {
3599    /// Unique rule ID (CRS convention: 9xxxx). Used for exclusions
3600    /// and logging.
3601    pub id: u32,
3602    /// Rule severity: 1 = critical, 2 = warning, 3 = notice, 4 = info.
3603    /// Higher severity contributes more to the anomaly score.
3604    #[serde(default = "default_crs_severity")]
3605    pub severity: u8,
3606    /// Evaluation phase: 1 = request headers (path, query, headers),
3607    /// 2 = request body. Default 1.
3608    #[serde(default = "default_crs_phase")]
3609    pub phase: u8,
3610    /// Tags for grouping and exclusion (e.g. "OWASP_CRS", "SQL_INJECTION").
3611    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
3612    pub tags: Vec<String>,
3613    /// The regex pattern to match against the target values.
3614    pub pattern: String,
3615    /// Which request parts to inspect: "path", "query", "header",
3616    /// "headers" (all selected headers), "body". Default: ["path", "query"].
3617    #[serde(default = "default_crs_targets")]
3618    pub targets: Vec<String>,
3619    /// Optional list of transformations to apply before matching
3620    /// (e.g. "lowercase", "url_decode", "html_entity_decode"). Default: [].
3621    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
3622    pub transformations: Vec<String>,
3623}
3624
3625fn default_crs_severity() -> u8 {
3626    2
3627}
3628
3629fn default_crs_phase() -> u8 {
3630    1
3631}
3632
3633fn default_crs_targets() -> Vec<String> {
3634    vec!["path".into(), "query".into()]
3635}
3636
3637/// SEC-08 (#212): Global WAF policy with CRS-compatible rules. When
3638/// enabled, the rules are evaluated on every request (regardless of
3639/// route-level WAF config). The anomaly score is accumulated across
3640/// all matching rules; the request is blocked when the score exceeds
3641/// the threshold. Rule exclusions can disable specific rules by ID or
3642/// tag. The paranoia level controls which rules are active (1 =
3643/// default, 2 = more aggressive, 3 = paranoid, 4 = very paranoid).
3644#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3645#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
3646pub struct GlobalWaf {
3647    /// Master switch. Default false.
3648    #[serde(default = "default_false", skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
3649    pub enabled: bool,
3650    /// Audit-log-only mode (same semantics as RouteWaf::dry_run).
3651    #[serde(default = "default_false", skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
3652    pub dry_run: bool,
3653    /// Paranoia level (1-4). Rules with a higher paranoia level than
3654    /// this are skipped. Default 1.
3655    #[serde(
3656        default = "default_crs_paranoia",
3657        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_crs_paranoia"
3658    )]
3659    pub paranoia_level: u8,
3660    /// Anomaly score threshold for blocking. A request is blocked
3661    /// when the sum of matching rule severities reaches this value.
3662    /// Default 5 (critical rules block immediately, warnings need
3663    /// multiple hits).
3664    #[serde(
3665        default = "default_crs_threshold",
3666        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_crs_threshold"
3667    )]
3668    pub anomaly_threshold: u32,
3669    /// Maximum body bytes to inspect (same semantics as
3670    /// RouteWaf::max_body_inspect_bytes). Default 131072.
3671    #[serde(
3672        default = "default_waf_max_body_inspect_bytes",
3673        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_waf_max_body_inspect_bytes"
3674    )]
3675    pub max_body_inspect_bytes: u64,
3676    /// CRS-compatible rules to evaluate.
3677    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
3678    pub rules: Vec<CrsRule>,
3679    /// Rule IDs to exclude (skip even if the rule is in the rules
3680    /// list). Useful for tuning false positives.
3681    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
3682    pub exclude_rule_ids: Vec<u32>,
3683    /// Tags to exclude (skip all rules carrying any of these tags).
3684    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
3685    pub exclude_tags: Vec<String>,
3686}
3687
3688fn default_crs_paranoia() -> u8 {
3689    1
3690}
3691
3692fn is_default_crs_paranoia(v: &u8) -> bool {
3693    *v == 1
3694}
3695
3696fn default_crs_threshold() -> u32 {
3697    5
3698}
3699
3700fn is_default_crs_threshold(v: &u32) -> bool {
3701    *v == 5
3702}
