3704/// Route-scoped GraphQL awareness config (DW-099, `routes[].graphql`).
3705///
3706/// When enabled (compiled into every build; no cargo feature), the
3707/// gateway enforces query depth/complexity limits and optional
3708/// persisted-query enforcement on every request the route matches,
3709/// BEFORE the route limits and authentication. A query whose depth or
3710/// complexity exceeds the configured limits is rejected with 400
3711/// (`graphql_depth_exceeded` or `graphql_complexity_exceeded`); a
3712/// query whose SHA-256 hash is not in the persisted-query store is
3713/// rejected with 400 (`graphql_persisted_query_required`).
3714///
3715/// The check is compiled into every build (no cargo feature): a
3716/// config carrying the block behaves identically in any build.
3717///
3718/// Depth is the maximum brace-nesting level of the query (the
3719/// top-level operation body is depth 1). Complexity is the sum of
3720/// per-field costs: each field's cost is looked up in
3721/// `cost_per_field`, falling back to `complexity_coefficient` (default
3722/// 1) when the field name is not in the map.
3723///
3724/// Both are computed by a
3725/// bounded hand-rolled scanner (no external GraphQL parser dependency).
3726#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3727#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
3728pub struct RouteGraphql {
3729    /// Master switch. Default false: the GraphQL block is inert even
3730    /// when present (allows staged rollout). When true, the depth and
3731    /// complexity checks run on every request the route matches.
3732    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
3733    pub enabled: bool,
3734    /// Maximum query depth (max brace-nesting level). A query whose
3735    /// depth exceeds this is rejected with 400
3736    /// `graphql_depth_exceeded`. Must be > 0 when the block is
3737    /// enabled (validation rejects 0 or absent). The top-level
3738    /// operation body is depth 1, so a value of 1 permits only a
3739    /// flat selection set with no nested fields.
3740    pub depth_limit: usize,
3741    /// Maximum query complexity (sum of per-field costs). A query
3742    /// whose complexity exceeds this is rejected with 400
3743    /// `graphql_complexity_exceeded`. Must be > 0 when the block is
3744    /// enabled.
3745    pub complexity_limit: u64,
3746    /// Default cost per field when the field name is not in
3747    /// `cost_per_field` (default 1). The complexity total is
3748    /// `sum(cost_per_field[name].unwrap_or(complexity_coefficient))`.
3749    #[serde(
3750        default = "default_graphql_complexity_coefficient",
3751        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_graphql_complexity_coefficient"
3752    )]
3753    pub complexity_coefficient: u64,
3754    /// Per-field cost overrides. Maps a field name to its explicit
3755    /// cost; any field not in this map uses
3756    /// `complexity_coefficient`. Empty (the default): every field
3757    /// costs `complexity_coefficient`.
3758    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_graphql_cost_per_field_empty")]
3759    pub cost_per_field: std::collections::HashMap<String, u64>,
3760    /// Persisted-query enforcement (Apollo APQ + GET-by-hash variant).
3761    /// When enabled, every request's query SHA-256 hash must be in the
3762    /// configured `store` (a map of hash -> query text). A hash not in
3763    /// the store is rejected with 400
3764    /// `graphql_persisted_query_required`. Absent (the default): no
3765    /// persisted-query enforcement.
3766    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3767    pub persisted_queries: Option<GraphqlPersistedQueries>,
3768    /// Federation passthrough config (DP-11, #256). When enabled, the
3769    /// gateway treats the route as a federation passthrough: it
3770    /// forwards GraphQL requests to a single federated upstream
3771    /// (subgraph or supergraph router) while preserving
3772    /// federation-specific headers and allowing introspection queries
3773    /// (`_service`, `__schema`) to bypass depth/complexity checks.
3774    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3775    pub federation: Option<GraphqlFederation>,
3776}
3777
3778/// Federation passthrough config (DP-11, #256,
3779/// `routes[].graphql.federation`).
3780///
3781/// When `passthrough` is true, the gateway:
3782/// - Preserves Apollo federation headers on the proxied request
3783///   (`Apollo-Require-Preflight`, `x-apollo-operation-name`,
3784///   `graphql-preflight`, `x-apollo-operation-id`).
3785/// - Allows federation introspection queries (`_service { sdl }`,
3786///   `__schema`, `__type`) to bypass depth and complexity checks.
3787/// - Still applies persisted-query enforcement if configured.
3788///
3789/// This is **passthrough mode**: the gateway does NOT implement query
3790/// planning or subgraph stitching. It proxies to a single upstream
3791/// that is expected to be either a federation router (subgraph-aware)
3792/// or a single subgraph.
3793#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3794#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
3795pub struct GraphqlFederation {
3796    /// Master switch. Default false.
3797    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
3798    pub passthrough: bool,
3799}
3800
3801/// Persisted-query enforcement config (DW-099,
3802/// `routes[].graphql.persisted_queries`).
3803///
3804/// When enabled, the gateway enforces that every request's query
3805/// SHA-256 hash is in the config-supplied `store`. This is the Apollo
3806/// APQ (Automatic Persisted Queries) + GET-by-hash variant: the
3807/// client sends the query text, the gateway computes its SHA-256, and
3808/// verifies the hash is known. An external store (Redis, etc.) is a
3809/// future extension point; v1 uses a config-supplied map.
3810#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3811#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
3812pub struct GraphqlPersistedQueries {
3813    /// Master switch. Default false: the persisted-query block is
3814    /// inert even when present.
3815    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
3816    pub enabled: bool,
3817    /// The persisted-query store: a map of SHA-256 hash (lowercase
3818    /// hex) to query text. Every key must be a non-empty string
3819    /// (validation rejects empty keys). The query text is stored for
3820    /// future use (e.g. GET-by-hash resolution); v1 only checks hash
3821    /// membership.
3822    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_graphql_persisted_store_empty")]
3823    pub store: std::collections::HashMap<String, String>,
3824}
3825
3826fn default_graphql_complexity_coefficient() -> u64 {
3827    1
3828}
3829
3830fn is_default_graphql_complexity_coefficient(v: &u64) -> bool {
3831    *v == 1
3832}
3833
3834fn is_graphql_cost_per_field_empty(v: &std::collections::HashMap<String, u64>) -> bool {
3835    v.is_empty()
3836}
3837
3838fn is_graphql_persisted_store_empty(v: &std::collections::HashMap<String, String>) -> bool {
3839    v.is_empty()
3840}

3842/// Route-scoped gRPC-Web policy (DW-101, `routes[].grpc_web`).
3843///
3844/// When enabled, the gateway translates gRPC-Web framing (the browser-
3845/// friendly variant that replaces HTTP/2 trailers with a trailing
3846/// frame) to native gRPC for the upstream, and wraps the native gRPC
3847/// response back into gRPC-Web framing for the browser. Streaming
3848/// (server-streaming and bidi) is supported: each upstream data chunk
3849/// becomes its own gRPC-Web data frame, and the trailers become a
3850/// final gRPC-Web trailer frame.
3851///
3852/// When `transcoding` is enabled, the gateway additionally accepts
3853/// plain JSON requests (Content-Type: application/json) and translates
3854/// them to protobuf wire bytes for the upstream, and translates the
3855/// protobuf response back to JSON. The mapping is driven by .proto
3856/// descriptors supplied via config.
3857///
3858/// The block parses and validates in every build (no cargo feature);
3859/// the runtime framing/transcoding translation is scaffolded and not
3860/// yet dispatched from the request path. See [`GrpcWebTranscoding`] and
3861/// [`GrpcWebDescriptor`].
3862#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3863#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
3864pub struct GrpcWeb {
3865    /// Master switch. Default false: the gRPC-Web block is inert even
3866    /// when present (allows staged rollout). When true, the gateway
3867    /// translates gRPC-Web framing to native gRPC on the forward path
3868    /// and back on the response path.
3869    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
3870    pub enabled: bool,
3871    /// JSON-to-gRPC transcoding configuration. When enabled, the
3872    /// gateway accepts JSON requests and translates them to protobuf
3873    /// using the supplied .proto descriptors. Absent (the default):
3874    /// only gRPC-Web framing translation runs (no JSON transcoding).
3875    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3876    pub transcoding: Option<GrpcWebTranscoding>,
3877}
3878
3879/// gRPC-Web JSON transcoding configuration (DW-101,
3880/// `routes[].grpc_web.transcoding`).
3881///
3882/// Supplies the .proto descriptor files that map HTTP/JSON requests to
3883/// gRPC methods. Each descriptor names a file (a FileDescriptorSet in
3884/// binary protobuf format), a package, and a service. At config publish
3885/// the gateway loads the descriptors, resolves the service's methods,
3886/// and builds a method map keyed by the fully-qualified gRPC method
3887/// path. The google.api.http annotation on each RPC maps an HTTP path +
3888/// verb to the gRPC method.
3889#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3890#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
3891pub struct GrpcWebTranscoding {
3892    /// Master switch for transcoding. Default false: even when the
3893    /// `transcoding` block is present, transcoding is inert unless
3894    /// this is true. (Allows the descriptor list to be staged before
3895    /// activation.)
3896    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
3897    pub enabled: bool,
3898    /// The .proto descriptor files to load. Each entry names a
3899    /// FileDescriptorSet file (binary protobuf format, as produced by
3900    /// `protoc --descriptor_set_out=...`), the package, and the
3901    /// service within that package. At least one descriptor is
3902    /// required when transcoding is enabled.
3903    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
3904    pub descriptors: Vec<GrpcWebDescriptor>,
3905}
3906
3907/// A .proto descriptor file entry (DW-101,
3908/// `routes[].grpc_web.transcoding.descriptors[]`).
3909#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3910#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
3911pub struct GrpcWebDescriptor {
3912    /// Path to the FileDescriptorSet file (binary protobuf format, as
3913    /// produced by `protoc --descriptor_set_out=...`). The file must
3914    /// exist and be readable at config publish time; a missing or
3915    /// unparseable file fails validation.
3916    pub file: String,
3917    /// The protobuf package name (e.g. `my.api`). Used to locate the
3918    /// service's methods in the descriptor set.
3919    pub package: String,
3920    /// The service name within the package (e.g. `MyService`).
3921    pub service: String,
3922}
3923
3924/// Route-scoped protocol translation policy (DW-100,
3925/// `routes[].translation`).
3926///
3927/// When enabled, the gateway translates the request/response bodies
3928/// between two wire protocols on this route's forward and response
3929/// paths. The `kind` selects the translation direction; the `graphql`
3930/// and `soap` sub-blocks carry the direction-specific config.
3931///
3932/// The block parses and validates in every build (no cargo feature);
3933/// the runtime translators are scaffolded and not yet dispatched from
3934/// the request path.
3935#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3936#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
3937pub struct Translation {
3938    /// The translation direction. Selects which translator runs and
3939    /// which sub-block (`graphql` or `soap`) carries the config.
3940    pub kind: TranslationKind,
3941    /// REST <-> GraphQL translation config. Required when `kind` is
3942    /// `rest_to_graphql` or `graphql_to_rest`; ignored otherwise.
3943    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3944    pub graphql: Option<GraphqlTranslation>,
3945    /// SOAP/XML translation config. Required when `kind` is
3946    /// `rest_to_soap` or `soap_to_rest`; ignored otherwise.
3947    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
3948    pub soap: Option<SoapTranslation>,
3949}
3950
3951/// The protocol translation direction (DW-100,
3952/// `routes[].translation.kind`).
3953#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3954#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
3955pub enum TranslationKind {
3956    /// A REST/JSON client -> GraphQL upstream. The gateway builds a
3957    /// GraphQL query from a config-supplied query template and the
3958    /// REST JSON body.
3959    RestToGraphql,
3960    /// A GraphQL client -> REST upstream. The gateway unwraps the
3961    /// GraphQL `data` envelope into a REST JSON body on the response
3962    /// path.
3963    GraphqlToRest,
3964    /// A REST/JSON client -> SOAP/XML upstream. The gateway wraps the
3965    /// JSON body in a SOAP envelope with the configured operation name
3966    /// and namespace.
3967    RestToSoap,
3968    /// A SOAP/XML client -> REST/JSON upstream. The gateway parses the
3969    /// SOAP envelope and converts the Body's payload element to JSON.
3970    SoapToRest,
3971}
3972
3973/// REST <-> GraphQL translation config (DW-100,
3974/// `routes[].translation.graphql`).
3975#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
3976#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
3977pub struct GraphqlTranslation {
3978    /// The GraphQL query template. `$variable` references are resolved
3979    /// from the REST JSON body's top-level fields and substituted into
3980    /// the query (the resolved values are also sent as the GraphQL
3981    /// `variables` map). Required for `rest_to_graphql`; ignored for
3982    /// `graphql_to_rest` (which only unwraps the response envelope).
3983    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "String::is_empty")]
3984    pub query_template: String,
3985    /// The path to send the GraphQL upstream (REST-to-GraphQL rewrites
3986    /// every request to this path). Default `/graphql`.
3987    #[serde(
3988        default = "default_graphql_upstream_path",
3989        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_graphql_upstream_path"
3990    )]
3991    pub upstream_path: String,
3992}
3993
3994fn default_graphql_upstream_path() -> String {
3995    "/graphql".to_string()
3996}
3997
3998fn is_default_graphql_upstream_path(s: &str) -> bool {
3999    s == "/graphql"
4000}
4001
4002/// SOAP/XML translation config (DW-100, `routes[].translation.soap`).
4003#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
4004#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
4005pub struct SoapTranslation {
4006    /// The SOAP operation name: the Body's first child element name
4007    /// (e.g. `GetUser`). Required for both directions.
4008    pub operation: String,
4009    /// The XML namespace for the operation element
4010    /// (e.g. `http://example.com/api`). Required for `rest_to_soap`
4011    /// (the envelope's payload element carries the namespace); used
4012    /// for `soap_to_rest` only to validate the payload element's
4013    /// namespace when present.
4014    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "String::is_empty")]
4015    pub namespace: String,
4016}

4018/// Route-scoped WebSocket policy (DW-039, `routes[].websocket`).
4019///
4020/// Both knobs are opt-in and independent: an `origins` list gates the
4021/// upgrade handshake (403 before any upstream contact), and
4022/// `max_frames_per_sec` polices the ESTABLISHED tunnel (a client
4023/// sending data frames faster than the allowance is closed with a
4024/// 1008 policy-violation close frame and disconnected). The policing
4025/// works on the client-to-upstream direction only — the gateway
4026/// protects upstreams from abusive clients, not the reverse (an
4027/// abusive upstream is the operator's backend problem).
4028#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
4029#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
4030pub struct RouteWebsocket {
4031    /// Allowed `Origin` header values for the upgrade handshake
4032    /// (case-sensitive exact match, e.g. `https://app.example.com`).
4033    /// Empty or absent: every origin is allowed (the transparent
4034    /// default). Non-empty: ONLY listed origins upgrade — a missing
4035    /// `Origin` header is DENIED (browsers always send one on
4036    /// WebSocket handshakes; a request without one is not a browser
4037    /// and must be named explicitly or fail closed). The literal
4038    /// string `null` matches the sandboxed-document origin by exact
4039    /// match.
4040    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
4041    pub origins: Vec<String>,
4042    /// Post-upgrade policing: the sustained allowance of DATA frames
4043    /// (text, binary, continuation — control frames are free) the
4044    /// CLIENT may send per second, with a one-second burst of the same
4045    /// size. A client past its allowance is closed with close code
4046    /// 1008 (policy violation) and disconnected. Absent: no policing
4047    /// (unlimited, the transparent default). Validation enforces
4048    /// 1..=[`limits::MAX_WEBSOCKET_FRAMES_PER_SEC`].
4049    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4050    pub max_frames_per_sec: Option<u64>,
4051    /// Post-upgrade idle timeout in seconds (DP-05, #253). If no data
4052    /// flows in either direction for this duration, the tunnel is
4053    /// closed. Absent: no idle timeout (the tunnel stays open until
4054    /// one side closes, the prior default). Validation enforces
4055    /// 1..=86400 (1 second to 24 hours).
4056    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4057    pub idle_timeout_s: Option<u64>,
4058    /// Maximum payload size of a single WebSocket DATA frame in bytes
4059    /// (DP-05, #253). A frame exceeding this limit causes the
4060    /// connection to be closed with close code 1009 (message too big).
4061    /// Absent: no frame size limit (the prior default). Validation
4062    /// enforces 1..=16777216 (1 byte to 16 MiB).
4063    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4064    pub max_frame_size_bytes: Option<u64>,
4065}
4066
4067/// Per-route SLO objectives (DW-052, `routes[].slo`).
4068#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
4069#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
4070pub struct RouteSlo {
4071    /// Availability target as a PERCENTAGE in (0, 100]: the share of
4072    /// requests the gateway must answer outside the 5xx class.
4073    /// `99.9` allows one bad request per thousand.
4074    pub availability: f64,
4075    /// Latency objective threshold in milliseconds: a request is BAD
4076    /// for the latency objective when its end-to-end duration exceeds
4077    /// this. Absent: no latency objective (only availability is
4078    /// exported for the route).
4079    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4080    pub latency_ms: Option<f64>,
4081    /// Latency target as a PERCENTAGE in (0, 100): the share of
4082    /// requests that must complete within `latency_ms` (default 99).
4083    /// Requires `latency_ms`.
4084    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4085    pub latency_target: Option<f64>,
4086}
4087
4088/// Route maintenance mode (DW-041): a per-route availability state that
4089/// short-circuits every matched request with a gateway-generated 503.
4090///
4091/// Frozen semantics (enforcement lives in `dataplane::proxy`):
4092///
4093/// - Checked IMMEDIATELY after route resolution, BEFORE the route's
4094///   request limits: maintenance is a statement about the route's
4095///   availability, not about any one request's shape, so every matched
4096///   request gets the same 503 + `Retry-After` answer (an over-limit
4097///   request during maintenance is told "we're down", not "your headers
4098///   are too big" — fixing the headers would still leave it refused).
4099/// - The response is `503` with `Retry-After: <retry_after_secs>` (a
4100///   whole-seconds delay-suggestion, NOT a promise of recovery) and the
4101///   uniform JSON error envelope; `message` replaces the envelope's
4102///   default human text (it never leaks upstream internals — it is
4103///   operator-authored).
4104/// - CORS: an actual (non-preflight) request on a CORS-configured route
4105///   gets the policy's actual-response headers on the 503, so a browser
4106///   client can READ the maintenance envelope cross-origin. A CORS
4107///   PREFLIGHT is exempt: it still answers 204 exactly as without
4108///   maintenance. The preflight is a Fetch-protocol handshake about the
4109///   GATEWAY's cross-origin policy, answered from static config and sent
4110///   without credentials — failing it would surface in the browser as an
4111///   opaque CORS error and hide the very 503 the operator wants clients
4112///   to see on the subsequent actual request.
4113/// - Reserved paths (`/healthz`, `/readyz`, `/metrics`) never match a
4114///   route, so probes and scrapes keep answering through maintenance —
4115///   an orchestrator must not restart a deliberately idling gateway.
4116/// - Unrouted traffic is unaffected (maintenance is per-route; a 404
4117///   stays a 404).
4118#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
4119#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
4120pub struct Maintenance {
4121    /// `Retry-After` value, in whole seconds, sent with the 503. Absent:
4122    /// 60 (one minute — long enough to shed load, short enough that a
4123    /// client polling for recovery notices the route return). Validation
4124    /// rejects 0 (it would invite an immediate retry stampede against a
4125    /// route the operator just took down).
4126    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4127    pub retry_after_secs: Option<u64>,
4128    /// Human-readable text for the error envelope's `message` field.
4129    /// Absent: "route under maintenance". Validation rejects an
4130    /// empty/whitespace string (omit the field for the default).
4131    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4132    pub message: Option<String>,
4133}
4134
4135/// Default `maintenance.retry_after_secs` (DW-041).
4136pub const DEFAULT_MAINTENANCE_RETRY_AFTER_SECS: u64 = 60;
4137
4138impl Maintenance {
4139    /// The `Retry-After` seconds to send (the configured value or the
4140    /// default; validation has rejected 0, so this is always >= 1).
4141    pub fn retry_after(&self) -> u64 {
4142        self.retry_after_secs
4143            .unwrap_or(DEFAULT_MAINTENANCE_RETRY_AFTER_SECS)
4144            .max(1)
4145    }
4146
4147    /// The envelope `message` text (the configured value or the default).
4148    pub fn message(&self) -> &str {
4149        self.message.as_deref().unwrap_or("route under maintenance")
4150    }
4151}
4152
4153/// Route-level API deprecation policy (DW-048): automates the RFC
4154/// deprecation signal headers on every response of the route.
4155///
4156/// What the block emits (computed once at snapshot compile; see
4157/// [`CompiledDeprecation`] and `dataplane::versioning`):
4158///
4159/// - `since` (header emitted only when set): `Deprecation:
4160///   @<unix-seconds>` — the RFC 9745 structured-date form (RFC 9651
4161///   dates). RFC 9745 carries no URI inside the `Deprecation` field;
4162///   the human-readable notice travels in the `Link` below.
4163/// - `sunset` (optional): `Sunset: <HTTP-date>` verbatim — RFC 8594
4164///   requires an HTTP-date, and validation accepts exactly the
4165///   IMF-fixdate form generators must send (see `config::versioning`).
4166/// - `uri` (optional, requires `since`): `Link: <uri>;
4167///   rel="deprecation"` appended — the RFC 9745 companion link to the
4168///   migration documentation.
4169///
4170/// Semantics (frozen):
4171///
4172/// - Headers are stamped on the route's ACTION responses (proxy,
4173///   redirect, respond) in the response decoration tail — after
4174///   compression wrapping (the codec only rewrites `Content-Length`,
4175///   `Content-Encoding`, and `Vary`, so these headers pass through
4176///   verbatim) and beside the CORS headers (independent families).
4177///   Gateway-generated short-circuits on the route (413/431 limit
4178///   rejections, CORS preflights, authn/authz/rate-limit rejections,
4179///   503 sheds) do NOT carry them: those responses describe the
4180///   gateway's opinion of the REQUEST, not the route's lifecycle.
4181///   (The eager HMAC body-digest 401 is an action-path response and
4182///   DOES carry them.) Unrouted traffic (404) never matches the route
4183///   at all.
4184/// - The gateway is the source of truth for the headers it is configured
4185///   to emit: an upstream-sent `Deprecation`/`Sunset` on a route WITH a
4186///   `deprecation` block is replaced; on a route WITHOUT one, upstream
4187///   values pass through untouched. `Link` is appended (a list header —
4188///   upstream links survive).
4189/// - Validation rejects: a block with neither `since` nor `sunset` (no
4190///   effect — omit it), dates that are not IMF-fixdate, a `sunset`
4191///   already in the past (advertising a removal that already happened;
4192///   remove the route or extend the date — this also stops a long-lived
4193///   generation from silently re-publishing a stale sunset), a `sunset`
4194///   before `since` (removed before deprecated), and a `uri` without
4195///   `since` (the link documents the `Deprecation` header).
4196#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
4197#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
4198pub struct Deprecation {
4199    /// When this API version was (or will be) deprecated, as an
4200    /// IMF-fixdate HTTP-date (`Sun, 06 Nov 1994 08:49:37 GMT`). Emits
4201    /// `Deprecation: @<unix-seconds>` (RFC 9745). A past date is normal
4202    /// (the deprecation is in effect); a date before 1970 cannot form
4203    /// the structured date and is rejected.
4204    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4205    pub since: Option<String>,
4206    /// When this API version is expected to stop working, as an
4207    /// IMF-fixdate HTTP-date. Emits `Sunset` (RFC 8594) verbatim.
4208    /// Validation rejects a date already in the past and a date before
4209    /// `since`.
4210    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4211    pub sunset: Option<String>,
4212    /// URI of the deprecation/migration notice (absolute `http(s)` URL).
4213    /// Requires `since` — the RFC 9745 `Link; rel="deprecation"` it
4214    /// emits documents the `Deprecation` header.
4215    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4216    pub uri: Option<String>,
4217}
4218
4219/// Snapshot-compiled form of a [`Deprecation`] block (DW-048): the
4220/// emitted header VALUES, precomputed at snapshot-compile time — the
4221/// HTTP-date strings are parsed once here (never per response) and the
4222/// RFC 9745 structured date is rendered from the parsed seconds. Lives
4223/// in `config` beside [`CompiledCorsOrigins`] for the same reason:
4224/// `snapshot` builds it into the route table and `dataplane::versioning`
4225/// consumes it. Validation has already rejected unparseable dates, so
4226/// compilation drops nothing on any publishable config; entries that
4227/// would fail are skipped exactly like the CORS matcher's fallback.
4228#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
4229pub struct CompiledDeprecation {
4230    deprecation: Option<String>,
4231    sunset: Option<String>,
4232    link: Option<String>,
4233}
4234
4235impl CompiledDeprecation {
4236    /// Compile a (validated) policy's header values.
4237    pub fn compile(dep: &Deprecation) -> Self {
4238        CompiledDeprecation {
4239            deprecation: dep
4240                .since
4241                .as_deref()
4242                .and_then(versioning::parse_http_date)
4243                .filter(|d| d.unix_seconds() >= 0)
4244                .map(|d| format!("@{}", d.unix_seconds())),
4245            sunset: dep
4246                .sunset
4247                .as_deref()
4248                .filter(|s| versioning::parse_http_date(s).is_some())
4249                .map(str::to_string),
4250            link: dep
4251                .uri
4252                .as_deref()
4253                .map(|uri| format!("<{uri}>; rel=\"deprecation\"")),
4254        }
4255    }
4256
4257    /// The `Deprecation` header value (`@<unix-seconds>`), if the policy
4258    /// sets `since`.
4259    pub fn deprecation_header(&self) -> Option<&str> {
4260        self.deprecation.as_deref()
4261    }
4262
4263    /// The `Sunset` header value (the validated config string, verbatim),
4264    /// if the policy sets `sunset`.
4265    pub fn sunset_header(&self) -> Option<&str> {
4266        self.sunset.as_deref()
4267    }
4268
4269    /// The `Link` header value (`<uri>; rel="deprecation"`), if the
4270    /// policy sets `uri`.
4271    pub fn link_header(&self) -> Option<&str> {
4272        self.link.as_deref()
4273    }
4274}
4275
4276/// Route-level authorization rules (DW-020, feature analysis 4.7).
4277///
4278/// Rule semantics (frozen here; evaluation lives in the `authz` module):
4279///
4280/// - `denied_consumers` / `denied_groups` beat `allowed_*` at the SAME
4281///   level: within one [`Authz`], a deny always wins a tie.
4282/// - `allowed_consumers`, when non-empty, is a closed set: the
4283///   authenticated consumer must be listed. Empty = any authenticated
4284///   consumer passes the consumer rule.
4285/// - `allowed_groups`, when non-empty, requires the consumer to be a
4286///   member of at least one listed group. Group membership comes from
4287///   the CONFIG consumer's `groups` field; store-only consumers
4288///   (DWARA_STATE_DB deployments whose consumer has no config entry)
4289///   have no groups and therefore never satisfy an `allowed_groups`
4290///   rule (documented limitation until the state store carries groups).
4291/// - `required_scopes`: every listed scope must appear in the JWT
4292///   `scope` claim. The claim may be a space-separated string
4293///   (`"read write"`, the OAuth convention) or a JSON array of strings
4294///   (`["read", "write"]`, joined to its space-separated form when the
4295///   identity's claims are captured in `authn`); non-JWT identities
4296///   (API key / Basic) carry no claims and never satisfy scope rules.
4297/// - `required_claims`: exact string equality on the stringified claim
4298///   value; a claim absent from the token fails the match. Only
4299///   string- and number-valued claims are captured on the identity
4300///   (see `authn`), so a claim that is a JSON `true`/`false`, `null`,
4301///   object, or nested structure can NEVER satisfy a `required_claims`
4302///   entry — there is no stringified form to compare. Comparisons are
4303///   CASE-SENSITIVE throughout: consumer names, groups, scopes, and
4304///   claim values must match byte-for-byte.
4305/// - `ip_acl`: evaluated against the EFFECTIVE client IP — the
4306///   `X-Forwarded-For`-resolved client when the direct peer is inside
4307///   `gateway.trusted_proxies` (DW-009 chain), otherwise the direct
4308///   peer. See [`IpAcl`].
4309///
4310/// Authentication implication: an [`Authz`] carrying ANY identity rule
4311/// (consumer/group/scope/claim) rejects anonymous requests with 401; an
4312/// `ip_acl`-only [`Authz`] is the one authorization shape that can
4313/// ADMIT anonymous traffic (from an IP the ACL allows). A denial of an
4314/// AUTHENTICATED request is 403 (forbidden), never 401.
4315#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
4316#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
4317pub struct Authz {
4318    /// Consumers allowed to call the route (empty = any authenticated).
4319    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
4320    pub allowed_consumers: Vec<String>,
4321    /// Consumers explicitly rejected, even when otherwise allowed.
4322    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
4323    pub denied_consumers: Vec<String>,
4324    /// Groups allowed to call the route (empty = no group constraint).
4325    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
4326    pub allowed_groups: Vec<String>,
4327    /// Groups explicitly rejected, even when otherwise allowed.
4328    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
4329    pub denied_groups: Vec<String>,
4330    /// JWT scopes (from the `scope` claim) every request must carry.
4331    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
4332    pub required_scopes: Vec<String>,
4333    /// Claims (name -> exact stringified value) every request must
4334    /// carry. A listed claim absent from the token's claims fails.
4335    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::collections::BTreeMap::is_empty")]
4336    pub required_claims: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, String>,
4337    /// IP allow/deny gate on the effective client IP.
4338    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4339    pub ip_acl: Option<IpAcl>,
4340    /// GeoIP gate on the effective client IP (DW-050): country/ASN
4341    /// allow and deny lists resolved through `gateway.geoip`'s
4342    /// database. An address the database does not resolve (private
4343    /// ranges, not-in-DB, no database loaded) is UNKNOWN and matches
4344    /// NEITHER list: deny-lists keep passing unknowns, allow-lists
4345    /// keep rejecting them. Requires a `gateway.geoip` block —
4346    /// validation rejects the predicate without one. See
4347    /// [`GeoipRules`].
4348    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4349    pub geoip: Option<GeoipRules>,
4350    /// Monitor mode (DW-041): evaluate this block's rules, LOG and count
4351    /// every would-be denial (the `dwara_policy_dry_run_total{phase=
4352    /// "authz"}` counter plus a `dwara::policy` warn event), but let the
4353    /// request PROCEED as if allowed. The flag is per ATTACHMENT and
4354    /// mutes only this block's OWN denials: a LIVE deny at any other
4355    /// level of the chain still enforces (dry run never makes
4356    /// enforcement more permissive — the resolver walks past a dry deny
4357    /// and stops only at a live one). Route `auth_required` is an
4358    /// authentication-phase check, not an authorization rule, and is
4359    /// never muted by this flag.
4360    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
4361    pub dry_run: bool,
4362}
4363
4364/// IP access control on the effective client IP (DW-020, feature
4365/// analysis 4.15). Entries are IP addresses (e.g. `10.1.2.3`) or CIDRs
4366/// (e.g. `10.0.0.0/8`); anything else fails config validation (the same
4367/// parser as `gateway.trusted_proxies`). Evaluation order: the `deny`
4368/// list first (a match rejects with 403 regardless of the allow list),
4369/// then the `allow` list, then `default` for IPs matched by neither.
4370///
4371/// A `/0` (all-addresses) entry such as `0.0.0.0/0` or `::/0` is
4372/// REJECTED by validation in the `allow` list: an allow-all entry
4373/// filters nothing and is always a mistake — the intended shape is an
4374/// empty allow list with `default: allow` (the ACL's default mode).
4375/// A `/0` in the `deny` list is meaningful (deny-all) and is accepted.
4376#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
4377#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
4378pub struct IpAcl {
4379    /// CIDRs/IPs allowed through the gate.
4380    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
4381    pub allow: Vec<String>,
4382    /// CIDRs/IPs rejected; a deny match wins over any allow match.
4383    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
4384    pub deny: Vec<String>,
4385    /// What happens to an IP matched by NEITHER list: `allow` (the
4386    /// default — the lists are exceptions) or `deny` (closed mode — only
4387    /// allow-listed IPs pass).
4388    #[serde(
4389        default = "default_ip_acl_default",
4390        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_ip_acl_default"
4391    )]
4392    pub default: IpAclDefault,
4393}
4394
4395/// Fallback decision of an [`IpAcl`] for IPs matched by neither list.
4396#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
4397#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
4398pub enum IpAclDefault {
4399    /// Unmatched IPs pass the IP gate (the default).
4400    Allow,
4401    /// Unmatched IPs are rejected: only the allow list passes.
4402    Deny,
4403}
4404
4405fn default_ip_acl_default() -> IpAclDefault {
4406    IpAclDefault::Allow
4407}
4408
4409fn is_default_ip_acl_default(d: &IpAclDefault) -> bool {
4410    *d == IpAclDefault::Allow
4411}
4412
4413/// Route-scoped CORS policy (DW-027, feature analysis 4.14).
4414///
4415/// Semantics (frozen; evaluation lives in `dataplane::cors`):
4416///
4417/// - `allowed_origins` is a closed set of exact origins
4418///   (`https://api.example.com`, scheme + host + optional port, compared
4419///   in normalized form: lowercase scheme/host, default port dropped) or
4420///   the single entry `*` (any origin). It must be non-empty; `*`
4421///   combined with `allow_credentials: true` is rejected by validation
4422///   (the Fetch spec forbids wildcard-credentialed responses).
4423/// - A PREFLIGHT (`OPTIONS` + `Origin` + `Access-Control-Request-Method`)
4424///   on a CORS-configured route is answered 204 by the gateway —
4425///   validated against the policy, never forwarded upstream, and never
4426///   subject to authn/authz/rate limiting (browsers send preflights
4427///   without credentials). A preflight that fails validation (origin,
4428///   method, or requested headers not allowed) still short-circuits:
4429///   204 with NO CORS headers, which the browser reads as a failed
4430///   preflight. A plain `OPTIONS` without the preflight markers is NOT
4431///   intercepted; it proxies normally.
4432/// - ACTUAL responses on the route carry `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`
4433///   (`*`, or the echoed request origin when the list is specific),
4434///   `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true` when configured,
4435///   `Access-Control-Expose-Headers` when configured, and `Vary: Origin`
4436///   (merged into any existing `Vary`). A request whose `Origin` is not
4437///   allowed simply gets no CORS headers — the response passes through
4438///   as-is (same-origin reads do not consult CORS at all).
4439#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
4440#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
4441pub struct Cors {
4442    /// Origins allowed to call this route cross-origin: exact origin
4443    /// strings (`https://api.example.com`, `http://localhost:8080`) or
4444    /// `*` (any origin; never together with `allow_credentials`).
4445    pub allowed_origins: Vec<String>,
4446    /// Methods allowed for cross-origin requests (preflight echo and
4447    /// actual-request check). Default: the common API set
4448    /// GET/POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE/HEAD/OPTIONS. Each entry must be a
4449    /// valid HTTP method token; comparison is case-insensitive.
4450    #[serde(default = "default_cors_methods")]
4451    pub allowed_methods: Vec<String>,
4452    /// Request headers clients may send: exact header names, or `*` to
4453    /// allow any requested header (never `*` together with
4454    /// `allow_credentials`). Validated against the preflight's
4455    /// `Access-Control-Request-Headers` list; empty means no headers
4456    /// beyond the CORS-safelisted set are permitted in preflight checks.
4457    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
4458    pub allowed_headers: Vec<String>,
4459    /// Response headers exposed to the browser (readable by cross-origin
4460    /// client script). Absent: only the CORS-safelisted response headers
4461    /// are exposed.
4462    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
4463    pub expose_headers: Vec<String>,
4464    /// Allow credentialed cross-origin requests (cookies, TLS client
4465    /// certificates). Default false. When true, the origin list must be
4466    /// specific (`*` is rejected by validation) and responses echo the
4467    /// request origin rather than sending `*`.
4468    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
4469    pub allow_credentials: bool,
4470    /// `Access-Control-Max-Age` for preflight responses, in seconds (how
4471    /// long the browser may cache the preflight result). Absent: the
4472    /// header is omitted (each preflight hits the gateway).
4473    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4474    pub max_age_secs: Option<u64>,
4475}
4476
4477fn default_cors_methods() -> Vec<String> {
4478    ["GET", "POST", "PUT", "PATCH", "DELETE", "HEAD", "OPTIONS"]
4479        .iter()
4480        .map(|s| s.to_string())
4481        .collect()
4482}
4483
4484/// Canonical form of one origin (DW-027): lowercase scheme and host,
4485/// default port dropped, no userinfo, no path/query — the exact string
4486/// the runtime CORS matcher compares request `Origin` values against
4487/// and validation checks config entries with. `None` when the input is
4488/// not a well-formed `http(s)` origin. Lives in `config` (the lowest
4489/// consuming domain) so validation (`snapshot::validate`) and matching
4490/// (`dataplane::cors`) share one grammar, the same way `net.rs` owns the
4491/// IP/CIDR grammar.
4492pub fn normalize_origin(origin: &str) -> Option<String> {
4493    let uri: hyper::Uri = origin.parse().ok()?;
4494    let scheme = uri.scheme()?;
4495    if !matches!(scheme.as_str(), "http" | "https") {
4496        return None;
4497    }
4498    // Browsers never serialize userinfo into an Origin (the grammar is
4499    // scheme "://" host [":" port]); authority userinfo is a config
4500    // authoring error or a smuggle attempt, never a real origin.
4501    if uri.authority().is_some_and(|a| a.as_str().contains('@')) {
4502        return None;
4503    }
4504    let host = uri.host()?;
4505    let default_port = match scheme.as_str() {
4506        "http" => 80,
4507        _ => 443,
4508    };
4509    let port = uri
4510        .port_u16()
4511        .and_then(|p| (p != default_port).then_some(p));
4512    // An origin carries no path, query, or fragment; tolerate only the
4513    // empty path "/" (browsers serialize the origin WITHOUT it, but
4514    // configs hand-writing "https://host/" are unambiguous).
4515    match uri.path_and_query() {
4516        None => {}
4517        Some(pq) if pq.as_str() == "/" => {}
4518        _ => return None,
4519    }
4520    Some(match port {
4521        Some(p) => format!("{}://{}:{}", scheme.as_str(), host.to_lowercase(), p),
4522        None => format!("{}://{}", scheme.as_str(), host.to_lowercase()),
4523    })
4524}
4525
4526/// Snapshot-compiled form of a `cors.allowed_origins` list (DW-027): the
4527/// wildcard flag plus the normalized form of every specific entry,
4528/// computed once at snapshot-compile time so the request path never
4529/// re-parses config strings (per-request work is the request's own
4530/// origin only). Lives in `config` beside [`normalize_origin`] because
4531/// the compiled set is the grammar's runtime consumer: `snapshot`
4532/// builds it into the route table (`snapshot::RouteTable`) and
4533/// `dataplane::cors` matches against it. Validation has already
4534/// rejected entries that do not normalize, so compilation drops
4535/// nothing on any publishable config; entries that would fail
4536/// normalization are skipped exactly as the pre-compiled matcher was.
4537#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
4538pub struct CompiledCorsOrigins {
4539    wildcard: bool,
4540    origins: Vec<String>,
4541}
4542
4543impl CompiledCorsOrigins {
4544    /// Compile a (validated) policy's origin list.
4545    pub fn compile(cors: &Cors) -> Self {
4546        CompiledCorsOrigins {
4547            wildcard: cors.allowed_origins.iter().any(|o| o == "*"),
4548            origins: cors
4549                .allowed_origins
4550                .iter()
4551                .filter(|o| *o != "*")
4552                .filter_map(|o| normalize_origin(o))
4553                .collect(),
4554        }
4555    }
4556
4557    /// Does a request `Origin` value match this set? `*` matches any
4558    /// origin string; otherwise normalized exact match (the same
4559    /// comparison the pre-compiled matcher ran, without re-normalizing
4560    /// the config side per request).
4561    pub fn allows(&self, origin: &str) -> bool {
4562        if self.wildcard {
4563            return true;
4564        }
4565        let Some(request_origin) = normalize_origin(origin) else {
4566            return false;
4567        };
4568        self.origins.iter().any(|o| o == &request_origin)
4569    }
4570
4571    /// Was the list the single entry `*`?
4572    pub fn wildcard(&self) -> bool {
4573        self.wildcard
4574    }
4575}
4576
4577/// Route-scoped response compression policy (DW-027, feature analysis
4578/// 4.13).
4579///
4580/// Semantics (frozen; negotiation and encoding live in
4581/// `dataplane::compression`):
4582///
4583/// - `algorithms` is the PREFERENCE order: the first entry the request's
4584///   `Accept-Encoding` accepts wins. `q=0` entries in `Accept-Encoding`
4585///   are treated as refusal.
4586/// - `level` is clamped per algorithm at encode time (gzip 0-9, brotli
4587///   0-11, zstd 0-22; validation bounds the field to 0-22). Absent: the
4588///   algorithm's library default.
4589/// - `min_size` skips compression of responses whose size is known and
4590///   below it — a declared `Content-Length` or the exact size of the
4591///   gateway's own respond/redirect bodies. Responses of UNKNOWN length
4592///   (streaming) are always candidates.
4593/// - `content_types` (when non-empty) restricts compression to responses
4594///   whose `Content-Type` starts with one of the entries; entries are
4595///   lowercase prefix matches (e.g. `text/`). `excluded_content_types`
4596///   is checked after the include list and wins (the way to express
4597///   "compress everything text/ except `text/event-stream`").
4598/// - Responses carrying an existing `Content-Encoding`, 1xx/204/304
4599///   statuses, zero-length bodies, and 101 upgrade responses are never
4600///   compressed. `Vary: Accept-Encoding` is merged into every response
4601///   on a compression-configured route that is not already encoded —
4602///   compressed or not — so caches key correctly.
4603/// - The compressed body streams chunk-by-chunk with a flush per chunk
4604///   (streaming/SSE-friendly, at a small compression-ratio cost); the
4605///   gateway never buffers the whole body to compress it. The frame
4606///   codec's internal buffer is bounded by the chunk size.
4607#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
4608#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
4609pub struct Compression {
4610    /// Enabled algorithms in preference order (default gzip, brotli,
4611    /// zstd). Must be non-empty and duplicate-free (validation).
4612    #[serde(default = "default_compression_algorithms")]
4613    pub algorithms: Vec<CompressionAlgorithm>,
4614    /// Compression level; clamped per algorithm (gzip <= 9, brotli <= 11,
4615    /// zstd <= 22). Absent: library default per algorithm.
4616    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4617    pub level: Option<u8>,
4618    /// Minimum response size (bytes) to compress, applied whenever the
4619    /// size is known: a declared `Content-Length` or the body's exact
4620    /// size (the gateway's own respond/redirect bodies, which carry no
4621    /// `Content-Length`). Below it the response passes through (still
4622    /// carrying `Vary: Accept-Encoding`). Default 1024. Responses of
4623    /// unknown length (streaming) are always candidates.
4624    #[serde(default = "default_compression_min_size")]
4625    pub min_size: u64,
4626    /// Content-Type prefix include list (lowercase, e.g. `text/`,
4627    /// `application/json`); empty = every content type is a candidate.
4628    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
4629    pub content_types: Vec<String>,
4630    /// Content-Type prefix exclude list, checked after `content_types`;
4631    /// a match passes the response through uncompressed.
4632    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
4633    pub excluded_content_types: Vec<String>,
4634}
4635
4636fn default_compression_algorithms() -> Vec<CompressionAlgorithm> {
4637    vec![
4638        CompressionAlgorithm::Gzip,
4639        CompressionAlgorithm::Brotli,
4640        CompressionAlgorithm::Zstd,
4641    ]
4642}
4643
4644fn default_compression_min_size() -> u64 {
4645    1024
4646}
4647
4648/// One response compression algorithm (DW-027).
4649#[derive(
4650    Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema,
4651)]
4652#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
4653pub enum CompressionAlgorithm {
4654    Gzip,
4655    Brotli,
4656    Zstd,
4657}
4658
4659impl CompressionAlgorithm {
4660    /// The `Accept-Encoding` / `Content-Encoding` token of the algorithm.
4661    pub fn encoding_token(self) -> &'static str {
4662        match self {
4663            CompressionAlgorithm::Gzip => "gzip",
4664            CompressionAlgorithm::Brotli => "br",
4665            CompressionAlgorithm::Zstd => "zstd",
4666        }
4667    }
4668}
4669
4670/// Snapshot-compiled form of a compression policy's content-type prefix
4671/// lists (DW-027): every include/exclude entry trimmed and lowercased
4672/// once at snapshot-compile time; the request path compares the
4673/// response's (lowercased) media type against the precomputed prefixes
4674/// instead of re-normalizing config strings per response. Same
4675/// placement rationale as [`CompiledCorsOrigins`].
4676#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
4677pub struct CompiledContentTypeFilter {
4678    include: Vec<String>,
4679    exclude: Vec<String>,
4680}
4681
4682impl CompiledContentTypeFilter {
4683    /// Compile a (validated) policy's prefix lists.
4684    pub fn compile(policy: &Compression) -> Self {
4685        fn normalize_list(list: &[String]) -> Vec<String> {
4686            list.iter().map(|p| p.trim().to_lowercase()).collect()
4687        }
4688        CompiledContentTypeFilter {
4689            include: normalize_list(&policy.content_types),
4690            exclude: normalize_list(&policy.excluded_content_types),
4691        }
4692    }
4693
4694    /// Is `media_type` (lowercase, parameters stripped) a compression
4695    /// candidate? An empty include list admits every type; the exclude
4696    /// list is checked after it and wins.
4697    pub fn allows(&self, media_type: &str) -> bool {
4698        (self.include.is_empty() || self.include.iter().any(|p| media_type.starts_with(p)))
4699            && !self.exclude.iter().any(|p| media_type.starts_with(p))
4700    }
4701}
4702
4703/// Route-scoped request size limits (DW-027, feature analysis 4.16).
4704///
4705/// Semantics (frozen; enforcement lives in `dataplane::hardening`):
4706///
4707/// - Header limits (`max_header_count`, `max_header_bytes`) are checked
4708///   immediately after route resolution, before CORS preflight handling
4709///   and authentication; a violation answers 431 with the JSON error
4710///   envelope. `max_header_bytes` is the SUM of all header name and
4711///   value bytes of the request (hyper's per-connection buffer limits
4712///   still apply beneath this, process-wide).
4713/// - `max_body_bytes`: a request whose `Content-Length` declares more is
4714///   rejected 413 before any upstream contact. A body of UNKNOWN length
4715///   (chunked / h2 without content-length) is wrapped in a counting
4716///   guard: crossing the cap aborts the request (the upstream attempt
4717///   fails; the client receives 413 when the failure precedes the
4718///   upstream's response headers, a torn stream otherwise).
4719/// - A block with NO field set is rejected by validation (always an
4720///   authoring mistake; omit the block).
4721#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
4722#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
4723pub struct RequestLimits {
4724    /// Maximum request body size in bytes (declared via `Content-Length`
4725    /// or enforced streaming). Absent: unlimited.
4726    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4727    pub max_body_bytes: Option<u64>,
4728    /// Maximum number of header fields on the request. Absent:
4729    /// unlimited (hyper's parser bound applies).
4730    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4731    pub max_header_count: Option<u32>,
4732    /// Maximum total size of request headers (names + values, bytes).
4733    /// Absent: unlimited.
4734    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4735    pub max_header_bytes: Option<u64>,
4736    /// Monitor mode (DW-041): evaluate the limits, LOG and count every
4737    /// would-be rejection (`dwara_policy_dry_run_total{phase="route_
4738    /// limits"}` + a `dwara::policy` warn event), but let the request
4739    /// PROCEED — including its body: the streaming `max_body_bytes` guard
4740    /// is not armed, so a chunked body that would have been aborted mid-
4741    /// stream streams through (only the cheap up-front checks — header
4742    /// count/bytes, a declared `Content-Length` — are observable in dry
4743    /// run). The staging tool for turning limits on against live traffic
4744    /// without breaking clients.
4745    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
4746    pub dry_run: bool,
4747}
4748
4749/// Matching rules for incoming requests.
4750#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
4751#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
4752pub struct RouteMatch {
4753    pub path: PathMatch,
4754    /// Exact host match (e.g. `api.example.com`).
4755    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4756    pub host: Option<String>,
4757    /// Allowed HTTP methods; empty means all methods.
4758    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
4759    pub methods: Vec<String>,
4760    /// Exact header matches.
4761    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::collections::BTreeMap::is_empty")]
4762    pub headers: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, String>,
4763    /// Query-parameter matches; every entry must match (AND). Name-only
4764    /// entries match on presence; a `value` requires an exact raw match (no
4765    /// percent-decoding in v1).
4766    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
4767    pub query: Vec<NameValueMatch>,
4768    /// Cookie matches (parsed from the `Cookie` header); every entry must
4769    /// match (AND). Name-only entries match on presence; a `value` requires
4770    /// an exact match (no cookie-unquoting in v1).
4771    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
4772    pub cookies: Vec<NameValueMatch>,
4773    /// Media-type version selection (DW-048): a bare `type/subtype` the
4774    /// request's `Accept` header must name explicitly, e.g.
4775    /// `application/vnd.acme.v2+json`. A comma-separated Accept list
4776    /// matches on ANY entry; parameters and q-values on the request side
4777    /// are ignored; wildcard entries (`*/*`, `type/*`) and a missing
4778    /// Accept header never match — version selection requires the client
4779    /// to NAME the version, so unconstrained clients fall through to the
4780    /// route without this criterion (the unversioned default). Like every
4781    /// non-path criterion this is AND-ed and applied AFTER path
4782    /// resolution: a miss does not fall through to another route (404),
4783    /// and two routes cannot share one path to offer multiple versions —
4784    /// version families use distinct paths (`/v1/`, `/v2/`) or this
4785    /// criterion on a versioned path. Validation rejects anything but a
4786    /// bare lowercase-able `type/subtype` (`config::versioning` owns the
4787    /// grammar). Responses of a matched route carry `Vary: Accept`
4788    /// (merged), so shared caches key on the negotiated representation.
4789    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4790    pub accept: Option<String>,
4791}
4792
4793/// One query-parameter or cookie criterion: the parameter/cookie must be
4794/// present; when `value` is given it must equal that exact string.
4795#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
4796#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
4797pub struct NameValueMatch {
4798    pub name: String,
4799    /// Exact value required; `None` means "present is enough".
4800    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4801    pub value: Option<String>,
4802}
4803
4804/// How a route's path pattern is interpreted.
4805#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
4806#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
4807pub struct PathMatch {
4808    #[serde(rename = "type")]
4809    pub kind: PathMatchKind,
4810    /// The pattern value, e.g. `/v1/users` or `/v1/.*`.
4811    pub value: String,
4812}
4813
4814#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
4815#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
4816pub enum PathMatchKind {
4817    Exact,
4818    Prefix,
4819    Regex,
4820}
4821
4822/// What the gateway does when a route matches.
4823#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
4824#[serde(tag = "type", rename_all = "snake_case", deny_unknown_fields)]
4825pub enum RouteAction {
4826    /// Forward to the route's service (its upstream).
4827    ///
4828    /// `rewrite` (at most ONE per action in v1) is applied to the inbound
4829    /// path before the request is sent upstream; the query string is always
4830    /// preserved verbatim.
4831    Proxy {
4832        #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4833        rewrite: Option<PathRewrite>,
4834    },
4835    Redirect {
4836        #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4837        scheme: Option<String>,
4838        #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4839        host: Option<String>,
4840        #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4841        path: Option<String>,
4842        status: u16,
4843    },
4844    Respond {
4845        status: u16,
4846        #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
4847        body: Option<String>,
4848        /// Extra response headers (name -> value), emitted verbatim.
4849        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::collections::BTreeMap::is_empty")]
4850        headers: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, String>,
4851    },
4852    /// Serve a canned response without contacting any upstream (DW-047
4853    /// mock mode). The route's `service` is still required by the
4854    /// schema (the frozen vocabulary), but a mock route never dials
4855    /// it — the whole point is serving without a backend. Pair with
4856    /// `request_validation` to mock a write endpoint that still
4857    /// rejects malformed bodies. See [`MockAction`].
4858    Mock {
4859        #[serde(flatten)]
4860        mock: MockAction,
4861    },
4862    /// AI provider translation (DW-075): the request body is parsed as
4863    /// an OpenAI chat-completions request, its `model` resolved through
4864    /// the gateway's `ai:` block to a provider, translated to that
4865    /// provider's wire format (OpenAI/Anthropic/Gemini), sent through
4866    /// the provider's upstream, and the response translated back to the
4867    /// OpenAI shape. The route's `service` is still required by the
4868    /// schema but never dialed. Requires an `ai:` block (validation
4869    /// rejects the pairing otherwise). `stream: true` requests are
4870    /// served via zero-buffer SSE pass-through (DW-077).
4871    ///
4872    /// AI-02: the `endpoint` field selects which AI endpoint the route
4873    /// serves. The default is `chat` (the existing behavior). Other
4874    /// endpoints (`embeddings`, `images`, `audio`, `moderation`) are
4875    /// proxied as a passthrough: the request body is forwarded to the
4876    /// provider's upstream as-is (no adapter translation), and the
4877    /// response is returned as-is. Model governance (allowlist check)
4878    /// still applies using the `model` field from the request body.
4879    Ai {
4880        #[serde(default)]
4881        endpoint: AiEndpoint,
4882        /// AI-05 (#195): The client-facing request dialect. Defaults
4883        /// to `openai` (the historical behavior). When set to
4884        /// `anthropic` or `gemini`, the gateway parses the incoming
4885        /// request using that dialect's native format and translates
4886        /// to the canonical `ChatRequest`. When set to `passthrough`,
4887        /// the request body is forwarded to the provider as-is (no
4888        /// translation, still metered/governed).
4889        #[serde(default)]
4890        dialect: AiIngressDialect,
4891    },
4892    /// WASM route handler (nano-service, DW-106): instead of proxying
4893    /// to an upstream, the route action runs a WASM module that
4894    /// generates the response directly. The module implements a simple
4895    /// request->response handler ABI over the existing wasmtime runtime
4896    /// (compiled into every build; no cargo feature). The route's
4897    /// `service` is still
4898    /// required by the schema (the frozen vocabulary) but never dialed
4899    /// -- the whole point is serving without a backend. See
4900    /// [`NanoServiceAction`].
4901    NanoService {
4902        #[serde(flatten)]
4903        nano: NanoServiceAction,
4904    },
4905}
4906
4907/// AI-05 (#195): The client-facing request dialect for an AI route.
4908/// Determines how the gateway parses the incoming request body.
4909#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
4910#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
4911pub enum AiIngressDialect {
4912    /// OpenAI chat-completions shape (the historical default). The
4913    /// request is parsed by `openai_compat::parse_chat_request`.
4914    #[default]
4915    Openai,
4916    /// Anthropic Messages API shape. The request is parsed by
4917    /// `ingress::parse_anthropic_request` and translated to the
4918    /// canonical `ChatRequest`.
4919    Anthropic,
4920    /// Gemini generateContent shape. The request is parsed by
4921    /// `ingress::parse_gemini_request` and translated to the
4922    /// canonical `ChatRequest`.
4923    Gemini,
4924    /// Passthrough mode: the request body is forwarded to the
4925    /// provider as-is (no translation, still metered/governed).
4926    /// The response is returned as-is.
4927    Passthrough,
4928}
4929
4930/// AI-02: which AI endpoint a route serves. The `chat` endpoint (the
4931/// default) uses the full adapter translation pipeline (DW-075). The
4932/// other endpoints are proxied as a passthrough: the request body is
4933/// forwarded to the provider's upstream as-is, and the response is
4934/// returned as-is. Model governance (allowlist check) still applies
4935/// using the `model` field from the request body.
4936#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
4937#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
4938pub enum AiEndpoint {
4939    /// OpenAI chat-completions (`POST /v1/chat/completions`). The
4940    /// full adapter translation pipeline applies (DW-075). This is the
4941    /// default.
4942    #[default]
4943    Chat,
4944    /// OpenAI embeddings (`POST /v1/embeddings`). Proxied as a
4945    /// passthrough; the request body `{model, input}` is forwarded
4946    /// as-is. Token usage from the response is recorded for budget
4947    /// accounting.
4948    Embeddings,
4949    /// OpenAI images (`POST /v1/images/generations`). Proxied as a
4950    /// passthrough; no token usage (image generation is priced per
4951    /// image, not per token).
4952    Images,
4953    /// OpenAI audio (TTS `POST /v1/audio/speech`, transcription
4954    /// `POST /v1/audio/transcriptions`). Proxied as a passthrough.
4955    Audio,
4956    /// OpenAI moderation (`POST /v1/moderations`). Proxied as a
4957    /// passthrough.
4958    Moderation,
4959    /// AI-04 (#194): OpenAI Batch API (`POST /v1/batches`). Proxied
4960    /// as a passthrough for batch creation/status/cancel. When batch
4961    /// results are retrieved (`GET /v1/batches/{id}/output`), the
4962    /// JSONL result file is parsed and each line's `usage` is metered
4963    /// at the batch (50% discount) rate.
4964    Batch,
4965}

4967/// A WASM route handler (nano-service) action (DW-106,
4968/// `routes[].action` with `type: nano_service`). The gateway loads the
4969/// named WASM module, calls its `handle` export with the serialized
4970/// request (method, path, headers, body), and returns the response the
4971/// module produces (status, headers, body). No upstream is contacted.
4972///
4973/// `module` is the filesystem path to the `.wasm` module (checked for
4974/// existence and readability at config publish time). `memory_limit` is
4975/// the maximum linear memory in bytes the module may allocate (must be
4976/// positive and at most 64 MiB). `execution_timeout_ms` is the maximum
4977/// wall-clock time the `handle` call may run (must be positive and at
4978/// most 5000ms); a module that exceeds it is interrupted and the route
4979/// answers 504.
4980#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
4981#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
4982pub struct NanoServiceAction {
4983    /// Filesystem path to the `.wasm` module. Must exist and be
4984    /// readable at config publish time.
4985    pub module: String,
4986    /// Maximum linear memory the module may allocate, in bytes. Must be
4987    /// > 0 and <= 67108864 (64 MiB). Default: 1048576 (1 MiB).
4988    #[serde(default = "default_nano_memory_limit")]
4989    pub memory_limit: usize,
4990    /// Maximum wall-clock execution time for the `handle` call, in
4991    /// milliseconds. Must be > 0 and <= 5000. Default: 100.
4992    #[serde(default = "default_nano_execution_timeout_ms")]
4993    pub execution_timeout_ms: u64,
4994}
4995
4996fn default_nano_memory_limit() -> usize {
4997    1_048_576
4998}
4999
5000fn default_nano_execution_timeout_ms() -> u64 {
5001    100
5002}

5004/// A canned response served without any upstream contact (DW-047,
5005/// `routes[].action.mock`). The body comes from either `body` (an
5006/// inline string) or `body_file` (a path relative to the config file
5007/// directory); exactly one of the two may be set, or neither for an
5008/// empty body. `delay_ms` simulates latency (useful for testing
5009/// timeouts and retries against a mock). Validation rejects a status
5010/// outside 100..=599, a `delay_ms` above 30000, and setting both
5011/// `body` and `body_file`.
5012#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5013#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
5014pub struct MockAction {
5015    /// HTTP status code (100..=599).
5016    pub status: u16,
5017    /// Extra response headers (name -> value), emitted verbatim. When
5018    /// `content-type` is not set here, the gateway defaults it to
5019    /// `application/json` if the body parses as JSON, else `text/plain`.
5020    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::collections::BTreeMap::is_empty")]
5021    pub headers: std::collections::BTreeMap<String, String>,
5022    /// Inline response body. Mutually exclusive with `body_file`.
5023    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5024    pub body: Option<String>,
5025    /// Path to a file whose contents are served as the body, read at
5026    /// config publish time (the bytes are held in the snapshot). The
5027    /// path is relative to the config file directory. Mutually
5028    /// exclusive with `body`.
5029    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5030    pub body_file: Option<String>,
5031    /// Simulated latency in milliseconds (0..=30000). The response is
5032    /// delayed by this long before the status/headers/body are sent.
5033    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5034    pub delay_ms: Option<u32>,
5035}
