5037/// Path rewrite applied before proxying (DW-010). Exactly one variant per
5038/// proxy action; there is no rewrite chaining in v1. All variants operate
5039/// on the path component only — the inbound query string is re-attached
5040/// untouched.
5041#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5042#[serde(tag = "type", rename_all = "snake_case", deny_unknown_fields)]
5043pub enum PathRewrite {
5044    /// Strip the route's matched prefix (the `match.path.value` with
5045    /// trailing slashes trimmed) from the front of the request path.
5046    /// Meaningful for prefix-kind routes; for other kinds it strips the
5047    /// pattern's byte length when the path starts with the pattern value
5048    /// and is a no-op otherwise. If nothing remains (or the remainder
5049    /// lacks a leading `/`), the result is normalized to `/rest`.
5050    StripPrefix {},
5051    /// Replace a literal prefix: when the request path starts with
5052    /// `prefix`, that prefix is replaced by `replacement`; otherwise this
5053    /// rewrite is a no-op (the path is forwarded unchanged).
5054    ReplacePrefix { prefix: String, replacement: String },
5055    /// Replace the FIRST regex match on the request path with
5056    /// `substitution`. Substitution references: `$1`..`$9` / `${n}` for
5057    /// capture groups of `pattern`; `$name` / `${name}` for named capture
5058    /// groups of `pattern`, falling back to path parameters captured by
5059    /// the route's `{param}` template. Unknown references expand to the
5060    /// empty string. The pattern must compile — checked at config compile
5061    /// time, never at request time.
5062    Regex {
5063        pattern: String,
5064        substitution: String,
5065    },
5066}
5067
5068/// The logical API being exposed: base path, version, default policies;
5069/// targets exactly one upstream, or a weighted split of upstreams
5070/// since DW-040 (canary / blue-green).
5071#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5072#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
5073pub struct Service {
5074    pub name: String,
5075    /// Name of the upstream this service targets. Exactly one of
5076    /// `upstream` and [`Service::split`] must be set: a single-target
5077    /// service names its pool here; a canary/blue-green service uses
5078    /// `split` instead and leaves this unset. Optional since DW-040
5079    /// (it was the only shape before).
5080    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5081    pub upstream: Option<String>,
5082    /// Weighted traffic split across upstreams (DW-040, canary and
5083    /// blue-green): requests for this service are dispatched to one of
5084    /// the named upstreams by a deterministic weighted pick (the
5085    /// sticky cookie's value when [`Service::sticky`] is configured
5086    /// and the request carries one — sessions stay on their branch;
5087    /// otherwise the request id — per-request distribution whose
5088    /// ratios converge on the weights statistically). A weight of 0
5089    /// parks an upstream in the split without serving traffic (the
5090    /// blue-green switch: flip the weights and re-publish — the next
5091    /// request dispatches by the new generation, no restart). Ramp
5092    /// rule: change weights by RE-BALANCING (keep the total constant,
5093    /// e.g. 95/5 -> 90/10) — growing one side alone changes the pick's
5094    /// modulus and reshuffles every session's branch.
5095    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5096    pub split: Option<ServiceSplit>,
5097    /// Cookie affinity (DW-040): when set, the gateway sets a cookie
5098    /// on responses for this service whose VALUE consistently selects
5099    /// the split branch (and, when the branch upstream runs the
5100    /// `ip_hash` balancer, the endpoint — the cookie value becomes the
5101    /// ring key, reusing the consistent-hash machinery). The value is
5102    /// an opaque affinity handle: not a secret, carrying no identity,
5103    /// and never trusted as one.
5104    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5105    pub sticky: Option<StickyAffinity>,
5106    /// Base path prefix the API is served under (e.g. `/v1`).
5107    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5108    pub base_path: Option<String>,
5109    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5110    pub version: Option<String>,
5111    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
5112    pub policies: Vec<String>,
5113    /// Service-level authorization rules (#123): applies to every request
5114    /// whose route targets this service. Link of the frozen chain
5115    /// consumer > route > service > listener > global (a deny at any
5116    /// level wins; otherwise the most specific level with rules governs —
5117    /// see [`Authz`] and the `authz` module). Same validation rules as
5118    /// route-level authorization.
5119    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5120    pub authorization: Option<Authz>,
5121}
5122
5123/// Weighted traffic split across upstreams (DW-040,
5124/// `services[].split`): canary releases and blue-green switches at the
5125/// SERVICE level — each target is a full upstream (its own endpoints,
5126/// protocol, health, and balancer), so a canary pool is isolated from
5127/// the stable one by construction. The pick is a deterministic
5128/// weighted hash (see [`Service::split`]): ratios hold statistically
5129/// per request and exactly per session when sticky affinity is on.
5130#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5131#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
5132pub struct ServiceSplit {
5133    /// The weighted targets. Validation enforces 2..=8 entries (one
5134    /// target is what `services[].upstream` expresses), existing
5135    /// upstream names, no duplicates, and a positive total weight (a
5136    /// total of 0 would route nowhere; individual zeros are the
5137    /// parked side of a blue-green pair).
5138    pub targets: Vec<SplitTarget>,
5139    /// Auto-canary analysis (DW-091): when set, a background
5140    /// controller compares the canary (second target) vs the baseline
5141    /// (first target) on error rate or latency and adjusts the split
5142    /// weights automatically. Only valid when the split has exactly 2
5143    /// targets (baseline + canary). Weight changes are transient
5144    /// (Generation swap) and revert on config reload.
5145    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5146    pub canary_analysis: Option<CanaryAnalysis>,
5147}
5148
5149/// One weighted target of a service split (DW-040).
5150#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5151#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
5152pub struct SplitTarget {
5153    /// Name of an upstream in this config.
5154    pub upstream: String,
5155    /// Relative share of requests (default 1). `0` parks the target
5156    /// (compiled, validated, serving nothing — the blue-green
5157    /// shape); validation caps the sum at a sane bound.
5158    #[serde(
5159        default = "default_split_weight",
5160        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_split_weight"
5161    )]
5162    pub weight: u32,
5163}
5164
5165fn default_split_weight() -> u32 {
5166    1
5167}
5168fn is_default_split_weight(w: &u32) -> bool {
5169    *w == 1
5170}
5171
5172/// Auto-canary analysis (DW-091): metrics-driven promotion/rollback of
5173/// canary split weights. A background controller compares the canary
5174/// side against the baseline on error rate or latency; on regression it
5175/// rolls the canary weight back (toward 0), on success it promotes
5176/// (toward the total weight). Severe regression (>2x the rollback
5177/// threshold) triggers an immediate rollback to 0. The total weight
5178/// stays constant: when the canary weight changes, the baseline
5179/// absorbs the difference, preserving the hash distribution for
5180/// existing sessions (the DW-040 invariant). Weight changes are
5181/// TRANSIENT (a Generation swap) and revert on config reload.
5182#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5183#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
5184pub struct CanaryAnalysis {
5185    /// Master switch. Default true: an analysis block is active unless
5186    /// explicitly disabled.
5187    #[serde(default = "default_true")]
5188    pub enabled: bool,
5189    /// Look-back window for metrics, in seconds. The controller
5190    /// evaluates canary health over the recent window of observations.
5191    pub window_seconds: u64,
5192    /// Weight delta per adjustment (in weight units). Promote:
5193    /// increase canary weight by `step`. Rollback: decrease by `step`.
5194    /// Severe regression (>2x the rollback threshold) rolls back to 0
5195    /// immediately, ignoring the step.
5196    pub step: u32,
5197    /// Minimum total requests (baseline + canary) before the
5198    /// controller acts. Prevents acting on insufficient data.
5199    pub min_requests: u64,
5200    /// Cooldown between adjustments, in seconds. Prevents rapid
5201    /// oscillation: after one adjustment, the next is suppressed until
5202    /// the cooldown elapses.
5203    pub cooldown_seconds: u64,
5204    /// The promotion rule: when the canary metric is BELOW this
5205    /// threshold (good), the controller promotes (increases canary
5206    /// weight by `step`).
5207    pub promote: CanaryRule,
5208    /// The rollback rule: when the canary metric is ABOVE this
5209    /// threshold (bad), the controller rolls back (decreases canary
5210    /// weight by `step`). When the canary metric exceeds 2x this
5211    /// threshold (severe regression), the controller rolls back to 0
5212    /// immediately.
5213    pub rollback: CanaryRule,
5214}
5215
5216/// One canary analysis rule (DW-091): a metric to compare and the
5217/// threshold that triggers the rule.
5218#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5219#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
5220pub struct CanaryRule {
5221    /// The metric to compare canary vs baseline.
5222    pub metric: CanaryMetric,
5223    /// The threshold. For `promote`: the canary metric must be BELOW
5224    /// this (good) to promote. For `rollback`: the canary metric must
5225    /// be ABOVE this (bad) to roll back.
5226    pub threshold: f64,
5227}
5228
5229/// The metric a canary rule compares (DW-091).
5230#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5231#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
5232pub enum CanaryMetric {
5233    /// HTTP error rate (status >= 500 / total requests).
5234    ErrorRate,
5235    /// P99 latency in milliseconds.
5236    LatencyP99,
5237    /// P95 latency in milliseconds.
5238    LatencyP95,
5239    /// P50 (median) latency in milliseconds.
5240    LatencyP50,
5241}

5243/// Cookie affinity for a service (DW-040, `services[].sticky`).
5244#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5245#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
5246pub struct StickyAffinity {
5247    /// The cookie name the gateway reads and sets. Must be a valid
5248    /// cookie-name token (RFC 6265: the RFC 2616 `token` grammar);
5249    /// validation rejects anything else.
5250    pub cookie: String,
5251    /// `Max-Age` of the set cookie, in seconds (default 3600 — one
5252    /// hour; validated to 1..=2_592_000, 30 days).
5253    #[serde(
5254        default = "default_sticky_ttl_s",
5255        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_sticky_ttl_s"
5256    )]
5257    pub ttl_s: u64,
5258}
5259
5260fn default_sticky_ttl_s() -> u64 {
5261    3_600

5267/// DNS-based dynamic upstream discovery (DW-042): when present on an
5268/// upstream, a background discovery task resolves `hostname` via DNS,
5269/// watches the record TTL, and re-resolves when the TTL expires (or at
5270/// `refresh_interval_s`, whichever comes first), updating the upstream's
5271/// endpoint set live — without a restart. The `endpoints` field becomes
5272/// the INITIAL/fallback set: used until the first resolution completes
5273/// and as a fallback when DNS fails and `fail_open` is true. When
5274/// absent, endpoints remain static (the current behavior — no change).
5275///
5276/// Only A and SRV records are supported. A records pair each resolved
5277/// address with `port`; SRV records carry their own port. Consul watch
5278/// and Kubernetes EndpointSlice watch are deferred to a future
5279/// milestone — DNS is the first discovery source.
5280#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5281#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
5282pub struct DnsDiscovery {
5283    /// The DNS hostname to resolve (e.g. `my-service.example.com`).
5284    pub hostname: String,
5285    /// The port to pair with each resolved A-record address. Ignored
5286    /// for SRV records (they carry their own port).
5287    pub port: u16,
5288    /// How often to re-resolve, in seconds (default 30; bounded to
5289    /// 1..=3600). The actual refresh interval is
5290    /// `min(refresh_interval_s, record_ttl)` so a short-TTL record is
5291    /// refreshed sooner.
5292    #[serde(
5293        default = "default_dns_discovery_refresh_s",
5294        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_dns_discovery_refresh_s"
5295    )]
5296    pub refresh_interval_s: u64,
5297    /// DNS record type: `"A"` (default) or `"SRV"`.
5298    #[serde(
5299        default = "default_dns_discovery_record_type",
5300        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_dns_discovery_record_type"
5301    )]
5302    pub record_type: String,
5303    /// If true (default), keep the last resolved endpoint set when DNS
5304    /// fails. If false, clear endpoints on failure (the upstream
5305    /// answers 503 until DNS recovers).
5306    #[serde(
5307        default = "default_dns_discovery_fail_open",
5308        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_dns_discovery_fail_open"
5309    )]
5310    pub fail_open: bool,
5311    /// Minimum endpoints to keep serving (default 1). If a resolution
5312    /// yields fewer than this, the previous set is kept (the upstream
5313    /// does not shrink below the floor).
5314    #[serde(
5315        default = "default_dns_discovery_min_endpoints",
5316        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_dns_discovery_min_endpoints"
5317    )]
5318    pub min_endpoints: u32,
5319}
5320
5321fn default_dns_discovery_refresh_s() -> u64 {
5322    30
5323}
5324fn is_default_dns_discovery_refresh_s(v: &u64) -> bool {
5325    *v == 30
5326}
5327fn default_dns_discovery_record_type() -> String {
5328    "A".to_string()
5329}
5330fn is_default_dns_discovery_record_type(v: &String) -> bool {
5331    v == "A"
5332}
5333fn default_dns_discovery_fail_open() -> bool {
5334    true
5335}
5336fn is_default_dns_discovery_fail_open(v: &bool) -> bool {
5337    *v
5338}
5339fn default_dns_discovery_min_endpoints() -> u32 {
5340    1
5341}
5342fn is_default_dns_discovery_min_endpoints(v: &u32) -> bool {
5343    *v == 1
5344}

5346/// Load-balancing pool: algorithm, protocol, endpoints.
5347#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5348#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
5349pub struct Upstream {
5350    pub name: String,
5351    #[serde(default = "default_load_balancer")]
5352    pub load_balancer: LoadBalancer,
5353    /// DP-07 (#236): what to hash for consistent-hash load balancers
5354    /// (`ip_hash` and `maglev`). Defaults to `client_ip` (the pre-#236
5355    /// behavior). When set to `cookie` or `header`, the gateway extracts
5356    /// the value from the request and uses it as the hash key; if the
5357    /// cookie/header is absent, it falls back to the client IP.
5358    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5359    pub hash_on: Option<HashOn>,
5360    /// Protocol used toward upstream endpoints.
5361    #[serde(default = "default_upstream_protocol")]
5362    pub protocol: UpstreamProtocol,
5363    /// Path to a PEM file of CA certificates this upstream's TLS
5364    /// connections trust INSTEAD of the default public (webpki) root set
5365    /// (#121): the way to proxy an `https`/`http2` upstream whose server
5366    /// certificate chains to a private CA. The file may carry several
5367    /// certificates (a typical CA bundle). Only meaningful for the TLS
5368    /// protocols — no TLS is negotiated toward `http1` endpoints, so
5369    /// validation rejects that combination. Active `http` health probes
5370    /// for this upstream verify against the same roots. The file must
5371    /// exist and be readable at config compile time; validation names
5372    /// this field otherwise.
5373    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5374    pub trusted_ca_file: Option<String>,
5375    /// REL-08 (#219): when true, the upstream's TLS connections trust the
5376    /// OS-native root CA store (loaded via `rustls-native-certs`) INSTEAD
5377    /// of the bundled webpki root set. Useful in deployments whose
5378    /// private CAs are provisioned through the host's trust store (e.g.
5379    /// corporate PKI, cloud metadata CAs). Mutually exclusive with
5380    /// `trusted_ca_file` (validation rejects a configuration that sets
5381    /// both). Only meaningful for the TLS protocols (`https`/`http2`/`h3`)
5382    /// — no TLS is negotiated toward `http1` endpoints, so validation
5383    /// rejects that combination. Active health probes for this upstream
5384    /// verify against the same roots. Defaults to `false` (webpki roots).
5385    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::ops::Not::not")]
5386    pub use_system_roots: bool,
5387    /// Static endpoint list for this upstream. When `dns_discovery` is
5388    /// present, this becomes the initial/fallback set (used until the
5389    /// first DNS resolution completes and as a fallback when DNS fails
5390    /// and `fail_open` is true); it may be empty. When `dns_discovery`
5391    /// is absent, this is the complete endpoint set and must be
5392    /// non-empty (validation rejects an empty list).
5393    #[serde(default)]
5394    pub endpoints: Vec<Endpoint>,
5395    /// Maximum number of concurrent outbound connections to this upstream
5396    /// (active plus pooled idle). Defaults to 256 when absent. Enforced by
5397    /// the upstream client (DW-008); excess connection attempts wait for a
5398    /// slot rather than fail. Raised from 64 to 256 (#270) to avoid
5399    /// unbounded memory growth under high concurrency.
5400    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5401    pub connection_cap: Option<u32>,
5402    /// Slow-start window in milliseconds (DW-011): an endpoint entering the
5403    /// upstream's set ramps its effective load-balancing weight from ~0 up
5404    /// to its configured weight over this window. Absent (or 0) disables
5405    /// the ramp. Applies to the weighted algorithms (round_robin; ip_hash
5406    /// vnode counts stay fixed so ring consistency is preserved).
5407    /// Validation bounds the value to at most 10 minutes.
5408    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5409    pub slow_start_ms: Option<u64>,
5410    /// Passive health checking / outlier detection (DW-012): eject
5411    /// endpoints that fail real traffic (transport errors and 5xx), let
5412    /// them back via half-open trial probes after `eject_ms`. Absent
5413    /// disables passive health entirely (no ejections).
5414    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5415    pub health: Option<PassiveHealth>,
5416    /// Active health checks (DW-013): synthetic HTTP/TCP probes per
5417    /// endpoint on a fixed interval with full jitter. Probe results report
5418    /// into the SAME per-endpoint ejection machinery as passive health, so
5419    /// an endpoint failing its probes leaves load-balancer rotation and a
5420    /// success streak returns it. Requires the passive `health` block
5421    /// (which owns the ejection/recovery windows); rejected by validation
5422    /// otherwise. Absent disables active probing.
5423    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5424    pub active_health: Option<ActiveHealth>,
5425    /// Upstream retries (DW-014): bounded per-request retry attempts with
5426    /// exponential backoff + full jitter, a retry budget, and opt-in
5427    /// size-capped request-body buffering. Absent (or `attempts` left at
5428    /// its default 0) disables retries entirely: every request gets exactly
5429    /// one attempt and the proxy path keeps its zero-copy streaming body.
5430    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5431    pub retries: Option<RetryConfig>,
5432    /// Per-upstream circuit breaker (DW-015): opens the WHOLE upstream on
5433    /// consecutive failures or a rolling error ratio, fails fast with 503
5434    /// while open, probes half-open after `breaker.open_ms`. Absent
5435    /// disables the breaker entirely (no fail-fast, behavior identical to
5436    /// pre-DW-015).
5437    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5438    pub breaker: Option<BreakerConfig>,
5439    /// Maximum number of requests WAITING for an outbound connection slot
5440    /// to this upstream (DW-015). 0/absent (the default) means a bounded
5441    /// queue of 256 — the default bounds memory under high concurrency
5442    /// (#270). A positive value rejects excess requests IMMEDIATELY with
5443    /// 503 "upstream saturated" instead of letting them wait. To restore
5444    /// the pre-#270 unbounded queueing behavior, set a very large value.
5445    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5446    pub max_pending: Option<u32>,
5447    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5448    pub timeouts: Option<Timeouts>,
5449    /// OAuth2 client-credentials proxying (DW-035): the gateway acts as
5450    /// an OAuth2 client itself, obtaining an access token from an
5451    /// external token endpoint via the client-credentials flow and
5452    /// forwarding it to this upstream as a `Bearer` token (replacing
5453    /// any client-supplied `Authorization` header). For service-to-
5454    /// service calls where the gateway authenticates to the upstream
5455    /// with a token it obtains from an OAuth2 token endpoint. Absent
5456    /// (the default): no token acquisition; the upstream sees whatever
5457    /// `Authorization` the client sent (subject to the authn family's
5458    /// pass-through rules). See the `security::oauth2` module docs for
5459    /// the flow, caching, and the mTLS-to-the-token-endpoint option.
5460    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5461    pub oauth2_client_credentials: Option<OAuth2ClientCredentials>,
5462    /// DNS-based dynamic upstream discovery (DW-042): when present, a
5463    /// background task resolves `hostname` via DNS and updates the
5464    /// endpoint set live as records change. The `endpoints` field
5465    /// becomes the initial/fallback set. Absent (the default): endpoints
5466    /// remain static (the current behavior — no change).
5467    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5468    pub dns_discovery: Option<DnsDiscovery>,
5469    /// Peak-EWMA tuning (DW-090): only meaningful when
5470    /// `load_balancer: peak_ewma`. Absent (the default) uses the built-in
5471    /// defaults (10 s decay tau, 250 ms initial cost). Ignored for the
5472    /// other algorithms.
5473    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5474    pub peak_ewma: Option<PeakEwmaConfig>,
5475    /// DW-094 (Ent): locality-aware routing and data residency. When
5476    /// present, the balancer filters or prefers endpoints by region/zone
5477    /// before applying the configured `load_balancer` algorithm. Ent-only:
5478    /// rejected at validation when the `ent` cargo feature is off.
5479    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5480    pub locality: Option<UpstreamLocality>,
5481    /// DW-105: opt in to post-quantum hybrid key exchange
5482    /// (X25519+ML-KEM) for this upstream's TLS connections. When
5483    /// `true`, the X25519+ML-KEM
5484    /// hybrid kx group is prepended to the rustls client config's kx
5485    /// group list, PREFERRING the hybrid group while keeping the
5486    /// classical X25519 fallback for non-PQ upstreams. Compiled into
5487    /// every build (no cargo feature).
5488    /// EXPERIMENTAL: the rustls PQ API is not yet stable. Only
5489    /// meaningful for the TLS protocols (`https`, `http2`); validation
5490    /// rejects `pq: true` on an `http1` upstream (no TLS is
5491    /// negotiated). Rejected when combined with FIPS mode (ML-KEM is
5492    /// not on the FIPS-validated list for aws-lc-rs).
5493    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
5494    pub pq: bool,
5495    /// DW-109 / SEC-04: upstream TLS certificate pinning by SPKI
5496    /// SHA-256 hash. When present, the gateway's outbound TLS client
5497    /// installs a custom certificate verifier that extracts the peer
5498    /// cert's SubjectPublicKeyInfo, computes its SHA-256, and compares
5499    /// against the configured pins. A mismatch rejects the connection
5500    /// (fail-closed: no fallback to CA-based verification). Compiled
5501    /// into every build (no cargo feature). Only meaningful for the
5502    /// TLS protocols
5503    /// (`https`, `http2`); validation rejects it on an `http1`
5504    /// upstream.
5505    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5506    pub cert_pinning: Option<CertPinningConfig>,
5507    /// SEC-03: upstream mTLS client certificates. When present, the
5508    /// gateway presents this client certificate to the upstream during
5509    /// the TLS handshake (in addition to verifying the upstream's
5510    /// server cert against `trusted_ca_file` or the public roots).
5511    /// Required for upstreams that demand client authentication
5512    /// (banking/payment APIs, mesh-internal services). Only meaningful
5513    /// for the TLS protocols (`https`, `http2`); validation rejects it
5514    /// on an `http1` upstream.
5515    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5516    pub mtls: Option<UpstreamMtls>,
5517    /// DP-03: hyper connection-pool and HTTP/2 tuning for this
5518    /// upstream's outbound client. Absent (the default): every knob
5519    /// falls back to hyper-util's built-in defaults (the prior
5520    /// behavior — only the pool timer was wired). When present, the
5521    /// configured knobs are applied to the per-upstream hyper-util
5522    /// legacy client builder; omitted sub-fields keep their hyper
5523    /// defaults. The pool timer is ALWAYS installed (it is required
5524    /// for idle-timeout eviction to fire), so this block only needs to
5525    /// carry the knobs an operator wants to change. Meaningful for
5526    /// every protocol: `http1`/`https` connections reuse the pool's
5527    /// idle-timeout and max-idle-per-host knobs, while `http2`
5528    /// connections additionally honor the keep-alive and
5529    /// concurrent-streams knobs.
5530    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5531    pub pool: Option<UpstreamPoolConfig>,
5532}
5533
5534/// DP-03: hyper connection-pool and HTTP/2 tuning for an upstream
5535/// (`upstreams[].pool`). Every field is optional; an omitted field
5536/// keeps hyper-util's built-in default (the same value the gateway
5537/// used before this block existed), so the block is purely additive.
5538/// Validation rejects zero/negative-forbidden values; the runtime
5539/// clamps nothing beyond the documented bounds.
5540#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5541#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
5542pub struct UpstreamPoolConfig {
5543    /// How long an idle connection may sit in the pool before it is
5544    /// closed (hyper-util `pool_idle_timeout`). In milliseconds.
5545    /// Absent: hyper-util's default (90 s). `0` is rejected at
5546    /// validation (use a very small positive value to evict
5547    /// aggressively, or omit the block for the default). Bounds: at
5548    /// most 10 minutes (600000 ms).
5549    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5550    pub pool_idle_timeout_ms: Option<u64>,
5551    /// Maximum number of idle connections retained per host
5552    /// (hyper-util `pool_max_idle_per_host`). Absent: hyper-util's
5553    /// default. The per-upstream `connection_cap` still bounds the
5554    /// total (active + idle) connections, so this only shapes the
5555    /// idle fraction. Bounds: at most 1024.
5556    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5557    pub pool_max_idle_per_host: Option<u32>,
5558    /// HTTP/2 PING keep-alive interval (hyper-util
5559    /// `http2_keep_alive_interval`). In milliseconds. Absent: disabled
5560    /// (hyper's default — no PINGs). A positive value sends a PING
5561    /// this often on an otherwise-idle h2 connection so proxies/ALB
5562    /// in front of the upstream do not reap it. Only meaningful for
5563    /// the `http2` protocol; ignored for `http1`/`https`. Bounds: at
5564    /// most 10 minutes (600000 ms).
5565    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5566    pub http2_keep_alive_interval_ms: Option<u64>,
5567    /// HTTP/2 keep-alive timeout (hyper-util
5568    /// `http2_keep_alive_timeout`): the time the client waits for a
5569    /// PING ACK before closing the connection. In milliseconds.
5570    /// Absent: hyper's default (20 s). Does nothing when
5571    /// `http2_keep_alive_interval_ms` is absent. Only meaningful for
5572    /// the `http2` protocol. Bounds: at most 10 minutes (600000 ms).
5573    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5574    pub http2_keep_alive_timeout_ms: Option<u64>,
5575    /// Enable HTTP/2 adaptive flow-control window (hyper-util
5576    /// `http2_adaptive_window`). Absent: false (hyper's default —
5577    /// fixed initial window). When true, the connection window grows
5578    /// and shrinks based on throughput, which helps large/long
5579    /// responses on high-BDP paths. Only meaningful for the `http2`
5580    /// protocol; ignored otherwise.
5581    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
5582    pub http2_adaptive_window: bool,
5583    /// Initial cap on the number of concurrent HTTP/2 streams the
5584    /// client will send over a single connection (hyper-util
5585    /// `http2_initial_max_send_streams`). This is an *initial* value
5586    /// only: hyper-util's default is 100, and the effective cap is
5587    /// further bounded by the server's advertised
5588    /// SETTINGS_MAX_CONCURRENT_STREAMS once the peer's SETTINGS frame
5589    /// arrives. A finite value bounds head-of-line blocking when one
5590    /// stream stalls. Only meaningful for the `http2` protocol.
5591    /// Bounds: at least 1, at most 1_000_000.
5592    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5593    pub max_concurrent_streams: Option<u32>,
5594    /// SCALE-10 (#189): number of connections to pre-establish per
5595    /// endpoint on startup/reload. Pre-warming removes cold-start
5596    /// latency spikes after reloads and upgrade hand-offs. `0` or
5597    /// absent: no pre-warming (the default — connections are
5598    /// established on first use). Bounds: at most 64 per endpoint.
5599    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5600    pub pre_warm: Option<u32>,
5601    /// SCALE-10 (#189): per-endpoint connection cap, bounding
5602    /// concurrent connections to a SINGLE endpoint (address:port)
5603    /// within the upstream. The per-upstream `connection_cap` still
5604    /// bounds the total across all endpoints; this knob prevents one
5605    /// busy endpoint from monopolizing the upstream's connection
5606    /// budget. Absent: no per-endpoint cap (only the per-upstream
5607    /// cap applies). Bounds: at least 1, at most 1024.
5608    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5609    pub per_endpoint_cap: Option<u32>,
5610    /// SCALE-10 (#189): maximum age of a pooled connection before it
5611    /// is recycled. In milliseconds. Absent: no max-age (connections
5612    /// live until the idle timeout or a peer-initiated close). A
5613    /// positive value sets the pool idle timeout to the minimum of
5614    /// this and `pool_idle_timeout_ms`, so connections are evicted
5615    /// before they become stale. Bounds: at most 10 minutes
5616    /// (600000 ms).
5617    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5618    pub max_connection_age_ms: Option<u64>,
5619    /// Initial stream-level window size in bytes for the upstream h2
5620    /// client (hyper `http2_initial_stream_window_size`). Absent:
5621    /// hyper's default. Larger values reduce flow-control stalls on
5622    /// streaming (SSE) responses from the upstream but increase
5623    /// memory per stream. Only meaningful for the `http2` protocol.
5624    /// Bounds: at most 16 MiB. (PERF-11, #244)
5625    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5626    pub http2_initial_stream_window_size: Option<u32>,
5627    /// Initial connection-level window size in bytes for the upstream
5628    /// h2 client (hyper `http2_initial_connection_window_size`).
5629    /// Absent: hyper's default. Bounds: at most 64 MiB.
5630    /// (PERF-11, #244)
5631    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5632    pub http2_initial_connection_window_size: Option<u32>,
5633}
5634
5635/// SEC-03: upstream mTLS client certificate configuration. Reuses the
5636/// two-path shape (cert file + key file) proven by the OAuth2 mTLS
5637/// config (`OAuth2Mtls`).
5638#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5639#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
5640pub struct UpstreamMtls {
5641    /// Path to the PEM client certificate chain to present to the
5642    /// upstream during the TLS handshake.
5643    pub client_cert_file: String,
5644    /// Path to the PEM private key for `client_cert_file`.
5645    pub client_key_file: String,
5646}
5647
5648/// DW-109 / SEC-04: certificate pinning configuration for an upstream.
5649/// Holds a list of allowed SPKI SHA-256 hashes (lowercase hex, 64
5650/// chars). A peer cert whose SPKI hash matches ANY entry is accepted;
5651/// otherwise the connection is rejected (fail-closed).
5652#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5653#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
5654pub struct CertPinningConfig {
5655    /// The allowed SPKI SHA-256 hashes (lowercase hex, 64 chars each).
5656    /// Must be non-empty (validation rejects an empty pin list — a
5657    /// pin-less pinning block is an authoring mistake, not "pin
5658    /// nothing").
5659    pub pins: Vec<CertPin>,
5660}
5661
5662/// A single certificate pin: the SHA-256 hash of the upstream
5663/// certificate's SubjectPublicKeyInfo (SPKI), as lowercase hex (64
5664/// chars). Pinning the SPKI (rather than the full certificate) allows
5665/// rotation of the leaf certificate as long as the key pair is
5666/// unchanged.
5667#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5668#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
5669pub struct CertPin {
5670    /// The SHA-256 hash of the SPKI, as lowercase hex (64 chars).
5671    pub spki_sha256: String,
5672}
5673
5674/// DW-094 (Ent): locality-aware routing and data residency configuration
5675/// for an upstream. Controls how the balancer uses endpoint `region`/
5676/// `zone` fields to filter or prefer endpoints.
5677#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5678#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
5679pub struct UpstreamLocality {
5680    /// The routing mode (DW-094):
5681    /// - `prefer`: prefer endpoints in the edge's region; fall back to
5682    ///   other regions if none are available (healthy).
5683    /// - `strict`: only route to endpoints in the edge's region; fail
5684    ///   with 503 if none are available (no cross-region fallback).
5685    /// - `failover`: prefer the edge's region, fall back to other
5686    ///   regions only when all local endpoints are ejected (like
5687    ///   `prefer` but with a stricter local-first policy — local
5688    ///   endpoints are always tried first even if unhealthy).
5689    #[serde(default)]
5690    pub mode: LocalityMode,
5691    /// Data residency enforcement: when true, the balancer drops
5692    /// endpoints whose `region` is not in `allowed_regions` BEFORE
5693    /// applying the locality mode. This is a hard constraint —
5694    /// endpoints outside the allowed set are never used, even as a
5695    /// fallback. Use this to comply with data sovereignty requirements
5696    /// (e.g. EU traffic must stay in EU regions).
5697    #[serde(default)]
5698    pub enforce_data_residency: bool,
5699    /// The allowed regions when `enforce_data_residency` is true. An
5700    /// endpoint with no `region` field is always allowed (locality-
5701    /// agnostic). An endpoint with a `region` not in this list is
5702    /// dropped. Must be non-empty when `enforce_data_residency` is true.
5703    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
5704    pub allowed_regions: Vec<String>,
5705    /// The denied regions (optional). An endpoint with a `region` in
5706    /// this list is always dropped, regardless of other settings. Use
5707    /// this to exclude specific regions without enumerating all
5708    /// allowed ones.
5709    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
5710    pub denied_regions: Vec<String>,
5711}
5712
5713/// DW-094 (Ent): locality routing mode.
5714#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema, Default)]
5715#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
5716pub enum LocalityMode {
5717    /// Prefer endpoints in the edge's region; fall back to other
5718    /// regions if none are available (healthy). The default.
5719    #[default]
5720    Prefer,
5721    /// Only route to endpoints in the edge's region; fail with 503 if
5722    /// none are available (no cross-region fallback).
5723    Strict,
5724    /// Prefer the edge's region, fall back to other regions only when
5725    /// all local endpoints are ejected (local-first with failover).
5726    Failover,
5727}
5728
5729/// DW-094 (Ent): the edge's locality context — its region and zone.
5730/// Threaded into the balancer at request time to enable locality-aware
5731/// endpoint selection. When both `region` and `zone` are `None`, the
5732/// balancer skips locality filtering (the OSS behavior).
5733///
5734/// The context is set from `EdgeState.labels` (the `region` and `zone`
5735/// keys) when running as an edge, or from the `DWARA_REGION`/
5736/// `DWARA_ZONE` environment variables in embedded mode.
5737#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
5738pub struct LocalityContext {
5739    /// The edge's region (e.g. `us-east-1`).
5740    pub region: Option<String>,
5741    /// The edge's zone (e.g. `us-east-1a`).
5742    pub zone: Option<String>,
5743}
5744
5745impl LocalityContext {
5746    /// Create a new locality context.
5747    pub fn new(region: Option<String>, zone: Option<String>) -> Self {
5748        Self { region, zone }
5749    }
5750
5751    /// Whether locality-aware routing should be applied (at least one
5752    /// of region/zone is set).
5753    pub fn is_set(&self) -> bool {
5754        self.region.is_some() || self.zone.is_some()
5755    }
5756
5757    /// Whether an endpoint region matches this context's region.
5758    /// An endpoint with no region is always a match (locality-agnostic).
5759    pub fn region_matches(&self, endpoint_region: Option<&str>) -> bool {
5760        match (&self.region, endpoint_region) {
5761            (Some(ctx_region), Some(ep_region)) => ctx_region == ep_region,
5762            _ => true,
5763        }
5764    }
5765
5766    /// Whether an endpoint zone matches this context's zone.
5767    /// An endpoint with no zone is always a match (zone-agnostic).
5768    pub fn zone_matches(&self, endpoint_zone: Option<&str>) -> bool {
5769        match (&self.zone, endpoint_zone) {
5770            (Some(ctx_zone), Some(ep_zone)) => ctx_zone == ep_zone,
5771            _ => true,
5772        }
5773    }
5774
5775    /// Load the locality context from environment variables
5776    /// (`DWARA_REGION`, `DWARA_ZONE`). Returns a context with `None`
5777    /// values when the variables are not set.
5778    pub fn from_env() -> Self {
5779        Self {
5780            region: std::env::var("DWARA_REGION").ok(),
5781            zone: std::env::var("DWARA_ZONE").ok(),
5782        }
5783    }
5784}
5785
5786/// Peak-EWMA load-balancing tuning (DW-090, `upstreams[].peak_ewma`).
5787/// Only consulted when `load_balancer: peak_ewma`; ignored otherwise.
5788#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5789#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
5790pub struct PeakEwmaConfig {
5791    /// Decay tau in milliseconds (default 10 000 = 10 s). The EWMA weight
5792    /// is `exp(-td / tau)` where `td` is the time since the last update.
5793    /// A larger tau makes the cost track slow endpoints more sluggishly
5794    /// (more stable, less reactive); a smaller tau reacts faster to
5795    /// latency changes. Must be > 0.
5796    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5797    pub decay_ms: Option<u64>,
5798    /// Initial cost in milliseconds (default 250). A fresh endpoint's
5799    /// EWMA cost starts here (converted to nanoseconds) so the first
5800    /// picks have a reasonable baseline before any real RTT is observed.
5801    /// Must be > 0.
5802    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5803    pub default_rtt_ms: Option<u64>,
5804}
5805
5806/// OAuth2 client-credentials configuration for an upstream (DW-035,
5807/// `upstreams[].oauth2_client_credentials`).
5808///
5809/// The gateway obtains an access token from `token_endpoint` using the
5810/// client-credentials grant (RFC 6749 section 4.4) and forwards it to
5811/// the upstream as `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. The client
5812/// authenticates to the token endpoint with HTTP Basic auth
5813/// (`client_id:client_secret`, RFC 6749 section 2.3.1). Tokens are
5814/// cached per upstream (keyed by upstream name on the dataplane) and
5815/// refreshed lazily on the first request after expiry — no background
5816/// refresh task. An optional `mtls` block configures a client
5817/// certificate for the TLS handshake to the token endpoint itself
5818/// (RFC 8705, the `tls_client_auth` method).
5819#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5820#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
5821pub struct OAuth2ClientCredentials {
5822    /// The token endpoint URL (RFC 6749 section 3.2). Must be an
5823    /// absolute `http(s)://` URL; validation rejects anything else.
5824    pub token_endpoint: String,
5825    /// The client identifier presented to the token endpoint
5826    /// (RFC 6749 section 2.2). Sent as the username of the HTTP Basic
5827    /// auth header.
5828    pub client_id: String,
5829    /// The client secret presented to the token endpoint (RFC 6749
5830    /// section 2.3.1). Either the value INLINE (redacted in every
5831    /// config echo, DW-045) or a `${...}` secret reference (env or
5832    /// file) resolved at config-compile time and re-read on every
5833    /// reload. Sent as the password of the HTTP Basic auth header.
5834    pub client_secret: String,
5835    /// Optional scope string (RFC 6749 section 3.3): a space-delimited
5836    /// list of requested scopes sent in the token request body. Absent
5837    /// = no scope parameter (the endpoint's default scopes apply).
5838    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
5839    pub scopes: Vec<String>,
5840    /// Optional mTLS client certificate for the TLS handshake to the
5841    /// token endpoint itself (RFC 8705 `tls_client_auth`). Absent: the
5842    /// token endpoint's TLS is negotiated with no client certificate
5843    /// (the default webpki root set, or a `trusted_ca_file` when the
5844    /// token endpoint is behind a private CA — note the token endpoint
5845    /// trust is NOT the upstream's `trusted_ca_file`; the token
5846    /// endpoint is a separate entity).
5847    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5848    pub mtls: Option<OAuth2Mtls>,
5849    /// Token cache TTL override in seconds. Absent (the default): use
5850    /// the token's own `expires_in` (minus a refresh skew margin). When
5851    /// set, the cache TTL is `min(token_expires_in - skew,
5852    /// token_cache_ttl_s)` so a shorter override never extends a
5853    /// token's real lifetime. Must be > 0.
5854    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5855    pub token_cache_ttl_s: Option<u64>,
5856}
5857
5858/// mTLS client certificate for an OAuth2 token endpoint (DW-035,
5859/// `oauth2_client_credentials.mtls`).
5860#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5861#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
5862pub struct OAuth2Mtls {
5863    /// Path to a PEM file containing the client certificate chain.
5864    pub client_cert: String,
5865    /// Path to a PEM file containing the matching private key.
5866    pub client_key: String,
5867}
5868
5869/// Upstream retry knobs (DW-014). All fields default; a `retries:` block
5870/// with no keys is equivalent to retries off (`attempts` defaults to 0).
5871///
5872/// Frozen semantics (see `upstream`/`proxy`/`retries` module docs):
5873/// - `attempts` is the maximum number of RETRIES beyond the first attempt
5874///   (0 = off). Validation caps it at 10.
5875/// - Only requests whose body was fully buffered within `buffer_max_bytes`
5876///   may be retried; an over-cap body streams without retry. Buffering is
5877///   opt-in: the default (0) buffers only empty bodies, so the default
5878///   proxy path stays unbuffered.
5879/// - Retries happen strictly BEFORE response headers arrive on the final
5880///   attempt; a response body that dies mid-stream is never retried (its
5881///   failure is reported to passive health instead).
5882/// - Every retried attempt is charged against the upstream's rolling-window
5883///   retry budget (`budget_percent`).
5884/// - `total_deadline_ms` (REL-01) caps the wall-clock time from the first
5885///   attempt to the last, INCLUDING backoff delays between attempts. Absent
5886///   leaves the cross-attempt budget unbounded (the v1 default, kept for
5887///   backwards compatibility). When set, a retry whose backoff would cross
5888///   the deadline is aborted and the last response/error is returned to the
5889///   client; the per-attempt `read_ms` timeout still bounds each individual
5890///   attempt. Standard in Envoy and NGINX.
5891#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5892#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
5893pub struct RetryConfig {
5894    /// Maximum retries beyond the first attempt (default 0 = off).
5895    #[serde(default = "default_retry_attempts")]
5896    pub attempts: u32,
5897    /// Retry non-idempotent POST requests. Default false: POST is never
5898    /// retried unless an operator explicitly opts in here (a retried POST
5899    /// may replay a body the upstream already partially processed).
5900    #[serde(default = "default_retry_post", skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
5901    pub retry_post: bool,
5902    /// Exponential backoff base in milliseconds (default 25): the nominal
5903    /// delay before retry n is `min(base * 2^(n-1), backoff_cap_ms)`.
5904    #[serde(default = "default_retry_backoff_base_ms")]
5905    pub backoff_base_ms: u64,
5906    /// Exponential backoff ceiling in milliseconds (default 250). Must be
5907    /// >= `backoff_base_ms`.
5908    #[serde(default = "default_retry_backoff_cap_ms")]
5909    pub backoff_cap_ms: u64,
5910    /// Response statuses that trigger a retry when received as the upstream
5911    /// response status (default `[502, 503, 504]`). Each entry must be a
5912    /// valid 4xx/5xx status. An empty list disables status-based retries.
5913    #[serde(default = "default_retry_statuses")]
5914    pub retry_statuses: Vec<u16>,
5915    /// Retry on transport errors (connect timeout/refusal/reset/framing)
5916    /// and per-attempt read timeouts (default true).
5917    #[serde(default = "default_retry_transport", skip_serializing_if = "is_true")]
5918    pub retry_transport: bool,
5919    /// Retry budget: the maximum percentage of requests to this upstream,
5920    /// in a rolling window, that may be retries (default 10). Must be in
5921    /// (0, 100]. When the budget is exhausted, failing requests fail
5922    /// through to the client instead of retrying.
5923    #[serde(default = "default_retry_budget_percent")]
5924    pub budget_percent: u32,
5925    /// Request-body buffering cap in bytes (default 0 = no buffering). A
5926    /// request body is buffered (and becomes replayable) only while it fits
5927    /// within this cap; larger bodies stream and are never retried.
5928    #[serde(default)]
5929    pub buffer_max_bytes: u64,
5930    /// Request hedging (DW-063): after `hedge_after_ms` milliseconds
5931    /// without a response, a speculative duplicate request is sent to a
5932    /// different endpoint; the first response wins and the loser is
5933    /// cancelled. Requires `buffer_max_bytes > 0` (the body must be
5934    /// replayable to send a hedge copy) and idempotent methods (GET,
5935    /// HEAD, OPTIONS, TRACE, PUT) — POST is hedged only when
5936    /// `retry_post` is also true. Absent (or `hedge_after_ms` = 0)
5937    /// disables hedging. At most `hedge_max` speculative copies are
5938    /// sent per request (default 1).
5939    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5940    pub hedge: Option<HedgeConfig>,
5941    /// Cross-attempt total retry deadline in milliseconds (REL-01): caps
5942    /// the wall-clock time from the first attempt to the last, INCLUDING
5943    /// backoff delays between attempts. Absent leaves the cross-attempt
5944    /// budget unbounded (the v1 default, kept for backwards
5945    /// compatibility). When set, a retry whose backoff would cross the
5946    /// deadline is aborted and the last response/error is returned to the
5947    /// client; the per-attempt `read_ms` timeout still bounds each
5948    /// individual attempt. Must be in (0, `MAX_RETRY_TOTAL_DEADLINE_MS`];
5949    /// `0` is rejected (omit the field for unbounded).
5950    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
5951    pub total_deadline_ms: Option<u64>,
5952    /// Streaming failover first-frame buffer cap in bytes (REL-05, #216).
5953    /// When > 0, the proxy buffers up to this many bytes of the upstream
5954    /// response body BEFORE forwarding any bytes to the client. If the
5955    /// upstream fails (connection reset, read timeout, framing error)
5956    /// before the buffer fills or the body completes, the proxy can
5957    /// still retry the request to a different endpoint — the client has
5958    /// not yet received any bytes, so the response is not committed.
5959    /// Once the buffer fills (or the body completes), the buffered
5960    /// prefix is flushed to the client and the rest of the body streams
5961    /// frame-by-frame (the stream is then committed; a later mid-body
5962    /// failure is terminal, as in v1). Default 0 = no first-frame
5963    /// buffering (v1 behavior: the response is committed as soon as
5964    /// headers resolve). Requires `attempts > 0` and
5965    /// `buffer_max_bytes > 0` (the request body must be replayable).
5966    #[serde(default)]
5967    pub buffer_first_frame_bytes: u64,
5968}
5969
5970impl Default for RetryConfig {
5971    fn default() -> Self {
5972        RetryConfig {
5973            attempts: default_retry_attempts(),
5974            retry_post: default_retry_post(),
5975            backoff_base_ms: default_retry_backoff_base_ms(),
5976            backoff_cap_ms: default_retry_backoff_cap_ms(),
5977            retry_statuses: default_retry_statuses(),
5978            retry_transport: default_retry_transport(),
5979            budget_percent: default_retry_budget_percent(),
5980            buffer_max_bytes: 0,
5981            hedge: None,
5982            total_deadline_ms: None,
5983            buffer_first_frame_bytes: 0,
5984        }
5985    }
5986}
5987
5988/// Request hedging configuration (DW-063, `upstreams[].retries.hedge`).
5989///
5990/// After `hedge_after_ms` without a response, a speculative duplicate
5991/// request is sent to a different endpoint. The first response (headers
5992/// resolved) wins; the loser is cancelled. Hedging requires a replayable
5993/// body (`buffer_max_bytes > 0`) and idempotent semantics (POST is hedged
5994/// only when `retry_post` is true).
5995#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
5996#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
5997pub struct HedgeConfig {
5998    /// Milliseconds to wait before sending a hedge copy (the "tail
5999    /// latency" threshold). Must be > 0. A typical value is the p90
6000    /// latency of the upstream — requests slower than this get a
6001    /// speculative parallel copy.
6002    pub hedge_after_ms: u64,
6003    /// Maximum number of speculative hedge copies per request (default
6004    /// 1). Must be in [1, 4]. Hedge copies are NOT charged against the
6005    /// retry budget — the budget prevents retry storms after failures,
6006    /// while hedging is a proactive performance optimization.
6007    #[serde(default = "default_hedge_max")]
6008    pub hedge_max: u32,
6009}
6010
6011fn default_hedge_max() -> u32 {
6012    1
6013}

6016    0
6017}

6025}

6028    250

6032    vec![502, 503, 504]
6033}

6036    true
6037}

6047/// Per-upstream circuit breaker knobs (DW-015). All fields default; a
6048/// `breaker:` block with no keys enables the breaker with the defaults.
6049///
6050/// Frozen semantics (see the `breaker` module docs):
6051/// - The breaker gates the WHOLE upstream (all endpoints); per-endpoint
6052///   ejection (DW-012) is an independent layer beneath it.
6053/// - It opens on `consecutive_failures` consecutive failures (5xx or
6054///   transport) OR an in-window error ratio >= `error_ratio` once at least
6055///   `error_volume` observations are in the 60 s window.
6056/// - While open, requests fail fast with 503 and a `Retry-After` header
6057///   (seconds until half-open); in-flight requests complete normally.
6058/// - After `open_ms` a half-open probe (`half_open_probes` concurrent
6059///   trials) closes the breaker on success or re-opens it on failure.
6060#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6061#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
6062pub struct BreakerConfig {
6063    /// Consecutive failures (5xx + transport) that open the breaker
6064    /// (default 5).
6065    #[serde(default = "default_breaker_consecutive_failures")]
6066    pub consecutive_failures: u32,
6067    /// In-window error ratio in (0, 1] that opens the breaker once
6068    /// `error_volume` observations exist (default 0.5).
6069    #[serde(default = "default_breaker_error_ratio")]
6070    pub error_ratio: f64,
6071    /// Minimum observations in the 60 s window before the ratio is
6072    /// evaluated (default 20).
6073    #[serde(default = "default_breaker_error_volume")]
6074    pub error_volume: u32,
6075    /// Cooling-off period in milliseconds before a half-open probe is
6076    /// admitted (default 30000).
6077    #[serde(default = "default_breaker_open_ms")]
6078    pub open_ms: u64,
6079    /// Concurrent trial requests admitted in half-open (default 1).
6080    #[serde(default = "default_breaker_half_open_probes")]
6081    pub half_open_probes: u32,
6082}
6083
6084impl Default for BreakerConfig {
6085    fn default() -> Self {
6086        BreakerConfig {
6087            consecutive_failures: default_breaker_consecutive_failures(),
6088            error_ratio: default_breaker_error_ratio(),
6089            error_volume: default_breaker_error_volume(),
6090            open_ms: default_breaker_open_ms(),
6091            half_open_probes: default_breaker_half_open_probes(),
6092        }
6093    }
6094}
6095
6096fn default_breaker_consecutive_failures() -> u32 {
6097    5
6098}
6099
6100fn default_breaker_error_ratio() -> f64 {
6101    0.5
6102}
6103
6104fn default_breaker_error_volume() -> u32 {
6105    20
6106}
6107
6108fn default_breaker_open_ms() -> u64 {
6109    30_000
6110}
6111
6112fn default_breaker_half_open_probes() -> u32 {
6113    1
6114}

6116fn is_true(b: &bool) -> bool {
6117    *b
6118}

6121    false

6124/// Passive health / outlier detection knobs (DW-012). All fields default;
6125/// a `health:` block with no keys enables ejection with the defaults.
6126///
6127/// Failure classification: transport errors (connect timeout, refusal,
6128/// reset) and HTTP statuses >= 500 are failures; 1xx-4xx are successes.
6129/// 429/408 are deliberately successes in v1 (they describe the caller or
6130/// queueing, not endpoint health).
6131#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6132#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
6133pub struct PassiveHealth {
6134    /// Rolling observation window for the failure ratio, in milliseconds
6135    /// (default 60000).
6136    #[serde(default = "default_health_window_ms")]
6137    pub window_ms: u64,
6138    /// Eject after this many consecutive failures (default 5).
6139    #[serde(default = "default_health_consecutive_failures")]
6140    pub consecutive_failures: u32,
6141    /// Eject when the in-window failure share is >= this ratio AND volume
6142    /// is >= `failure_min_volume`. Must be in (0, 1] (default 0.5).
6143    #[serde(default = "default_health_failure_ratio")]
6144    pub failure_ratio: f64,
6145    /// Minimum observations in the window before `failure_ratio` applies
6146    /// (default 20).
6147    #[serde(default = "default_health_failure_min_volume")]
6148    pub failure_min_volume: u32,
6149    /// How long an ejected endpoint stays out of rotation, in milliseconds
6150    /// (default 30000).
6151    #[serde(default = "default_health_eject_ms")]
6152    pub eject_ms: u64,
6153    /// Trial requests allowed through per half-open recovery attempt
6154    /// (default 1). A successful probe restores health; a failed probe
6155    /// re-ejects for another `eject_ms`.
6156    #[serde(default = "default_health_half_open_probes")]
6157    pub half_open_probes: u32,
6158    /// Recovery ramp window in milliseconds (REL-06, #217). When > 0,
6159    /// an endpoint that recovers from ejection (a half-open probe
6160    /// succeeds) does NOT immediately return to full traffic. Instead
6161    /// it ramps its effective weight from a floor (1) to its
6162    /// configured weight over this window, reducing the blast radius
6163    /// of a partial recovery (the endpoint may still be slow or
6164    /// partially degraded). Default 0 = no ramp (v1 behavior: a
6165    /// successful probe restores full weight immediately).
6166    #[serde(default)]
6167    pub recovery_ramp_ms: u64,
6168    /// Whether the in-flight counter is held until the response body
6169    /// completes (REL-06, #217). Default false: the in-flight counter
6170    /// is released when response headers resolve (v1 behavior). When
6171    /// true, the counter is held until the body stream ends or is
6172    /// dropped, giving `least_requests`, `random`, and `peak_ewma` a
6173    /// more accurate view of actual endpoint load. The trade-off is a
6174    /// slightly higher in-flight count under streaming workloads.
6175    #[serde(default = "default_false", skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
6176    pub body_completion_inflight: bool,
6177}
6178
6179impl Default for PassiveHealth {
6180    fn default() -> Self {
6181        PassiveHealth {
6182            window_ms: 60_000,
6183            consecutive_failures: 5,
6184            failure_ratio: 0.5,
6185            failure_min_volume: 20,
6186            eject_ms: 30_000,
6187            half_open_probes: 1,
6188            recovery_ramp_ms: 0,
6189            body_completion_inflight: false,
6190        }
6191    }
6192}
6193
6194fn default_health_window_ms() -> u64 {
6195    60_000
6196}
6197
6198fn default_health_consecutive_failures() -> u32 {
6199    5
6200}
6201
6202fn default_health_failure_ratio() -> f64 {
6203    0.5
6204}
6205
6206fn default_health_failure_min_volume() -> u32 {
6207    20
6208}
6209
6210fn default_health_eject_ms() -> u64 {
6211    30_000
6212}
6213
6214fn default_health_half_open_probes() -> u32 {
6215    1

6218/// Active health check knobs (DW-013). A block with no keys enables HTTP
6219/// probes with the defaults.
6220///
6221/// Probe semantics (frozen):
6222/// - `http` probes issue `GET {path}` over HTTP/1.1 DIRECTLY to the
6223///   endpoint (bypassing load balancing and the pooled client); success is
6224///   a 2xx status. Redirects (3xx) are NOT followed — a health endpoint
6225///   answering 3xx is treated as a failure (a load balancer must not chase
6226///   redirects to decide health).
6227/// - `tcp` probes succeed when a TCP connection completes within
6228///   `timeout_ms`.
6229/// - Full jitter: each loop sleeps `interval_ms` plus a uniform random
6230///   `0..jitter_ms` before the next probe.
6231#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6232#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
6233pub struct ActiveHealth {
6234    /// Probe kind (default `http`).
6235    #[serde(default = "default_probe_kind")]
6236    pub kind: ProbeKind,
6237    /// Path probed by `http` checks (default `/healthz`). Ignored by `tcp`.
6238    #[serde(
6239        default = "default_probe_path",
6240        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_probe_path"
6241    )]
6242    pub path: String,
6243    /// Time between probe attempts in milliseconds (default 5000). Must be
6244    /// >= `timeout_ms` and >= `jitter_ms`.
6245    #[serde(default = "default_probe_interval_ms")]
6246    pub interval_ms: u64,
6247    /// Per-probe timeout in milliseconds (default 2000), covering connect
6248    /// plus the response for http probes.
6249    #[serde(default = "default_probe_timeout_ms")]
6250    pub timeout_ms: u64,
6251    /// Consecutive probe SUCCESSES required to (re)admit an ejected
6252    /// endpoint (default 2).
6253    #[serde(default = "default_probe_success_threshold")]
6254    pub success_threshold: u32,
6255    /// Consecutive probe FAILURES required to eject a healthy endpoint
6256    /// (default 3). Reports the same per-endpoint streak the passive
6257    /// checker uses; see the active-health module docs for precedence.
6258    #[serde(default = "default_probe_failure_threshold")]
6259    pub failure_threshold: u32,
6260    /// Full-jitter bound in milliseconds (default 500): each loop sleeps a
6261    /// uniform random `0..jitter_ms` in addition to `interval_ms`. Must be
6262    /// <= `interval_ms`.
6263    #[serde(default = "default_probe_jitter_ms")]
6264    pub jitter_ms: u64,
6265}
6266
6267impl Default for ActiveHealth {
6268    fn default() -> Self {
6269        ActiveHealth {
6270            kind: default_probe_kind(),
6271            path: default_probe_path(),
6272            interval_ms: default_probe_interval_ms(),
6273            timeout_ms: default_probe_timeout_ms(),
6274            success_threshold: default_probe_success_threshold(),
6275            failure_threshold: default_probe_failure_threshold(),
6276            jitter_ms: default_probe_jitter_ms(),
6277        }
6278    }
6279}
6280

6282    ProbeKind::Http
6283}

6286    "/healthz".to_string()
6287}

6290    p == "/healthz"
6291}

6293fn default_probe_interval_ms() -> u64 {
6294    5_000
6295}
6296
6297fn default_probe_timeout_ms() -> u64 {
6298    2_000
6299}

6302    2

6304
