6313/// Kind of active health probe.
6314#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6315#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
6316pub enum ProbeKind {
6317    /// HTTP/1.1 GET to `path`; success = 2xx.
6318    Http,
6319    /// HTTP/2 GET to `path` over a pooled H2 connection (REL-07, #218);
6320    /// for HTTP/2-only and gRPC upstreams that refuse HTTP/1.1 on a
6321    /// separate connection. Success = 2xx.
6322    Http2,
6323    /// TCP connect within the timeout.
6324    Tcp,
6325}
6326
6327fn default_load_balancer() -> LoadBalancer {
6328    LoadBalancer::RoundRobin
6329}
6330
6331#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6332#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
6333pub enum LoadBalancer {
6334    RoundRobin,
6335    LeastRequests,
6336    Random,
6337    IpHash,
6338    /// Maglev consistent hashing (DP-07, #236): Google's Maglev algorithm
6339    /// uses a fixed-size lookup table (default 65537 entries) populated
6340    /// by each endpoint's permutation. Provides better distribution than
6341    /// ketama for large endpoint pools with tight balance bounds. The
6342    /// hash key is determined by `hash_on` (client IP, cookie, or header).
6343    Maglev,
6344    /// Peak-EWMA latency-aware load balancing (DW-090, Finagle-style):
6345    /// favors low-latency endpoints and degrades outliers. Each endpoint
6346    /// carries an atomic EWMA cost tracker (nanosecond resolution) updated
6347    /// with the Finagle peak-EWMA formula: a new RTT that EXCEEDS the
6348    /// current cost replaces it outright (the "peak"), otherwise the cost
6349    /// decays toward the new RTT at rate `exp(-td / tau)`. Selection picks
6350    /// the endpoint with the lowest `cost_ns * (inflight + 1)` score, so a
6351    /// slow endpoint with few in-flight requests can still win a pick
6352    /// while a fast one is saturated. Tunable via `upstreams[].peak_ewma`
6353    /// (decay tau and the initial cost); defaults are 10 s decay and a
6354    /// 250 ms initial cost. Carried across rebuilds by `address:port`,
6355    /// exactly like in-flight counters and health trackers.
6356    PeakEwma,
6357}
6358
6359/// What to hash for consistent-hash load balancers (`ip_hash` and
6360/// `maglev`). DP-07 (#236): extends the hash key source beyond the
6361/// client IP to support cookie-based sticky sessions and header-based
6362/// affinity.
6363#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6364#[serde(tag = "type", rename_all = "snake_case", deny_unknown_fields)]
6365pub enum HashOn {
6366    /// Hash the client IP (the default behavior, backward-compatible
6367    /// with pre-#236 configs).
6368    #[default]
6369    ClientIp,
6370    /// Hash a cookie value. If the cookie is not present in the request,
6371    /// the gateway falls back to the client IP. Used for sticky sessions
6372    /// without the `services[].sticky` split mechanism.
6373    Cookie {
6374        /// The cookie name to read.
6375        cookie: String,
6376    },
6377    /// Hash a request header value. If the header is not present, falls
6378    /// back to the client IP. Common for routing by `X-User-Id` or
6379    /// `X-Session-Id` for session affinity.
6380    Header {
6381        /// The header name to read (case-insensitive).
6382        header: String,
6383    },
6384}
6385
6386fn default_upstream_protocol() -> UpstreamProtocol {
6387    UpstreamProtocol::Http1
6388}
6389
6390#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6391#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
6392pub enum UpstreamProtocol {
6393    Http1,
6394    Http2,
6395    /// Cleartext HTTP/2 with prior knowledge (h2c, DP-02 #235). No TLS;
6396    /// the client speaks HTTP/2 directly over TCP. Common for internal
6397    /// east-west gRPC traffic without TLS.
6398    H2c,
6399    Https,
6400    /// HTTP/3 over QUIC (DW-108). QUIC mandates TLS 1.3, so an `h3`
6401    /// upstream always negotiates TLS with ALPN `h3`; the
6402    /// `trusted_ca_file` field selects the trust roots (the default
6403    /// webpki public set, or a private-CA bundle). Requires the `h3`
6404    /// cargo feature on dwara-bin to actually proxy: when the feature is
6405    /// off the protocol is accepted at validation but the upstream is
6406    /// inert (every dispatch fails closed with a clear error rather than
6407    /// silently falling back to a wrong transport).
6408    H3,
6409}
6410
6411/// One `address:port` inside an upstream.
6412#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6413#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
6414pub struct Endpoint {
6415    pub address: String,
6416    pub port: u16,
6417    /// Relative traffic weight (default 1 for all endpoints).
6418    #[serde(
6419        default = "default_endpoint_weight",
6420        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_weight"
6421    )]
6422    pub weight: u32,
6423    /// DW-094 (Ent): the region this endpoint belongs to (e.g.
6424    /// `us-east-1`, `eu-west-1`). Used by locality-aware load balancing
6425    /// and data residency enforcement. Optional — endpoints without a
6426    /// region are treated as locality-agnostic (always eligible).
6427    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6428    pub region: Option<String>,
6429    /// DW-094 (Ent): the zone within a region (e.g. `us-east-1a`).
6430    /// Used for zone-aware routing within a region. Optional.
6431    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6432    pub zone: Option<String>,
6433}
6434
6435fn default_endpoint_weight() -> u32 {
6436    1
6437}
6438
6439fn is_default_weight(w: &u32) -> bool {
6440    *w == 1
6441}
6442
6443/// Timeout hints, in milliseconds.
6444#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6445#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
6446pub struct Timeouts {
6447    /// Connect timeout in milliseconds.
6448    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6449    pub connect_ms: Option<u64>,
6450    /// Read timeout in milliseconds.
6451    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6452    pub read_ms: Option<u64>,
6453    /// Write timeout in milliseconds.
6454    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6455    pub write_ms: Option<u64>,
6456    /// Happy-eyeballs inter-connection delay in milliseconds (DW-030,
6457    /// RFC 8305): when an endpoint's address resolves to multiple
6458    /// addresses, the first is dialed immediately and each subsequent
6459    /// address — alternating address families after the resolver's
6460    /// first — is diailed this long after the previous START; the first
6461    /// successful connection wins and cancels the losers. Absent: the
6462    /// RFC 8305 recommended 250 ms. `0` disables racing (addresses are
6463    /// tried strictly in resolver order, one at a time). The upstream's
6464    /// `connect_ms` remains the bound over the WHOLE dial: resolution
6465    /// plus every interleaved attempt plus, for TLS upstreams, the
6466    /// handshake. Only the overall dial's single outcome reaches
6467    /// breaker/passive-health accounting — the losing arms of one dial
6468    /// are never counted as endpoint failures. Validation bounds the
6469    /// value to at most 10 minutes.
6470    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6471    pub happy_eyeballs_ms: Option<u64>,
6472}
6473
6474/// The principal kind of a consumer (DW-113): `user` (the default —
6475/// a human or application calling the gateway) or `agent` (a bot
6476/// principal with bot credentials, per-agent quotas/token budgets,
6477/// tool allowlists, and a session audit trail). Agent traffic is
6478/// identifiable, governable, and billable; the type threads through
6479/// Identity into analytics for typed attribution.
6480#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Default, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6481#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
6482pub enum ConsumerType {
6483    /// A human or application caller (the default).
6484    #[default]
6485    User,
6486    /// A bot principal: tool allowlists, per-agent token budgets,
6487    /// and typed analytics attribution apply.
6488    Agent,
6489}
6490
6491/// Identity of an API caller (app/team/user); owns credentials, quotas, and
6492/// the analytics identity.
6493#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6494#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
6495pub struct Consumer {
6496    pub name: String,
6497    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
6498    pub credentials: Vec<Credential>,
6499    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
6500    pub policies: Vec<String>,
6501    /// The principal kind (DW-113): `user` (default) or `agent`.
6502    /// Agent consumers gain a tool allowlist, a per-agent token
6503    /// budget, and typed analytics attribution. The field is renamed
6504    /// to `type` in YAML (`type` is a Rust keyword).
6505    #[serde(default, rename = "type")]
6506    pub consumer_type: ConsumerType,
6507    /// MCP tool allowlist (DW-113): when non-empty, the consumer may
6508    /// only call the named tools through the MCP gateway. Empty (the
6509    /// default) = no restriction. Validation checks that every name
6510    /// references a configured `ai.mcp.tools` entry.
6511    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
6512    pub tool_allowlist: Vec<String>,
6513    /// Per-agent AI token budget (DW-113): when set, this consumer's
6514    /// AI traffic is bounded by this budget BEFORE the policy chain
6515    /// (the most-specific budget). Same shape as a policy's
6516    /// `token_budget`. `None` (the default) = no per-agent budget;
6517    /// the policy chain governs as before.
6518    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6519    pub token_budget: Option<TokenBudget>,
6520    /// Consumer priority class for load shedding (DW-016): 0 (lowest) to 10
6521    /// (highest). Stored and validated now; it takes effect only once
6522    /// authentication (DW-019/DW-020) identifies the consumer on a request —
6523    /// until then, shedding priority comes from the matched route (or the
6524    /// default 5). Consumer priority overrides the route's when known, but
6525    /// it does NOT trigger reserved-bucket carving today — only a
6526    /// high-priority route does.
6527    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6528    pub priority: Option<u8>,
6529    /// Consumer group memberships (DW-020): group names this consumer
6530    /// belongs to, consulted by authorization `allowed_groups` /
6531    /// `denied_groups` rules. Empty (the default) = no groups. Group
6532    /// names are free-form strings; validation checks that authorization
6533    /// rules referencing groups resolve against at least one consumer's
6534    /// membership. Store-managed consumers carry their own groups in the
6535    /// state store (#124) — the group NAMESPACE is shared, but
6536    /// config-time validation can only see config consumers, so a rule
6537    /// referencing a group that exists solely on store consumers is
6538    /// flagged (grant at least one config consumer the same group name,
6539    /// or accept the issue is a false positive for store-only groups).
6540    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
6541    pub groups: Vec<String>,
6542    /// Consumer-level authorization rules (#123): the MOST specific link
6543    /// of the frozen chain consumer > route > service > listener >
6544    /// global — applies to every request authenticated as this consumer
6545    /// (naturally only once authentication has identified it). A deny at
6546    /// any level wins; otherwise the most specific level with rules
6547    /// governs (see [`Authz`] and the `authz` module). Same validation
6548    /// rules as route-level authorization.
6549    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6550    pub authorization: Option<Authz>,
6551    /// Request budgets for this consumer (DW-033): a daily and/or
6552    /// monthly request cap counted in fixed UTC calendar windows
6553    /// (midnight-to-midnight UTC; the first through the last instant of
6554    /// the UTC month). Budgets are DISTINCT from rate limits: a rate
6555    /// limit shapes traffic inside seconds or minutes, a budget bounds
6556    /// total volume across a day or month, and the two mechanisms run
6557    /// independently (both apply when both are configured). Enforcement
6558    /// needs the state store (`DWARA_STATE_DB`) for durable counters —
6559    /// without one, quota config is inert (logged at request time, see
6560    /// the `state::quotas` module docs). Store-managed consumers have no
6561    /// config record, so budgets are config-consumer-only in this
6562    /// edition; a distributed shared-counter variant is the Ent
6563    /// follow-up (issue "DW-155").
6564    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6565    pub quotas: Option<ConsumerQuotas>,
6566    /// AI prompt/response logging override (DW-081). None = inherit
6567    /// the global `ai.logging.enabled` setting; Some(false) = disable
6568    /// capture for this consumer even if global is on; Some(true) =
6569    /// enable capture for this consumer even if global is off
6570    /// (requires the `ai.logging` block to exist — the redactor and
6571    /// retention are always needed). The per-consumer toggle respects
6572    /// tenant preference: a consumer with capture disabled produces
6573    /// no stored prompts/responses.
6574    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6575    pub ai_logging: Option<bool>,
6576    /// AI-16 (#204): agent principal metadata. Only meaningful when
6577    /// `consumer_type` is `agent`; ignored otherwise. Carries the
6578    /// agent's display name, description, owner, and permission level
6579    /// for first-class agent principal attribution.
6580    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6581    pub agent: Option<AgentPrincipal>,
6582}
6583
6584/// AI-16 (#204): First-class agent principal metadata. Attached to a
6585/// `Consumer` with `consumer_type: agent` to carry agent-specific
6586/// attribution and permission data.
6587#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6588#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
6589pub struct AgentPrincipal {
6590    /// The agent's display name (human-readable). Defaults to the
6591    /// consumer name when not set.
6592    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6593    pub display_name: Option<String>,
6594    /// A human-readable description of the agent's purpose.
6595    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6596    pub description: Option<String>,
6597    /// The owner of this agent (a user or team name). Used for
6598    /// attribution and audit.
6599    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6600    pub owner: Option<String>,
6601    /// The agent's permission level. One of: `read_only`,
6602    /// `read_write`, `admin`. Default `read_only`.
6603    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6604    pub permissions: Option<AgentPermissionLevel>,
6605}
6606
6607/// AI-16 (#204): Agent permission levels for first-class agent
6608/// principals. Mirrors the `Permission` enum in the MCP module but
6609/// lives in config for declarative assignment.
6610#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Default, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6611#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
6612pub enum AgentPermissionLevel {
6613    /// Read-only access (list, get, stats, health, config).
6614    #[default]
6615    ReadOnly,
6616    /// Read-write access (read + create, update, delete).
6617    ReadWrite,
6618    /// Full admin access (all tools including purge).
6619    Admin,
6620}
6621
6622/// Per-consumer request budgets (DW-033): daily and/or monthly request
6623/// caps in fixed UTC calendar windows, counted by the state store's
6624/// quota counters. At least one budget must be set and every set budget
6625/// must be > 0 (validation); a budget of 0 would deny the consumer's
6626/// first request and is expressed by omitting the field instead.
6627#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6628#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
6629pub struct ConsumerQuotas {
6630    /// Maximum requests per UTC calendar day (midnight to midnight).
6631    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6632    pub daily_requests: Option<u64>,
6633    /// Maximum requests per UTC calendar month.
6634    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6635    pub monthly_requests: Option<u64>,
6636    /// SEC-14 / CFG-14: dry-run mode. When true, quota counters are
6637    /// evaluated and would-be rejections are recorded
6638    /// (`dwara_policy_dry_run_total{phase="quota"}` + a structured
6639    /// log) but the request is NOT rejected — it proceeds even when
6640    /// the quota is exceeded. Useful for calibrating quota limits
6641    /// before enforcing. Default false (reject on quota exceeded).
6642    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
6643    pub dry_run: bool,
6644}
6645
6646/// One authenticator bound to a consumer: API key, JWT issuer/audience
6647/// binding, mTLS client-certificate match, or HMAC signing key (DW-036).
6648///
6649/// `Debug` is manual (DW-045): the api-key and hmac-secret values are
6650/// SECRET and a derived impl would print them — this keeps the config
6651/// tree safe to Debug-log as a whole (`Gateway` and every holder derive
6652/// `Debug` over this type).
6653#[derive(Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6654#[serde(tag = "type", rename_all = "snake_case", deny_unknown_fields)]
6655pub enum Credential {
6656    ApiKey {
6657        /// The API key; hashed at rest by the state layer, never logged.
6658        /// Either the value INLINE (accepted, but redacted to a
6659        /// `${redacted:...}` placeholder in every config echo — admin
6660        /// `GET /config`, DW-045) or a reference resolved at
6661        /// config-compile time: `${ENV_NAME}` (an environment variable)
6662        /// or `${file:/path/to/secret}` (one trailing newline trimmed).
6663        /// References are re-read on every reload; an unresolvable
6664        /// reference fails validation naming this field.
6665        key: String,
6666    },
6667    /// HMAC request-signing credential (DW-036): the consumer presents
6668    /// per-request signatures over the canonical string documented in
6669    /// the `security::authn` module docs, carried in the
6670    /// `X-Dwara-Signature` header family. Unlike API keys the secret
6671    /// cannot be hash-at-rest stored — recomputing an HMAC needs the
6672    /// RAW key bytes — so this credential is config-declared only (the
6673    /// state store's hashed rows cannot serve it) and the resolved
6674    /// bytes live solely in the authenticator's memory, zeroized on
6675    /// drop. The pepper (#124) deliberately does NOT apply here: it
6676    /// guards stored hashes, and there is no stored hash to guard.
6677    Hmac {
6678        /// Public key identifier: the `X-Dwara-Key-Id` header value the
6679        /// client presents to SELECT this credential (the AWS
6680        /// access-key-id shape — it names the key, it is not secret).
6681        /// Non-empty visible ASCII, at most 128 bytes; unique across
6682        /// every consumer (validation rejects duplicates — an ambiguous
6683        /// selector cannot pick a consumer deterministically).
6684        key_id: String,
6685        /// The HMAC-SHA256 signing secret. INLINE values are accepted
6686        /// (redacted in every config echo, like api keys) but
6687        /// `${ENV_NAME}` / `${file:/path/to/secret}` references are the
6688        /// recommended shape; resolved at config-compile time and
6689        /// re-read on every reload. Must be non-empty; use at least 32
6690        /// bytes of entropy (shorter keys weaken the MAC construction
6691        /// — documented, not enforced, the same posture as the pepper).
6692        secret: String,
6693    },
6694    Jwt {
6695        issuer: String,
6696        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
6697        audiences: Vec<String>,
6698    },
6699    Mtls {
6700        /// SHA-256 fingerprint (lowercase hex of the DER) of the client
6701        /// certificate. Optional when `subject` is set (#124); a
6702        /// fingerprint match binds the credential to ONE exact
6703        /// certificate (a re-issued cert needs a new credential).
6704        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6705        fingerprint: Option<String>,
6706        /// Subject CommonName of the client certificate (#124): the
6707        /// credential matches any verified client certificate whose
6708        /// subject CN is exactly this string (case-sensitive). Binding
6709        /// by subject survives certificate re-issue under the same CN;
6710        /// the certificate must still chain to a listener's
6711        /// `client_ca_file` (or be otherwise verified) at the TLS layer
6712        /// — the matcher only maps an ALREADY-VERIFIED certificate to a
6713        /// consumer. Exactly one of `subject` / `fingerprint` must be
6714        /// set (validation rejects both-empty and both-set).
6715        #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6716        subject: Option<String>,
6717    },
6718}
6719
6720impl std::fmt::Debug for Credential {
6721    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
6722        // DW-045: the api-key and hmac-secret values are the inline
6723        // secret material in the schema; everything else is binding
6724        // metadata (issuer, fingerprint, subject, key id) that
6725        // validation matches, not a secret. Redact exactly the secrets
6726        // and keep the variant shape so debug output stays useful.
6727        match self {
6728            Credential::ApiKey { .. } => f
6729                .debug_struct("ApiKey")
6730                .field("key", &"[redacted]")
6731                .finish(),
6732            Credential::Hmac { key_id, .. } => f
6733                .debug_struct("Hmac")
6734                .field("key_id", key_id)
6735                .field("secret", &"[redacted]")
6736                .finish(),
6737            Credential::Jwt { issuer, audiences } => f
6738                .debug_struct("Jwt")
6739                .field("issuer", issuer)
6740                .field("audiences", audiences)
6741                .finish(),
6742            Credential::Mtls {
6743                subject,
6744                fingerprint,
6745            } => f
6746                .debug_struct("Mtls")
6747                .field("subject", subject)
6748                .field("fingerprint", fingerprint)
6749                .finish(),
6750        }
6751    }
6752}
6753
6754/// Named reusable rule bundle (rate limit, timeouts, ...); attachable at
6755/// several scopes. Plugin-backed phases arrive with the M3 plugin system.
6756#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6757#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
6758pub struct Policy {
6759    pub name: String,
6760    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6761    pub rate_limit: Option<RateLimit>,
6762    /// Stacked GCRA rate-limit rules (DW-017). Each rule stacks one or
6763    /// more windows (e.g. `s` AND `hour`); a request is admitted only if
6764    /// EVERY window of EVERY applicable rule allows it. The legacy
6765    /// single-window `rate_limit` field above still applies when set (see
6766    /// its mapping in the rate-limiter module docs); use `rate_limits`
6767    /// for new configs.
6768    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
6769    pub rate_limits: Vec<RateLimitRule>,
6770    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6771    pub timeouts: Option<Timeouts>,
6772    /// Monitor mode (DW-041) for this bundle's rate-limit rules: they
6773    /// still EVALUATE (their GCRA buckets advance exactly as if
6774    /// enforcing, so denial rates reflect what enforcement would do),
6775    /// but a request they would deny is LOGGED and counted
6776    /// (`dwara_policy_dry_run_total{phase="rate_limit"}` + a
6777    /// `dwara::policy` warn event) and PROCEEDS — no 429, and none of
6778    /// the rule's `X-RateLimit-*` headers on the response. LIVE policies
6779    /// attached to the same request still enforce: the flag mutes only
6780    /// this bundle's own denials. The flag sits on the named bundle (not
6781    /// on each `policies: [...]` attachment list entry) because rate
6782    /// limits attach BY NAME at the five precedence levels — marking the
6783    /// bundle dries every attachment of it uniformly. Timeouts carry no
6784    /// rejection and are unaffected.
6785    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "is_false")]
6786    pub dry_run: bool,
6787    /// AI token budget (DW-078): caps provider-reported tokens per
6788    /// minute and/or priced cost per UTC day for the budget holder.
6789    /// Distinct from every rule above: those count REQUESTS, this
6790    /// counts TOKENS. Resolution follows the frozen precedence chain
6791    /// (consumer > route > service > listener > global) with the MOST
6792    /// SPECIFIC level's budget governing (a limit-of-totals, not an
6793    /// AND-composed rule). `scope: consumer` (default) keys the budget
6794    /// to each attaching consumer; `scope: policy` makes one SHARED
6795    /// (team) budget keyed by the policy name. Consumers with no
6796    /// binding budget are unlimited.
6797    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6798    pub token_budget: Option<TokenBudget>,
6799    /// Anomaly scoring (DW-090): lightweight statistical detection of
6800    /// abusive request patterns (header entropy, request-shape outliers).
6801    /// When enabled, every matched request is scored against the
6802    /// configured signals; a score at or above `threshold` is blocked
6803    /// (403 `anomaly_blocked`) unless `dry_run` is set (scored and
6804    /// logged, request proceeds). Resolution follows the frozen
6805    /// precedence chain (consumer > route > service > listener > global);
6806    /// every level with an attachment applies and the applicable rules
6807    /// AND together. Absent (the default): no anomaly scoring.
6808    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6809    pub anomaly: Option<AnomalyPolicy>,
6810    /// Adaptive + origin-driven rate-limit tuning (DW-089): an EWMA of
6811    /// upstream error rate and latency dynamically scales this policy's
6812    /// rate-limit quotas, and upstream-originated signals (`Retry-After`)
6813    /// trigger an immediate backoff for the advertised window. The
6814    /// adaptive factor is bounded `[min_factor, max_factor]` (never 0,
6815    /// never unbounded); tightening under stress is faster than relaxing
6816    /// when healthy (asymmetric). Absent (the default): no adaptive
6817    /// tuning, the policy's quotas apply as configured.
6818    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6819    pub adaptive: Option<AdaptiveRateLimit>,
6820}
6821
6822/// An AI token budget (DW-078, `policies[].token_budget`): limits of
6823/// TOTALS over windows — provider-reported tokens per minute and/or
6824/// cost per UTC calendar day, enforced per consumer or shared per
6825/// policy (team). See the `ai::budget` module docs for the
6826/// check-then-spend contract.
6827#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6828#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
6829pub struct TokenBudget {
6830    /// Maximum provider-reported tokens per fixed 60-second window
6831    /// (epoch-minute aligned). Spent tokens come from the provider's
6832    /// own usage reports — the gateway never estimates.
6833    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6834    pub tokens_per_min: Option<u64>,
6835    /// Maximum spend per UTC calendar day, in integer micro-USD
6836    /// (1_000_000 = $1.00 — no floating-point money). Enforced end to
6837    /// end; the pricing input arrives with the DW-079 pricing tables,
6838    /// so the cost window is enforced-but-inert until then.
6839    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6840    pub cost_per_day_micros: Option<u64>,
6841    /// Whose budget this is: `consumer` (default — each attaching
6842    /// consumer gets its own window) or `policy` (one SHARED team
6843    /// budget keyed by the policy name).
6844    #[serde(
6845        default = "default_token_budget_scope",
6846        skip_serializing_if = "is_default_token_budget_scope"
6847    )]
6848    pub scope: TokenBudgetScope,
6849}
6850
6851/// The budget's key scope (DW-078).
6852#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6853#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
6854pub enum TokenBudgetScope {
6855    /// One budget per attaching consumer.
6856    Consumer,
6857    /// One shared (team) budget per policy.
6858    Policy,
6859}
6860
6861fn default_token_budget_scope() -> TokenBudgetScope {
6862    TokenBudgetScope::Consumer
6863}
6864
6865fn is_default_token_budget_scope(s: &TokenBudgetScope) -> bool {
6866    *s == TokenBudgetScope::Consumer
6867}
6868
6869/// One rate-limit rule (DW-017): a key selector plus one or more stacked
6870/// windows. `selector` names the request attributes that form the
6871/// counter key (all listed attributes are joined into ONE key, so
6872/// `[ip, route]` limits each (client IP, route) pair independently);
6873/// `requests_per` carries the sustained rates per window (at least one
6874/// window must be present); `burst` is the bucket size (defaults to the
6875/// window's request count).
6876#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6877#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
6878pub struct RateLimitRule {
6879    /// Optional label (documentation only; not part of the key).
6880    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6881    pub name: Option<String>,
6882    /// Key components: `ip`, `credential`, and/or `route` (at least one;
6883    /// order does not matter). `credential` falls back to the client IP
6884    /// until authentication (DW-019) identifies consumers.
6885    pub selector: Vec<RateLimitSelector>,
6886    pub requests_per: RateRequestsPer,
6887    /// Bucket size (burst capacity); must be >= 1 when present. Defaults
6888    /// to the window's request count.
6889    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6890    pub burst: Option<u32>,
6891}
6892
6893/// One attribute of a rate-limit key (DW-017).
6894#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6895#[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
6896pub enum RateLimitSelector {
6897    /// The direct connection peer (the same IP used for X-Real-IP).
6898    Ip,
6899    /// The authenticated consumer; until DW-019 this falls back to `ip`.
6900    Credential,
6901    /// The matched route's name.
6902    Route,
6903}
6904
6905/// Sustained rates per window (DW-017). At least one field must be set
6906/// and every set field must be > 0; each set field becomes one stacked
6907/// GCRA cell (a request must satisfy ALL set windows).
6908#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Default, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6909#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
6910pub struct RateRequestsPer {
6911    /// Requests per second.
6912    #[serde(rename = "s", skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6913    pub per_second: Option<u32>,
6914    /// Requests per minute.
6915    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6916    pub minute: Option<u32>,
6917    /// Requests per hour.
6918    #[serde(skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6919    pub hour: Option<u32>,
6920}
6921
6922/// Local GCRA-style rate limit.
6923#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
6924#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
6925pub struct RateLimit {
6926    /// Maximum requests allowed per window.
6927    pub requests: u64,
6928    /// Window length in seconds.
6929    pub window_seconds: u64,
6930}
6931
6932/// Parse a YAML configuration document into a [`Gateway`], rejecting invalid
6933/// input with path-precise error messages.
6934///
6935/// Error-path guarantee: serde-level failures (type mismatches, unknown
6936/// fields, missing fields) carry a precise dotted path from
6937/// `serde_path_to_error`, e.g. `routes[0].action`. Raw YAML syntax errors
6938/// are detected by the scanner before path tracking applies, so their path
6939/// is coarse (often the root); the precise location is still available as
6940/// line/column inside the message text itself.
6941pub fn parse_gateway(text: &str) -> Result<Gateway, ConfigError> {
6942    let de = serde_path_to_error::deserialize(serde_yaml_ng::Deserializer::from_str(text))
6943        .map_err(|e| ConfigError {
6944            path: e.path().to_string(),
6945            message: e.inner().to_string(),
6946        })?;
6947    Ok(de)
6948}
6949
6950/// Serialize a [`Gateway`] to normalized YAML text.
6951///
6952/// Field order follows struct declaration order; defaulted-empty collections
6953/// are omitted, so output is stable for a given typed value.
6954pub fn gateway_to_yaml(gateway: &Gateway) -> Result<String, serde_yaml_ng::Error> {
6955    serde_yaml_ng::to_string(gateway)
6956}
6957
6958/// Build the JSON Schema for the root [`Gateway`] type.
6959pub fn json_schema() -> schemars::Schema {
6960    schemars::schema_for!(Gateway)
6961}

6963// --- DW-055: proxy-wasm plugin config -------------------------------------
6964
6965/// A plugin definition (DW-055 / DW-119). Each plugin is either a
6966/// proxy-wasm `.wasm` module loaded at startup (`wasm`) or a compiled-in
6967/// native Rust filter (`native`), run on the request pipeline phases it
6968/// declares. Routes reference plugins by name. Exactly one of `wasm` or
6969/// `native` must be set (validation enforces this).
6970#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize, schemars::JsonSchema)]
6971#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
6972pub struct PluginConfig {
6973    /// Unique plugin name. Referenced by routes' `plugins` field.
6974    pub name: String,
6975    /// Path to the .wasm module file. Must be readable at startup.
6976    /// Mutually exclusive with `native`; exactly one of the two must be
6977    /// set. Absent when this is a native filter.
6978    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6979    pub wasm: Option<String>,
6980    /// Registered native filter implementation name (DW-119). The
6981    /// `plugins` cargo feature must be enabled and a factory registered
6982    /// under this name at startup. Mutually exclusive with `wasm`;
6983    /// exactly one of the two must be set. Absent when this is a WASM
6984    /// plugin.
6985    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6986    pub native: Option<String>,
6987    /// SCALE-12 (#192)/DW-165: remote source for this plugin. When
6988    /// set, the gateway resolves the artifact at every publish
6989    /// (startup and reload): cache-first, downloading on a miss, then
6990    /// verifying the mandatory SHA-256 digest and any configured
6991    /// Ed25519 signature BEFORE the wasm load step. A source that
6992    /// cannot be resolved or fails verification never loads (the
6993    /// plugin reads Crashed; its routes fail closed). Mutually
6994    /// exclusive with `native` and `wasm`. See
6995    /// [`PluginSourceConfig`] and the plugin registry guide for the
6996    /// cache layout and the registry spec.
6997    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
6998    pub source: Option<PluginSourceConfig>,
6999    /// Phases this plugin hooks. Must be a non-empty subset of:
7000    /// `request_headers`, `request_body`, `response_headers`,
7001    /// `response_body`. The host calls the plugin's corresponding
7002    /// `proxy_on_*` export at each declared phase (WASM) or the
7003    /// `NativeFilter` trait method (native).
7004    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
7005    pub phases: Vec<PluginPhase>,
7006    /// Plugin-specific configuration, passed to the plugin's
7007    /// `proxy_on_configure` as a byte string (WASM) or to the native
7008    /// filter's factory (native). Typically a JSON or YAML blob the
7009    /// plugin parses itself.
7010    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
7011    pub config: Option<String>,
7012    /// Resource limits for this plugin (fuel, memory, time). Absent
7013    /// uses the defaults: 1M fuel, 32MB memory, 100ms timeout. Only
7014    /// meaningful for WASM plugins (native filters run in-process and
7015    /// are bounded by the gateway's own resource limits).
7016    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
7017    pub limits: Option<PluginLimitsConfig>,
7018}
7019
7020/// SCALE-12 (#192)/DW-165: remote/signed plugin source configuration.
7021/// The gateway resolves the .wasm artifact at every publish: cache
7022/// lookup first, then a download from `url` on a miss, then SHA-256
7023/// `digest` verification (mandatory) and Ed25519 `signature`
7024/// verification (when configured) — only then is the artifact loaded.
7025/// Resolution failures are fail-closed per plugin (Crashed at publish,
7026/// its routes answer 500), never a partial load.
7027#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
7028#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
7029pub struct PluginSourceConfig {
7030    /// The registry URL to fetch the .wasm artifact from. Must be
7031    /// `https://`; fetched with one HTTP/1.1 GET (redirects are not
7032    /// followed — configure the artifact's final URL). `oci://`
7033    /// references are not supported in this version: publish the
7034    /// artifact over https or pre-fetch it with `dwara-cli plugin
7035    /// install` and reference the local file with `wasm`.
7036    pub url: String,
7037    /// Expected SHA-256 digest of the artifact, hex-encoded (64
7038    /// chars, case-insensitive). Verified against the cached AND the
7039    /// downloaded bytes before loading; a mismatch fails the plugin
7040    /// (never a partial load). Required — there are no unsigned
7041    /// remote artifacts (digest-pinning is the floor; signatures are
7042    /// the ceiling).
7043    pub digest: String,
7044    /// Optional Ed25519 signature over the artifact bytes, hex-encoded
7045    /// (64 bytes / 128 chars). When set, it must verify against
7046    /// `public_key` and, when `plugin_registry.public_keys` pins
7047    /// keys, against a pinned key too. Absent means digest-only
7048    /// verification — rejected when the registry pins public keys.
7049    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
7050    pub signature: Option<String>,
7051    /// Ed25519 public key (32 bytes, hex-encoded) for signature
7052    /// verification. Required when `signature` is set unless
7053    /// `plugin_registry.public_keys` pins keys. A `public_key` set
7054    /// WITHOUT a `signature` is rejected (a key that verifies nothing
7055    /// is a config mistake — sign the artifact or remove the key).
7056    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
7057    pub public_key: Option<String>,
7058    /// Local cache path for the resolved artifact. When absent, the
7059    /// artifact is cached content-addressed as
7060    /// `<cache_dir>/<digest>.wasm` (`plugin_registry.cache_dir`,
7061    /// default `./plugin-cache`). When set, the path must be a
7062    /// RELATIVE path under the cache dir with no `..` parent
7063    /// segments (containment: the resolver creates directories and
7064    /// writes artifact bytes at this path, so it must not be able to
7065    /// point outside the cache). The cached file is re-verified
7066    /// (digest and signature) on every publish; a cached file that
7067    /// does not match the pinned digest fails closed instead of being
7068    /// silently re-fetched.
7069    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
7070    pub cache_path: Option<String>,
7071}
7072
7073impl PluginSourceConfig {
7074    /// Why a `cache_path` value violates cache containment, or `None`
7075    /// when it is acceptable. The rule is deliberately simple: a
7076    /// non-empty RELATIVE path with no `..` parent segments — the
7077    /// resolver creates directories and writes artifact bytes at this
7078    /// path, so it must stay inside `plugin_registry.cache_dir`.
7079    /// Shared by snapshot validation (the operator-facing, earliest
7080    /// rejection) and the resolver (defense in depth); lives here
7081    /// because both may import `config` and nothing below it.
7082    pub fn cache_path_violation(cache_path: &str) -> Option<&'static str> {
7083        if cache_path.trim().is_empty() {
7084            Some("the path is empty")
7085        } else if std::path::Path::new(cache_path).is_absolute() {
7086            Some("the path is absolute")
7087        } else if std::path::Path::new(cache_path)
7088            .components()
7089            .any(|c| matches!(c, std::path::Component::ParentDir))
7090        {
7091            Some("the path contains '..' parent segments")
7092        } else {
7093            None
7094        }
7095    }
7096}
7097
7098/// SCALE-12 (#192)/DW-165: plugin registry configuration. When
7099/// present, signatures of `source:`-referenced artifacts are verified
7100/// against the registry's pinned public keys and resolved artifacts
7101/// are cached in the registry's cache directory. This enables
7102/// fleet-consistent plugin pinning: all gateways in a fleet reference
7103/// the same registry and get the same signed artifacts.
7104#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
7105#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
7106pub struct PluginRegistryConfig {
7107    /// Registry base URL (e.g., `https://registry.example.com/plugins`).
7108    /// Informational for fleet tooling (`dwara-cli plugin
7109    /// search/install` default); each plugin's `source.url` is a full
7110    /// URL.
7111    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
7112    pub base_url: Option<String>,
7113    /// Pinned Ed25519 public keys (hex-encoded, 32 bytes each). When
7114    /// set, plugin signatures are verified against these keys in
7115    /// addition to any per-plugin `public_key`, and a plugin with no
7116    /// signature is rejected (unsigned artifacts cannot ride a
7117    /// pinned-key registry).
7118    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
7119    pub public_keys: Vec<String>,
7120    /// Local cache directory for resolved artifacts, content-addressed
7121    /// as `<cache_dir>/<digest>.wasm`. Default: `./plugin-cache`
7122    /// (relative to the gateway working directory, the same posture
7123    /// as the ACME state directory).
7124    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
7125    pub cache_dir: Option<String>,
7126}
7127
7128/// The phases a plugin can hook (DW-055, §9.3 phase contract).
7129#[derive(
7130    Clone,
7131    Copy,
7132    Debug,
7133    PartialEq,
7134    Eq,
7135    std::hash::Hash,
7136    serde::Serialize,
7137    serde::Deserialize,
7138    schemars::JsonSchema,
7139)]
7140#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
7141pub enum PluginPhase {
7142    /// After route resolution, before authn.
7143    RequestHeaders,
7144    /// After authn/authz/rate-limit, before upstream.
7145    RequestBody,
7146    /// After the upstream responds, before masking.
7147    ResponseHeaders,
7148    /// After masking, before compression.
7149    ResponseBody,
7150}
7151
7152impl PluginPhase {
7153    /// The phase's stable wire label (the serde name), for the plugin
7154    /// status surface (`GET /plugins`, DW-158).
7155    pub fn as_str(self) -> &'static str {
7156        match self {
7157            PluginPhase::RequestHeaders => "request_headers",
7158            PluginPhase::RequestBody => "request_body",
7159            PluginPhase::ResponseHeaders => "response_headers",
7160            PluginPhase::ResponseBody => "response_body",
7161        }
7162    }
7163}
7164
7165/// SCALE-12 (#191): formalized request-pipeline filter phases. The
7166/// default order is the order the variants are declared (the same
7167/// order the dataplane has always executed them). Per-route
7168/// `filter_chain` overrides can reorder a subset of these phases;
7169/// phases not listed in an override keep their default relative
7170/// order.
7171#[derive(
7172    Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema,
7173)]
7174#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
7175pub enum FilterPhase {
7176    /// IP ACL and consumer ACL checks.
7177    Acl,
7178    /// Rate limiting (per-consumer and per-route limiters).
7179    RateLimit,
7180    /// Authentication (API key, JWT, OIDC, mTLS).
7181    Authn,
7182    /// Authorization (route/service/listener/global policy chain).
7183    Authz,
7184    /// Request body validation (JSON schema, size limits).
7185    Validate,
7186    /// Request transforms (path rewrite, header/query transforms).
7187    Transform,
7188    /// Response cache lookup.
7189    Cache,
7190    /// Route dispatch (proxy, redirect, respond, ai, etc.).
7191    Route,
7192}
7193
7194impl FilterPhase {
7195    /// The default pipeline order (the order the dataplane has
7196    /// always executed the phases).
7197    pub const DEFAULT_ORDER: [FilterPhase; 8] = [
7198        FilterPhase::Acl,
7199        FilterPhase::RateLimit,
7200        FilterPhase::Authn,
7201        FilterPhase::Authz,
7202        FilterPhase::Validate,
7203        FilterPhase::Transform,
7204        FilterPhase::Cache,
7205        FilterPhase::Route,
7206    ];
7207
7208    pub fn as_str(self) -> &'static str {
7209        match self {
7210            FilterPhase::Acl => "acl",
7211            FilterPhase::RateLimit => "rate_limit",
7212            FilterPhase::Authn => "authn",
7213            FilterPhase::Authz => "authz",
7214            FilterPhase::Validate => "validate",
7215            FilterPhase::Transform => "transform",
7216            FilterPhase::Cache => "cache",
7217            FilterPhase::Route => "route",
7218        }
7219    }
7220}
7221
7222/// SCALE-12 (#191): per-phase dry-run configuration. When dry-run is
7223/// enabled for a phase, the phase executes its checks but does NOT
7224/// enforce (reject/block). Failures are logged as warnings and the
7225/// request continues. This enables safe policy rollout: enable a
7226/// phase in dry-run mode, observe the would-be rejections, then
7227/// switch to enforcement.
7228#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
7229#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
7230pub struct FilterChainConfig {
7231    /// Phases to run in dry-run mode (log but don't enforce). Each
7232    /// entry is a phase name from [`FilterPhase`]. A phase in
7233    /// dry-run mode still executes its logic; the difference is that
7234    /// a rejection is logged as a warning and the request continues.
7235    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
7236    pub dry_run: Vec<FilterPhase>,
7237    /// Optional ordering override: a list of phase names in the
7238    /// order they should execute. Phases not listed keep their
7239    /// default relative order. The override must contain exactly the
7240    /// same set of phases as the default (no duplicates, no unknown
7241    /// phases); validation rejects invalid overrides.
7242    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
7243    pub order: Option<Vec<FilterPhase>>,
7244}
7245
7246/// Resource limits for a proxy-wasm plugin (DW-055 decision 4; §9.3).
7247#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize, schemars::JsonSchema)]
7248#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
7249pub struct PluginLimitsConfig {
7250    /// Maximum fuel (wasmtime operations). Default: 1,000,000.
7251    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
7252    pub fuel: Option<u64>,
7253    /// Maximum linear memory in MB. Default: 32.
7254    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
7255    pub memory_mb: Option<usize>,
7256    /// Maximum execution time in milliseconds. Default: 100.
7257    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
7258    pub timeout_ms: Option<u64>,
7259}
7260
7261/// Anomaly scoring policy (DW-090, `policies[].anomaly`): lightweight
7262/// statistical detection of abusive request patterns. Each enabled
7263/// signal produces a normalized [0, 1] score from the request shape
7264/// (header entropy, header count/bytes, path length/depth, query count,
7265/// body size, unusual method); the overall score is the average of the
7266/// configured signals' scores. A score at or above `threshold` is
7267/// blocked (403 `anomaly_blocked`) unless `dry_run` is set.
7268#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
7269#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
7270pub struct AnomalyPolicy {
7271    /// Whether anomaly scoring is enabled (default true). A `policies[].anomaly`
7272    /// block with `enabled: false` is a no-op (the scorer is not compiled).
7273    #[serde(default = "default_true")]
7274    pub enabled: bool,
7275    /// Monitor mode (DW-041 synergy): the score is computed and the
7276    /// would-block is logged + counted, but the request proceeds. Default
7277    /// false (enforce).
7278    #[serde(default)]
7279    pub dry_run: bool,
7280    /// The signals to score. Must be non-empty when `enabled` is true.
7281    /// Each signal contributes one normalized [0, 1] sub-score; the
7282    /// overall score is their average.
7283    pub signals: Vec<AnomalySignal>,
7284    /// Block threshold in [0, 1] (default 0.8). A score >= threshold is
7285    /// blocked (or logged in dry-run mode). Must be in (0, 1].
7286    #[serde(default = "default_anomaly_threshold")]
7287    pub threshold: f64,
7288    /// Maximum body bytes to inspect for the `body_size` signal (default
7289    /// 4096). The signal scores `min(body_len, cap) / cap`, so a body
7290    /// at or beyond the cap scores 1.0. Must be > 0.
7291    #[serde(default = "default_max_body_inspect")]
7292    pub max_body_inspect_bytes: u64,
7293}
7294
7295/// One anomaly signal (DW-090). Each produces a normalized [0, 1]
7296/// sub-score from one request-shape dimension.
7297#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
7298#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
7299pub enum AnomalySignal {
7300    /// Shannon entropy of concatenated header values (normalized by 8,
7301    /// the max entropy for byte values). High entropy can indicate
7302    /// randomized/obfuscated header payloads.
7303    HeaderEntropy,
7304    /// Header count outlier: `count / 50`, capped at 1.0.
7305    HeaderCount,
7306    /// Total header bytes outlier: `bytes / 8192`, capped at 1.0.
7307    HeaderBytes,
7308    /// Path length outlier: `len / 1024`, capped at 1.0.
7309    PathLength,
7310    /// Path depth outlier: segment count / 20, capped at 1.0.
7311    PathDepth,
7312    /// Query parameter count outlier: `count / 50`, capped at 1.0.
7313    QueryCount,
7314    /// Body size outlier: `min(body_len, cap) / cap`, capped at 1.0.
7315    BodySize,
7316    /// Unusual HTTP method: 1.0 if the method is not one of GET, POST,
7317    /// PUT, DELETE, PATCH, HEAD, OPTIONS; 0.0 otherwise.
7318    MethodUnusual,
7319}
7320
7321fn default_anomaly_threshold() -> f64 {
7322    0.8
7323}
7324
7325fn default_max_body_inspect() -> u64 {
7326    4096
7327}
7328
7329// --- DW-089: adaptive + origin-driven rate-limit tuning -------------------
7330
7331/// Adaptive + origin-driven rate-limit tuning (DW-089,
7332/// `policies[].adaptive`): an EWMA of upstream error rate and latency
7333/// dynamically scales this policy's rate-limit quotas at check time
7334/// (the factor multiplies the effective cost of each request against
7335/// the GCRA bucket), and upstream-originated signals trigger an
7336/// immediate backoff. The factor is bounded `[min_factor, max_factor]`
7337/// (never 0, never unbounded); tightening under stress is faster than
7338/// relaxing when healthy (asymmetric, so the gateway errs toward
7339/// protecting upstreams). `origin_signals` selects which upstream
7340/// response signals the controller honors (currently `retry_after`).
7341#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
7342#[serde(deny_unknown_fields)]
7343pub struct AdaptiveRateLimit {
7344    /// Whether adaptive tuning is enabled (default true). A
7345    /// `policies[].adaptive` block with `enabled: false` is a no-op
7346    /// (the controller is not compiled for that policy).
7347    #[serde(default = "default_true")]
7348    pub enabled: bool,
7349    /// EWMA decay window in seconds for error-rate and latency tracking
7350    /// (default 60). A larger window smooths transient spikes; a smaller
7351    /// window reacts faster. Must be > 0.
7352    #[serde(default = "default_ewma_window_secs")]
7353    pub ewma_window_secs: u64,
7354    /// Minimum adaptive factor (default 0.1): the tightest the quotas
7355    /// can shrink to (10% of the configured rate). Must be > 0 and <=
7356    /// `max_factor`. The factor is never 0.
7357    #[serde(default = "default_adaptive_min_factor")]
7358    pub min_factor: f64,
7359    /// Maximum adaptive factor (default 2.0): the loosest the quotas
7360    /// can grow to (200% of the configured rate). Must be > 0 and >=
7361    /// `min_factor`. The factor is never unbounded.
7362    #[serde(default = "default_adaptive_max_factor")]
7363    pub max_factor: f64,
7364    /// Error-rate EWMA threshold that triggers tightening (default 0.05
7365    /// = 5% 5xx rate). When the error EWMA exceeds this, the factor
7366    /// decreases toward `min_factor`. Must be in (0, 1].
7367    #[serde(default = "default_adaptive_error_threshold")]
7368    pub error_threshold: f64,
7369    /// Latency EWMA threshold in milliseconds that triggers tightening
7370    /// (default 500). When the latency EWMA exceeds this, the factor
7371    /// decreases toward `min_factor`. Must be > 0.
7372    #[serde(default = "default_adaptive_latency_threshold_ms")]
7373    pub latency_threshold_ms: u64,
7374    /// Upstream-originated signals the controller honors (default
7375    /// empty: EWMA-only tuning). Currently supported: `retry_after`
7376    /// (parse the upstream `Retry-After` header and back off for the
7377    /// advertised window).
7378    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty")]
7379    pub origin_signals: Vec<OriginSignal>,
7380}
7381
7382/// One upstream-originated signal the adaptive controller honors
7383/// (DW-089).
7384#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize, JsonSchema)]
7385#[serde(rename_all = "snake_case")]
7386pub enum OriginSignal {
7387    /// Parse the upstream `Retry-After` header (integer seconds or
7388    /// HTTP-date) and back off for the advertised window: the factor
7389    /// drops to `min_factor` immediately and stays there until the
7390    /// window elapses.
7391    RetryAfter,
7392}
7393
7394fn default_ewma_window_secs() -> u64 {
7395    60
7396}
7397
7398fn default_adaptive_min_factor() -> f64 {
7399    0.1
7400}
7401
7402fn default_adaptive_max_factor() -> f64 {
7403    2.0
7404}
7405
7406fn default_adaptive_error_threshold() -> f64 {
7407    0.05
7408}
7409
7410fn default_adaptive_latency_threshold_ms() -> u64 {
7411    500
7412}
